For width, two additional sources are queued on separate frames on opposite sides of the opening, spaced from its measured major radius; they remain attached to the cap and share the normal bleed lifetime. This increases Niagara component count and may add visual density or runtime cost. The cooked effect does not expose an identified parameter for outward particle velocity; its world-space emitters can still descend without first traveling outward. Placement, motion and cost need in-game verification; the requested outward-then-gravity trajectory remains unresolved.

The 17:46–17:49 test reported unrecognized projectile deaths, and network hooks alone do not establish that single-player hits are reported there. `SBProjectile:OnPhysicsShapeHit` and `OnCollisionOriginBeginOverlap` now record direct collisions with Eve, using the hit location and projectile velocity. This is time-limited evidence, not proof of damage: a collision shortly before an unrelated lethal hit could be misclassified. Some explosions may bypass these callbacks; event delivery and ordering need a playtest. `smash_hits` checks target filtering and the decision path with a fake engine.

Stump flow scales its local X/Y using the cut ellipse's measured half-widths and points the effect's local negative Z along the opening normal. The original Niagara asset has a local-space ribbon, but its two sprite emitters omit the local-space flag. World-space simulation and gravity may therefore still make some particles fall downward; component rotation cannot establish full directional flow. Width calibration and appearance likewise require rendering in game. The bone-core transform loop now avoids temporary vector tables; the last test logged 17–34 ms in stub geometry, while separate death-start, HUD, cut and movement spikes remain. The diagnostic GC probe is suspended for the first six death seconds, so it cannot identify GC in that interval; neither change establishes actual frame-time improvement without a game test.


F3 dependency colours are darker on normal rows and lighter on the selected row's light background. Ragdoll collision candidates include hands and forearms against the head; pairs initially touching or too near remain disabled to prevent a violent initial separation. `hairpairs` checks both cases; in-game contact remains unverified.

Projectile detection uses `SBNetworkPlayerController:ClientBroadcast_ProjectileHitResult` (target GUID, result, impact position and projectile GUID) for hits on Eve only. A recent nonzero projectile ID in the target-verified `ClientBroadcast_ActorDead` is a fallback; explicit fall-damage effects take priority. `death_hit` prefers those reports to a synthetic enemy swing, early lethal handling keeps the hit for a deferred torso smash, and other targets, misses, stale events and empty IDs are rejected. Static analysis of the executable's registered wrapper at 0x1427528a0 confirms the broadcast's argument dispatch; the SDK identifies the fields and result enum. It does not prove which single-player attacks deliver either broadcast or whether their timing precedes an already-started early cut. In particular, coverage of all projectile-driven explosions remains unverified. `sim_smash_hits` tests the decision paths, not game event delivery.

Upper-body smash cancels convulsion pulses and schedules no lower-body movement. An ordinary waist cut preserves upper-body struggle, but the struggle servo and convulsion pulse no longer push the parent body across a severed joint. This prevents a specific source of artificial motion below the cut; it does not establish that all visible thigh deformation or PhysX jitter is gone. `smash_struggle` and `waist` check the scripted movement, not the solver.

Dropped and gripped weapons now save and restore relative scale along with their original parent, socket, location and rotation. Unreal's attachment call uses snap rules including scale; retaining only location and rotation lost part of the original component transform. `sword_return` checks repeated restoration. Whether that is the cause of the reported cumulative hairpin position drift, rather than an animation or socket issue, still needs a game test.

Stump blood uses the existing `NS_A_BloodLoop` from the lobby elite finish, with additional sources across the opening, and deactivates after `bleedTime`. Inspection of its cooked Niagara exports shows a ribbon and two sprite emitters, no mesh renderer, and blood materials. The 0.6.0 `NS_HitBlood_PC_01` stump spurts are now a separate attached component, at the old scale and orientation, restarted at the `bleedPulse` interval; the continuous loop is never reset. A living low-HP arm cut (`q.why == "low HP"`) spawns an unattached one-shot blood burst at each stump, without a loop or attached pulsing component. Burst spots, the splat and floor drips/pools remain. Restarts are serviced at most one per frame when no cut or queued impact takes priority, so busy scenes can delay or coalesce spurts. `smash_effects` checks both components, repeat scheduling, stop and cleanup. The fake engine does not render Niagara: the look and extra cost require in-game verification, and the continuous effect's outward-then-downward direction remains unresolved.

Impact work remains FIFO, with at most one ready expensive impact step serviced per render frame and none on a cut-stage or copy-refresh frame; delays are still counted by gore updates. This can reduce queue latency above 30 fps without combining a cut stage and an effect. Fresh character lifetime guards are batched up to 64 per native command, and repeated blood-tint writes within one second are skipped when the settings are unchanged; a setting change or world reload invalidates that cache. `charcache`, `smash_effects`, `stages` and the smash stress tests cover ordering, lifecycle and bounded batches. Individual engine skin-weight updates, Niagara/audio work, scans and unrelated frame hitches can still be slow; actual timing must be measured in game.

Hits after death: an enemy with no active montage can still strike when its weapon or hand moves at least `afterHits.noMontageSpeed` (1500 cm/s) relative to its actor between samples; known non-attack montages stay rejected. `sim_after_nomontage` checks a fast graph-driven swing and actor translation. The observed pass was near a severed calf, so a live playtest remains necessary to establish its visible result. A decapitation-then-body-hit report (torso hit, no cut and no log line) traced to silent early returns: the follow-up path now names every drop with a capped log line (too soon after death, gated, no part near the swing, too far or too slow, later non-blows), and the hit geometry skips already-severed parts so a body swing after a beheading reads the torso instead of the flown head (`after_hit_geometry` takes an optional cut test from `gore.already_cut`; `sim_after_geometry` and `sim_hits_after` cover the skip and every new line).

Death-odds shaping (`blade_decision.near_odds`): a blow from above zeroes the waist factor, except a sharp blow landing at her waist and clearly nearer it than the neck, which keeps its distance factor ("from above, at the waist") — downward hair-whips at her waist rolled head cuts. Higher up the old rule stands. `sim_odds` covers both.

Follow-up timing (`tune.afterHits.from` 0.6 → 0.4 s): the window only ever ate the killing swing's own follow-through plus genuine combo hits landing right after death, while the slowdown/rearm arming already guards double-counts. Session diagnosis: with `enemies.afterDeath` off (default) there are no genuine corpse blows at all — logged passes were 0.7-9 m away at walking pace, correctly rejected; the caps were never even reached. For actual corpse combat the AI switch (F3 menu, Attacks After Death) is the lever, not looser gates.

Struggle servos (`struggle.reach`): the middle joint (elbow, knee) drives at 1.5x the upper joint's strength. Playtest errM showed mid joints ~100 deg off their fold targets at full strength while shoulders and hips converged, and `sim_fold` verifies the fold math, so this is authority, not aim; caps still bind well below frantic. Watch the `(elbows and knees N)` line: it should drop.

Corpse attacks end when she is gone (`gore.nothing_left`, checked in `enemies.after_tick`): once every PARTS root is cut or smashed away, the takeover is put back early with "nothing left of her". Smashed parts count through `already_cut`'s smashed-child and full-smash rules; a split keeps attacking (halves remain). Covered by `nothing_left` and `after_ai`.

Ordered-but-trailless swings are diagnosed: an AI-ordered attack that never yields weapon motion logged nothing (`best == nil` was the last silent path). `enemies.lua` stamps `ctx.afterOrderedAt` on every played order; `hits_after` logs a capped "no weapon trail" line when candidates stay empty within 1.5 s of one. Tracking also keeps a followed enemy across a single failed destroyed-check read instead of silently dropping its trail (`== true`, still drops the truly destroyed). Covered by a new `hits_after` check; existing tracking behavior unchanged when reads succeed.

Corpse attacks aim at what is left of her (`enemies.swings_tick`): the body point is her upper body while it is there and her pelvis once it is cut or smashed away, falling back across a smashed-away socket instead of ordering nothing. Geometry already read only remaining parts; this keeps the lunge and swing decisions on them too. Covered by `after_ai`.

Takeover cost (`enemies.after_tick`): a cold tree cache scanned synchronously on the death frame — hundreds of ms (632 ms of a 656 ms hitch, playtest; the smash stages themselves are ~60 ms and chunk spawn shows nothing). The takeover now waits 1.5 s with ordered attacks playing meanwhile. Blackboard target keys are re-read per enemy at most every 2 s instead of every 0.25 s (the steady 44-65 ms slow-tick cost with a pack around). Covered by `after_ai` deferral checks; the spread shows in the slow-tick breakdown, not the sims.

For the CNS dress investigation, F7 logs breast and hip relative transforms before and during ragdoll. A temporary test disabled the entire post-process graph and restored it during two F7 ragdolls; the breast links stayed twisted while the graph was off, so the temporary toggle was removed. This does not directly measure the physics-body pose. `assetkit/research/cns-wedding-findings.md` records the result and the separate mesh-weight calculation.

For this exact wedding dress mesh, `screen.wedding_blend` waits for the pelvis to simulate, then gives the `Dm-L/R-Breast` subtrees physics blend weight zero. Their bodies and joints remain simulated, but their bone skinning should use the animated pose. Plain ragdoll improved when only `Ab-L/R-Breast-Link` was blended out; after some cuts the log also showed their `Dm-L/R-Breast` parent twisting, so the scope was extended to it. If `bBlendPhysics` forces all physics regardless of weights, the code gives the other bodies full physics weight first, clears the override, and restores it after recovery. The fake F7 test checks the calls and recovery, but cannot model Unreal's blend or establish the visual result.

The dress mesh contains simulated cloth sections. On a successful cut, `gore.wedding_cloth` saves the component's `ClothBlendWeight` and sets it to zero so the dress uses its cut skin weights instead of cloth simulation; it restores the original value on recovery or outfit swap. This is scoped to the diagnosed dress. An earlier offline two-leg cut detected nine stretched triangles in section 7; the subsequent hanging-island classification fix eliminates them in the mesh harness, although the complete in-game result remains unconfirmed.

The cut's `keep_only` now computes a fallback bone only when a vertex actually needs it, rather than scanning for one for every vertex. A local two-leg real-mesh harness measured a median 159 ms before and 149 ms after (CPU time, three runs); this is not an in-game frame-time measurement and does not eliminate the observed 90–113 ms in-game cut stalls.

When a struggle ends, `struggle.lua` now wakes the ragdoll and delays its settle/sleep path for 1.5 seconds via `active.twitch`, giving gravity time to relax its last pose. The logs showed settling could occur at the end of a struggle, which could freeze the pose; the visible effect needs a playtest. The synthetic struggle tests check that bodies are awake half a second after the struggle ends; their fake ragdoll has no gravity and cannot verify the visual pose.

CNS-repacked physics assets are fixed at runtime regardless of their pelvis damping. The wedding dress's asset was read as patched by the damping heuristic; correcting that, tightening only the spine, and suspending its post-process physics nodes did not fix folding in playtests, so the latter two changes were removed. Direct comparison of its extracted physics asset with the original EVE_02 shows default or omitted angular joint profiles across the body, including spine, clavicles and head, and broader limits on other joints; computed twist angles alone do not restore their motion modes or swing limits. For that exact repacked asset, `physfix.lua` restores the EVE_02 angular profiles while retaining the dress's constraint frames and applying the normal bind-pose bend ranges. `sim_lemi` checks the restored profiles; a playtest reported a much better body pose.

After the original joint profiles were restored, a playtest reported the body improved but the dress's breasts still deformed. Pausing its breast spring nodes, locking its asset helper joints, replacing them with runtime constraints, and disabling Jiggle After Death did not fix it. Pausing the post-process RigidBody node after the body fix was also tested: the node was disabled but the same breast-link rotations remained, so the change was removed. Offline inspection showed all six breast-chain constraint frames match the bind pose within 0.1 degrees. The F7 probe showed the `Ab-*-Breast-Link` bones rotate roughly 90–145 degrees and move 2 cm from their parents despite 3-degree asset limits; it now also logs whether each bone simulates physics and its angular velocity at 0.2, 1 and 3 seconds. The cause and visual fix remain unconfirmed.

A 0.6.2 report shows `FRenderResource was deleted without being released first` after an enemy kill, without a stack or timing beyond the death. The exact trigger is unknown. Code review found three temporary skeletal-mesh destruction paths where an override could survive: a regrow copy (`V`), a settled-piece copy after copied weights (`y`) but failed pose (`P`), and the split copy after `R` restored a previous cut only on the source. Each now sends `x<copy>,0,1` before destroying it, preserving shared sinks while releasing the copy's override. `sim_regrow`, `sim_piece`, and `sim_smash_hits` assert the order and preserved-source behavior; `sim_revival_screen` covers the separately addressed stale-bar takeover. These simulations do not model Unreal's render thread or prove the reported crash fixed.
The missed-death watch marks a lethal hit as already handled when its ragdoll starts, retaining the hit for the cut but preventing `hm.take` from triggering on the same empty HP bar and death screen after recovery. A 0.6.1 report shows recovery followed immediately by a false takeover and a new cut during respawn, then a render-resource fatal error; a mesh change with active cut weights previously produced the same error, but no crash stack was supplied. `sim_revival_screen` reproduces the stale-bar takeover and checks that a subsequent death without a ragdoll is still taken over.

Full-body smash eligibility defaults on. With `gore.smash.fullBody` enabled, `fullBodyChance` (0–1, default 0.5) is a maximum multiplied by estimated footprint coverage squared, as described above (plus the volley bonus); setting 1 still requires a sufficiently broad, centered impact. The choice is stored across an early/deferred torso smash. Earlier cuts prevent full-body destruction to preserve detached pieces; an unavailable torso also falls back to a local smash. The staged torso cut hides the pelvis, suppresses stump effects and blocks further cuts; cleanup restores bones.

The default `poolScale` is now 0.85 and `poolGrow` is 4 seconds (the exponential growth timescale), reduced from 1.0 and from 8 respectively. Stub actors have a separate prewarmed stock of six procedural actors with the core material already applied; lids and plain actors retain their own stocks. They are created one at a time during the existing warm-up, and the pool is discarded on world reload. If depleted the same actor is created on demand. The latest session recorded 17–18 ms acquiring actors after exhausting the shared pool, and another 16–24 ms after section creation; material, repositioning and attachment are now separately timed. This change needs an in-game test, and moves actor/material setup into warm-up rather than removing its cost.

Floor-pool growth time is capped at the current verified radius while checks are pending, so a delayed check cannot release accumulated growth in a jump. The previous adjustment changed the default `poolScale` from 1.15 to 1.0; the current default is 0.85. `sim_smash_effects` checks a blocked pool and its first update after clearance. Slow pool updates report time spent growing, tracking/spawning decals, and checking geometry. Stub stages report geometry construction, actor acquisition and mesh-section creation separately. The latest session still showed 24–45 ms stub stages, so these diagnostics are intended to identify the remaining cost; they do not fix the cut hitch. In-game behavior needs verification.

Smash performance: the 13:51 playtest recorded a 91 ms hitch with a 45 ms two-cue smash sound and 12 ms blood burst in the same gore update; torso cut and opening stages also cost 24 and 19 ms. Delayed impact steps run oldest-ready first, at most one per render frame after their gore-update timers become ready, while unrelated timers continue normally. The smash sound's second cue is queued separately. Under load this trades some effect latency for fewer overlapping costs; individual sound loads, cut stages, physics and other systems may still hitch. `sim_smash_effects` checks FIFO spacing, independent timers and sound/effect behavior. A new in-game test is needed to measure the actual improvement.
Historically, hit bursts spawned immediately, but their transient floor splat was queued two gore updates later; hit particles are currently parked except for stump spurts. Cut sound, floor splat and each stump bleed are separately queued (one through four updates after the opening); simultaneous cuts can still overlap. Splat placement reuses the floor trace that was already done for its wall checks. `load_obj` reuses valid `/Script/` engine class/default-object wrappers, which live for the process, while game assets generally use fresh lookups; guarded rooted blood, sound and Niagara assets are the exceptions described above. The latest test showed 32 ms in death-hit blood, 29 ms in combined cut sound/splat and 29 ms in paired stump bleeds; these changes target those costs and require in-game verification. `sim_smash_effects` checks that the hit burst is immediate and the deferred splat appears. The 36 ms head-cut stage, 11–14 ms pool tracking and 416 ms enemy-near spike are separate and not established as fixed.

Opening position sampling rotates through three groups, one group per pool update, to distribute component lookups that appeared within 11–13 ms tracking/decal passes. Each opening is sampled roughly every 0.3 s and movement speed uses the actual elapsed sampling time; existing pool scale updates remain on every tick. Moving drips and pool placement may react later or miss brief motion. `sim_smash_effects` checks three openings are all sampled on separate passes and still creates chunk pools. Death logs also include each near-odds multiplier, so a rare waist outcome can be distinguished from a disabled option. `sim_odds` covers waist-level, downward and lengthwise hits. The latest session had one death, rolled a limb after a lengthwise hit; it does not prove waist cuts are broken.

Rooted gore sound, Niagara and floor material assets may be reused through a `ctx.keep` / `ctx.held` native lifetime guard. An asset is retained only if native rooting succeeded and the identity check succeeds immediately; a failed check discards the wrapper, clears the root record and goes through the normal path lookup and rooting again. Level reload discards these Lua references. Other game assets retain the fresh-lookup behavior. The decal timing now includes library/material lookup (previously excluded) as well as trace, spawn and scale; nearby-character timings distinguish building the live list from testing positions, and laser seeding reports its own scan cost. `sim_smash_effects` tests a guarded asset and replacement after its guard fails. The latest session reported 18–20 ms pool tracking/decal passes, a 23 ms cut splat, 11–12 ms bleeds, a 27 ms convulsion and 309/164 ms nearby-character spikes; whether this change improves them is unverified in game.

Floor-pool wall and floor-ring traces run incrementally, at most four traces per pool update globally, rotating fairly between active wounds. Wall and ring checks sample four directions (wall at two heights), instead of sixteen, including for one-shot splats. Narrow obstacles or gaps between samples can be missed. Only the first max(1, smash.drips) chunks are followed for pools and trails, reducing component polling and contention; other fragments have no landing pool. A playtest showed 13–21 ms pool updates and 39–83 ms splat-containing steps with the previous version. The improvement and pool appearance require in-game measurement. A pool remains at its last verified radius until the full wall or ring check finishes; many wounds can therefore make growth visibly slower. This bounds the wall/ring checks that appeared as repeated 20–79 ms pool updates in a multi-cut death. It does not eliminate separate one-shot splat, bleed, cut or sound costs. `sim_smash_effects` checks the trace budget, wall margin, successful ring and rejected gap. At ragdoll settle, `settle rotation` logs the fastest-turning main body once before it is stopped; this does not identify shaking on unmeasured accessory bones. In-game performance and reported ragdoll shaking remain unverified.

Hair and cinematics: automatic cloth resets and the follow-up F8 recovery are cancelled when the view switches to a cinematic camera, or when an active level sequence is detected as a reset becomes due. Temporary collision settings are restored immediately. A previous attempt to defer and retry scanned sequences repeatedly and was followed by reported stuttering while the reset remained blocked; that attempt was removed. The log names the camera class or all sequence actors that blocked the reset in one scan. The observed B07 ambient box, crane and robot actors share `EnvObj_SE05_` and are excluded by that prefix; a separately playing scene sequence still blocks the reset. `sim_cutscene_hair` checks these exclusions, concurrent sequences and that a persistent sequence does not trigger repeated scans. The prefix also excludes any cutscene sequence attached to one of these environment actors, so in-game cutscene verification remains important; the visual result needs a playtest.

Ragdoll setup remembers whether the pelvis was already simulating before `physfix.start`. If assigning or rebuilding a physics asset leaves it kinematic, the mod re-enables simulation before starting the death effects; a manual test starting without simulation is untouched. A 0.6.0 report showed the missing asset being assigned at the start of deaths that ended one poll later, but engine behavior on that player's setup still needs confirmation. `sim_animdeath` models the asset replacement stopping simulation.

Stump bleed history: the experimental `NS_StumpBlood` clone was removed after a crash, and the later repeated `NS_HitBlood_PC_01` player-hit effect showed visible pulses. The current code uses the unmodified game `NS_A_BloodLoop` as described above; the smash burst remains on the original flesh-slice effect. Its appearance and timing require an in-game check.

Weapon drop diagnostics log the component position and visibility when dropped, then the component and physics box positions, visibility, scale, pose master and attachment two seconds later; these observations do not change sword physics.

Smash weapon recovery: even when an animated death retains a ragdoll run without a grip, an upper-body smash also finds a visible weapon within 30 cm of either hand and drops it, leaving a weapon away from the hands alone. Before hiding a smashed subtree, the active ragdoll releases any gripped weapon on a hand in that subtree onto its normal collision box. If a drop cannot be made, the original grip attachment stays recorded for recovery, even if the weapon becomes hidden before its grip timer expires. `sim_smash_lifecycle` covers a torso release and failed-drop restoration.

`gore_hit_marks.lua` owns the living-hit decal query, placement, healing and removal; the gore module retains its public mark state for preload and world cleanup. A fake-engine simulation covers this lifecycle, while placement and appearance still need an in-game check. Living hit marks remain disabled by default and have no F3 toggle. The unfinished feature and its handler remain in the code; the developer test key is parked in `keys.lua`, so it is neither listed in F3 nor dispatched. The feature can be re-enabled manually through `gore.hitMarks.enabled` in settings.

Gorilla B’s two ThrowStone projectile blueprints have an `EventOnHit` carrying a world-space location; their collision sphere has a 100 cm radius in the asset. Once each class loads, a hook records nearby hits. A fresh mesh hit within 0.35 s and 150 cm of that event can use the closer head or upper-chest target (only when one is within 130 cm); this identifies the rock as blunt without relying on synthetic swing angle. Without that evidence the normal selection remains. Events and choices are logged, and `sim_smash_hits` covers targeting and stale/distant rejection; live event timing still requires game validation.

Blunt smash targeting uses estimated contact overlap area when a hit footprint is available, with surface and center-line distance breaking ties. Unknown sizes retain the previous comparison to approximate flesh radii (head and torso 14 cm, arms 4 cm, legs 6 cm): a surface-level tie within 2 cm can vary 25% of the time. In ordinary hit-based smashes, a torso result is kept with `torsoHitChance` (default 100%); an explicit lower value can redirect it to the nearest available head or limb. This balancing does not apply to a custom mix, full-body result or no-location death odds. Full-body eligibility defaults on and is weighted by footprint coverage; Custom Smash Mix defaults off and explicitly overrides category selection with relative `targetWeights` (all zero falls back to normal hit selection). Within a chosen category, impact geometry still selects the part. With no hit location an available part of the chosen category is selected at random. The decision persists across early/deferred processing. `sim_smash` and `sim_smash_area` exercise targeting and options; in-game balance is unverified. Mesh shake locations can be imprecise and do not identify the object that struck Eve.

Revive presentation: when manually revealing a held death screen skips the YouDied intro, play the game’s UI_YouDied SoundWave once (the widget itself uses this wave; the similarly named cue mixes in other sounds); ordinary unskipped intros retain the game’s sound. A death-screen button still triggers the safety-critical cleanup before respawn, but the visible pawn is hidden for 0.4 seconds around the reset and restored via its tracked live object; already-hidden pawns are untouched. `sim_revival_screen` checks the cue, hide, and restore.

The death roll excludes diagonal weight when diagonal cuts are disabled, including in proximity-adjusted odds, so an inactive saved weight cannot defeat a vertical-only selection. `sim_smash_hits` tests both disabled and enabled diagonal weights. No Vertical Splits redirects a lengthwise body blade to a neck or waist cut according to the hit’s position on the body axis, preserving follow-up limits and already-cut checks. The separate no-split death-roll path also leaves overhead torso rolls as torso cuts. Bare-hand detection off disables the no-weapon downward-hit fallback as well as hand/foot detection. `sim_nosplits_blade` and `sim_smash_hits` cover these cases.

Focus diagnostics: once per second the native foreground check tracks window focus; the log records up to three 10-second foreground windows of pawn ticks and average mod tick cost after a focus change. Menu-paused samples are discarded. Pawn ticks are only a proxy for FPS and do not measure renderer or GPU cost.

How the mod works, how it is built and every tuning knob, for anyone working on it. The player's guide is the [README](../README.md).

A gore mod for Stellar Blade: when Eve dies she ragdolls, bleeds and can lose a limb. Three parts:

- **Data patch**, `ZZ_StellarBladeGore_P` (under 1 MB: death shows that ragdoll the player, the death table, voice), an IoStore pak built from your installed game and the other mods in it, and `ZZ_StellarBladeGore_Face_P`, her death face animation alone (Proper Death Expression's `EVE_Facial_ActionVoice_DIE1`): Customized Ragdoll's `death_expression_p` ships a `DIE1` of its own, and two mod containers with the same package keep the game on its loading screen, so its users delete ours. The build also makes `ZZ_StellarBladeGore_Outfits_P` (under 1 MB: every outfit's physics asset with the ragdoll fixes), which no longer ships: `physfix.lua` and `sbslice.dll` make the same fixes in game on every outfit, any mod's too, and `test/run.sh phys` holds them to it value for value. It's installed only with `SB_OUTFITS=1`.
- **UE4SS script** (`StellarBladeGore`): [gore](#gore) (dismemberment through her own skinning, caps, blood), the [active ragdoll](#active-ragdoll) (masses, momentum, impact, muscle tone), [fixes for outfits the data patch missed](#other-mods), respawn fixes and a log.
- **`sbslice.dll`**: a small native helper the gore needs, loaded by UE4SS.

Formerly `sb-eve-ragdoll-death` / `EveRagdollDeath` / `EveRagdollProbe`; the installers remove the old names.

Copy synchronization is managed by `gore_copies.lua`: the native `y` command accepts at most 16 destinations, so synchronization and recovery each send larger groups in batches. Discovery reads the current clone and cut lists; world cleanup clears the shared copy bookkeeping. The flush preserves cuts queued from callbacks, retries after errors, and stops using a copy set or cut list replaced by cleanup. The fake-game regression checks these cases and 20 copies, but compatibility with actual external mods still needs a playtest.

`hit_classification.lua` owns blunt-versus-sharp classification and smash dispatch, using live settings, tracked swing and gore callbacks. `hit_classification` checks replacing those dependencies; `smash_hits`, `blast` and `rock` exercise the classifier in a fake game. Real attack evidence and appearance still require a playtest.

`projectile_hits.lua` handles projectile impact geometry, callbacks and rock correction; the main loop supplies the current pawn, mesh hit and gore service and retains post-death queue and cleanup ownership. `rock`, `blast`, `smash_area` and `smash_hits` cover fake hooks, impact-time pose, projectile identity and bounded queue behavior; actual engine hooks and hit locations need a playtest.

`hits_after.lua` owns post-death swing selection, attack gating, cut dispatch and queued projectile dispatch. Its shared hit counters and queues are reset by the main death lifecycle, and it obtains live settings, swing and gore services through callbacks. `hits_after`, `after`, `after_nomontage` and `smash_hits` cover fake projectile dispatch, motion and follow-up cuts; actual attack timing and geometry need game verification.

`after_hit_geometry.lua` reuses each successful socket read during a single follow-up hit calculation; shared forearm and calf joints no longer require a second engine read in that pass. The cache is discarded after each calculation and failures are retried. `after_geometry` checks call counts, movement on the next pass and retry; `after` and `after_nomontage` check fake-game cuts. No in-game frame-time improvement is established.

Weapon-sweep selection reuses successful shared-joint socket reads while building one fallback pose. It resamples on the next sweep and retries a failed shared read. `swing_perf` compares live-pose selection against the independent reference and checks those read counts; it does not measure actual game frame time.

The `lowarm` simulation now checks actual cut records across side selection, recovery, settings reload and outfit changes, while `fall` checks takeover and fall cases against cut state. These strengthen fake-game coverage; they cannot establish real outfit appearance or engine behavior.

`cloth_reset.lua` owns cloth-reset scheduling, cinematic suppression, collision restoration and hair simulation; state stays on `screen` and live lifecycle services are supplied by the main loop. `clothscan`, `f7cloth`, `cutscene_hair`, `reload` and `lowarm` cover fake resets, recovery interruptions and detached-arm collision; real hair and physics need a playtest.

`split_plane.lua` supplies anatomical side selection, split-plane construction and blade slant to the staged gore lifecycle. `split_plane` checks midline, yaw, side and mirror geometry; `split`, `lowarm`, `smash_hits` and opt-in `diag` exercise the callers in the fake game. Actual cut position, appearance and animation need in-game verification.

`outfit_animation.lua` manages outfit physics-node suppression and split rig restoration. `gore.forget` discards old rig references and address-keyed animation caches without probing old objects; ordinary recovery still restores live rigs. `outfit_animation` checks the production cleanup entry point, fake freed objects and address reuse; real reloads and outfit physics need a playtest.

`split_break.lua` selects spine constraint breaks for split halves, accounting for the plane, tolerance and retained limb ancestry. `split_break` checks synthetic topology, missing bones and break selection; `split` and opt-in `diag` exercise callers in the fake game. Real skeletons and physics need game verification.

`split_cut.lua` handles split preparation, first-half cut and second-half handoff, while the gore lifecycle retains scheduling, rollback and opening. It reads the current cut list and appends through the owner. `split_cut` tests prior-cut rejection, saved-weight restoration on copy failure, stage handoff and degenerate halves; `split`, `smash_hits` and opt-in `diag` exercise fake-game integration. Actual native cuts and physics still need a playtest.

## How it works

Deaths are driven by `SBShowData` "shows". `Local/Data/CharacterDeadTable` row `Player` picks the show per death type. Most of them (stand, skill steps, explosions) are the shared `Dead/Dead_Monster_*` shows, which already contain an `SBShowRagdollKey` (channel 23 in `KeyContanerMap`). The player never ragdolls only because the key's constructor sets `bIgnorePlayer = true` (`mov byte [rbx+0x1F0], 1` in `USBShowRagdollKey::USBShowRagdollKey`), and no asset overrides it.

The patch (`mod/ragdoll_spec.json`):

- **clone**: copies the shared `Dead_Monster_EffectType_Stand / Explosion / ExplosionLight` and `SkillType_Step0-3` shows to `CH_P_EVE_01/Ragdoll/EveRD_*` with `bIgnorePlayer=false` and Eve-sized impulses (the enemy values of 5k–18k launched her across the map). The `Player` row of the death table points at the copies, so enemy deaths stay fully vanilla. Every `*DeadUIDelayTime` of that row is 0, as vanilla: the death screen is made at once and the script holds it hidden (its intro waits while hidden) till `reviveAt` or Shift+F4, as `deathScreen` says, so it fades in only after her fall and the gore have played out. (At `reviveAt` the game started the intro only then, and Shift+F4 earlier showed an empty screen.) The game respawns her only once a button on that screen is pressed, and the script gets her up just before: when one of its buttons fires (`WB_MainHUD_Dead_C`'s `WB_Common_btn_1_Event`/`_2_Event` (either spelling, with `_0` or without) or `FinishPressAnimation`, hooked as soon as the class is loaded, tried once a second in a death; `FinishYouDieAnimation` says the screen came up; `hook death screen: ...` and `death screen: <function>` in the log) or the screen goes away once seen up (any instance of the widget but its class default, shown when it and its `CanvasPanel_Dead` are visible: it lives inside the main HUD and is never in the viewport itself; that one never before `recoverAfter` (12 s), so her cut body stays behind the screen till then). A button gets her up at once, `recoverAfter` or not: pressed 9 s in (the screen shown early with Shift+F4) the game's respawn came first and set her meshes while they still held her cut weights, which UE 4.26 frees unreleased when a mesh with another LOD count goes on (`InitLODInfos`, `CleanUpOverrideSkinWeights`): "A FRenderResource was deleted without being released first!" (playtest) (`tune.recoverOn` "button"; "timer" gets her up at `recoverAfter`, under the screen, as before). A death that shows no screen by `recoverAfter` + `screenWait` (4) s (the mod's own deaths, F5's) gets her up then. With a revive item on her the game puts its death screen up the moment she dies, over her death: whichever screen is up before `reviveAt` (10) s into the death (`WB_MainHUD_Dead`, or `WB_MainHUD_ResurrectionItem` should that be the one) is held hidden and disabled till then, about when the death screen fades in without one, and she waits while it is up. Both are caught as they are made (`newobj.lua`'s watch on `UserWidget`, the class read; the death screen is made as the level loads, while her tick still waits 2 s on a new level, and it went stale before it was ever checked (`keepFresh`), so the hold never found it and the screen came up with `deathScreen` "key" (playtest): a death screen not held is looked for once a death by its class from 1 s in): a scan of every object for the death screen every 2 s while it wasn't found was 60 ms a time in a big level, and its class loads only at the death; one scan a death is left, from 5 s in. When the held screen goes away after it showed and no death screen is up 2.5 s later, she was revived and is got up at once (`revive screen: made / up / shown / gone` and `recovering ... (revived with the item ...)` in the log). Shift+F4 in a death holds it (`screen.frozen`): both screens are held hidden and disabled whenever up, their going away is not counted, and she isn't got up, till Shift+F4 again restores them. While it is held the game's photo mode may open (`tune.deathPhoto`): photo mode is native (its UI, `ASBPhotoModePawn`), entered from the settings menu or the `PhotomodeOneKey` / `PhotomodeTwoKey1+2` actions (P; D-pad left + R1), and turned down while she is dead. The game keeps named constants in a singleton (`exe+0x7032540`, filled with defaults at `0x25d3ad0`); its bools are a `TMap<u32, bool>` at `+0x378+0xf0`, keyed by `FCrc::Strihash_DEPRECATED` of the name (`0x117c180`). The cheat that overrides one (`SBConfigOverrideBool`) is a stub in the shipping build, as are `SBPhotoMode` and the other photo mode cheats (their exec thunks read the parameters and return), so `sbslice.dll`'s `f1` / `f0` write `SBPhotoMode_Bypass_ActivationConditionChecks` (default 0) itself, the key hashed with the game's own function and `SBPhotoMode_Trim_LetterBox` looked for beside it as a check that it is the table. It is set while the death is held and cleared when it is let go or she gets up. The bypass alone did nothing: the death also keeps the key from reaching photo mode's key handler (`0x142362d00`), and the check that decides (`0x141cd3b10`, asked by the `PhotoModeActive` event's handler `0x141abe100` after the key handlers' `0x142361aa0` queues the event) never reads it in the shipping build, and says no to her dead (her state at `+0x2600`, the open UI list `SBPhotoMode_CheckUITypeList`, ...). So P in a held death has `sbslice.dll` (`f2`) call what that handler calls once the check passes, `0x1422a98b0()`: the enter itself, which takes the photo mode manager (a singleton at `exe+0x7032598`, made at `0x141adc360`), checks `0x14236b800`, sets its state `+0x84` to 1 and registers its tick. It is only called while the manager is idle (`+0x38`, `+0x84` 0); the answer also logs the manager and the key path's gates (`f3` only reports). P in a death not held yet holds it first (`screen.hold`, Shift+F4's), and lets that hold go as photo mode closes, or at once if it didn't open (`screen.photo_unhold`: kept, the death screen never came back and she couldn't respawn, with nothing to say Shift+F4 was needed, Nexus report); a hold made with Shift+F4 stays. Once in, photo mode sets every character mesh's collision to query only and its Pawn channel to overlap (`0x1423a33e0`, over the `MeshInfoList` slots: virtuals `+0x668` `SetCollisionEnabled(1)` and `+0x860` `SetCollisionResponseToChannel(2, 1)`), and puts them back on leaving: on her ragdoll that ended the physics, the mesh went back to its animated pose (a cut-off head onto her neck) and was flung from there as it closed. So `f2<mesh>` patches those two slots of her mesh's class vtable in place (once per class, at most 4) with stubs that drop her mesh's calls while the manager's `+0x84` is not 0 and pass every other call on to what the slots held; the patch stays (another mod may chain onto it), `f5` only stops naming her mesh (`blocked=N` in `f2` / `f3`'s answer counts the calls stubbed out). Her mesh used to be moved onto a copy of its vtable instead: UUEPBS (a body slider) patches `FinalizeBoneTransform` (slot 303) in the class vtable and finds the engine's function by the object's vtable, so on the copy it called nothing and her pose froze at the cut through every death (Nexus report). The game's own photo mode key works while she is alive, cut by `F9` or not dead yet, and it put an `F9`-cut head back on her neck the same way (playtest), so `screen.photo_guard` shields her mesh with `f4<mesh>` as soon as she has a cut (`f5` takes it off once none is left) and reads photo mode's state every 0.5 s while she has one: the watch below takes over however photo mode opened (`photo mode opened by the game's own key`). The shield never caught a call there (`blocked=0`) and the head still went back, 213 cm within 0.5 s, its body simulating: photo mode pauses the game but keeps her ticking, so her animation runs while the post-physics step that blends the simulated bodies over it doesn't (a whole ragdoll kept its pose). So while `IsGamePaused` with a piece on her and her pelvis not simulating, `screen.photo_freeze` turns her mesh's tick off, checked every frame from her `ReceiveTick` before the 33 ms throttle (her tick runs before her mesh's), and back on when the pause ends. A piece cut off her alive instead goes onto a copy of her mesh for photo mode (`hide_pieces`, sbslice `P`), so her own mesh ticks on and takes photo mode's poses and expressions: the copy keeps the piece's bones as they lie and moves every other bone onto the nearest piece root before scaling it to nothing (scaled where each stood as photo mode opened, skin weighted to several of them still spanned them, a patch of her suit left in the air once a pose moved her, playtest). Photo mode pauses the game but not the script, so while it is on (`screen.photo_watch`) the tick does nothing but read photo mode's state every 0.5 s (`f3`, its manager's `+0x84`) and her ragdoll's (`photo mode on: ...`, logged when it changes, with how far her head is from where it was before), undoing the game's lockup and pose snapshot whenever they show up. Once `+0x84` is 0 again the enemy's weapon trail starts over (its jump over the pause read as a swing) and hits after death wait `gore.photoGrace` s. Photo mode looks through its own pawn: the held camera's tick sees the view target turn into a `PhotoMode` class and stands aside, and once photo mode closes it makes itself the view target again. If the game respawns her while she is still down anyway (`NotifyBP_ReInitActor`), she is got up at once. Getting her up also clears the game's own ragdoll state (`RagdollInfo.EnableRagdollCount`, `CurrentActiveTime`, `BlendWeight`), which a real death leaves set until the respawn clears it: the revive in place never respawns her, so she stayed ragdolled to the game, stuck to the floor, and her next death's ragdoll key did nothing. Her movement, off (`MOVE_None`) since the death, is put back to walking if it still is 3 s after she got up with no respawn. The log's `recovering after ...` ends with which of these it was. While she plays, a limb cut off her alive goes onto such a copy too once its roots have moved under `hm.STILL_CM` (2) cm for `hm.STILL_S` (1) s (main.lua `hm.piece_watch`, `hide_pieces` with bake; the copy taken off her capsule so it stays put, her bones hidden on another mod's copies of her mesh as well): grabbing a ladder drew her mesh on her animation without its physics for a frame or two, and the cut-off arm showed on her shoulder (Nexus report, 0.5.0). Not a head or upper body (her face and hair would go along). It goes back on her mesh (`hm.piece_back`) for a new cut, a regrow (before the dissolve stops its bodies), her death, a change of her cut parts and `gore.cleanup` (F8, an outfit change); photo mode leaves it there. A copy that fails puts the piece back on her and isn't tried again for those parts (`piece settled: no copy ...`). `ue4ss/test/run.sh piece`.
- **inject**: adds a ragdoll key, cloned from `Dead_Monster_SkillType_Step1`, to Eve's own death shows: `P_Eve_Dead`, `P_Eve_Dead2`, `P_Eve_Dead3`, `P_Eve_Dead_Nikke`, `Eve_Dead_Down`, `P_Eve_Dead_Falling`, `P_Eve_AirDead` and the laser trap deaths `P_Eve_Dead_Laser` / `P_EVE_Dead_Laser_BalanceBeam`. The laser shows had no ragdoll key (a 2 s additive twitch, shock decal and sparks), so she never simulated and the mod never saw the death; their key starts the ragdoll 0.35 s into the jolt with a soft 300 push, and the death cuts like any other. Each key is tuned per show (impulse, start time), and `bKeepPlaying` is set. Every Eve ragdoll key also gets `ImpulseLimitVelocity=700` and `CapsuleComponentFollowingSocketName=None`. The constructor defaults that to `Bip001-Pelvis`, which drags the player's still-colliding movement capsule onto the ragdoll every frame and flings her around spinning.
- **table** (in game since 0.4.0, below): `Player.AirborneDeadShow` points at the vanilla `CH_P_EVE_01/Basic/P_Eve_Dead_Air`, which does not exist, so airborne deaths had no show at all. It now points at `P_Eve_AirDead`. A death screen up while her HP bar is at 0 and no death started (`hm.lethal_watch`, every 0.1 s) is a death whose show gave no ragdoll. The screen's widget is made with the level and reads visible all along, and her HP bar reads 0 at 1 HP (a high fall can leave her there), so it counts only with proof of the death (`hm.dead_proof`): the screen's intro animation playing (`IsAnimationPlaying` over its class's `Animations`) or done (`FinishYouDieAnimation`, hooked), or her movement off (`MOVE_None`). Her movement alone is no proof: the game's falling death leaves it walking (playtest). A taken-over death ends if the player moves her (`GetCurrentAcceleration`, input only), as she lived. From a fall (her HP bar at 0 within 1.5 s of landing from 2 m or more, or the game's death broadcast naming `Landing_Height_Damage` / `Landing_Height_Stiff_Damage`), on land, with Fall Damage Ragdoll on, the mod turns her ragdoll on itself (`hm.take`: the `Ragdoll` collision profile, her bodies from `Bip001` down simulating; `death: taken over (...)`), and the death runs from there as any other, the death screen held; her collision goes back at its end (`saved.takeColl`). From a 16 m drop the game's falling death (`Landing_Height_Stiff_Damage`, `Custom5`, `P_Eve_Dead_Falling`, its ragdoll key at 0 s) gave no ragdoll in two playtests, she froze where she landed and the death screen came up at once; why is not known. Any other such death is taken over the same way since 0.5.0's report (enemy attacks straight to the death screen), and cut as any death; the log names the montage she plays (`hm.montage`). Her swimming deaths, the scripted ones (`hm.SCRIPTED`: the ocean, sliding, the desert's sand hole, the grinder) and a death while still falling with no landing or recent physical hit (`Custom4`, `Dead/Dead_Empty`: the level's kill volumes, `LV_Dead_Falling*`, she is warped back to safe ground) stay the game's; a recent on-body hit with a force permits the usual fallback even while airborne (`sim_fall`), though this may not cover attacks without a recorded physical hit and log `death NOT seen: ...` with the drop, the broadcast and the montage. The death broadcast is `SBNetworkPlayerController:ClientBroadcast_ActorDead` (hooked; `death: the game's death broadcast: effect ...`), which the game may not send in single player.

The rest of the HUD goes for the death (`tune.hideHud`, `screen.hud`): 1 s in, the main HUD (`WB_MainHUD_Master_C`, reached from the death screen's widget by its outers, its widget tree then the master, else kept from the last death, else one `FindAllOf` a death) is walked from its widget tree's root (`Canvas_HUD`, `Canvas_Notice`, `Canvas_Post`, `Canvas_TPS` under `CanvasPanel_Root`): each child not on the way down to a widget whose name or class holds `Dead`, `Resurrection` or `Revive` is collapsed, its visibility noted, and put back as the death ends (`HUD: N widgets hidden for the death` / `shown again`). The death screen's own branch is gone into, not hidden, so it keeps to `deathScreen`. Not with F7's test ragdolls.

Since 0.4.0 the pak carries no data table. Whole copies of `CharacterDeadTable` and `EffectTable` overrode every other mod's (a moveset mod's nano suits gone, a shop mod's purchase crashing, Nexus reports), so `tables.lua` changes the rows in whatever tables the game loaded, every 3 s till both are done and again when a table object is remade: the `Player` row's eight death shows (sbslice `D`, an FString set in place), its `*DeadUIDelayTime` (`d`: 0 for `deathScreen` "game", else `reviveAt`), and `Down_Eve` (`A`: a deep copy of `Down` added to the row map, its FStrings and arrays in buffers of its own, the TSet's hash checked to be UE 4.26's FName hash (`fname_hash`: the entry's block and offset spread apart, plus the number; not the index plus the number, which no row matched in game), a free slot taken or the sparse array grown through GMalloc; then `d` sets its ID) with the three tumbling knockdowns' `ChainEffectAliasArray` re-pointed from `Down` to it (`N`). The heap fields to copy come from the row struct's reflection. The game resolves a chained effect through two maps of its own, built from the table at load: alias -> ID (`TMap<FName, int32>`) and ID -> alias (`TMap<int32, FName>`, hashed by the ID itself), elements of 20 bytes each. A row added to the table alone is in neither (a knockdown chained into it stood her straight up), so `tables.lua` finds them once a launch: `Y` scans private read-write memory for the knockdown row's name and `Yd` for its ID, the hits whose next value is the ID (or the name) are elements, `Mh` / `Mi` find the TSet header holding one (a qword pointing at or before it, a multiple of 20 back, followed by a plausible count, bits and power-of-two hash size, then every element checked to hash as UE 4.26's), and `M` adds `Down_Eve` to both after checking `Down`'s ID under its alias and its alias under its ID match the table's row (about a second in all, in the lobby). The scans (`&` + the command) run on a thread of the DLL's own so the game goes on meanwhile (`&?` asks for the answer); the game freeing memory under one (a level streaming in) crashed it, so an access violation on that thread is caught by a first-in-line vectored handler that resumes the thread at its `__builtin_setjmp` (`err memory freed`), and `tables.lua` runs the scan again 2 s later, up to 10 times. The readable-memory check's one-region cache (`stats.c` `readable`) belongs to the first thread that uses it; others always ask `VirtualQuery`. The log's `tables:` lines say what was done.

- **collision**: Eve's blueprint (`CH_P_EVE_01_Blueprint`) has two components on her own skeleton that block `PhysicsBody`: the sword box `WeaponPhysicsCollision` (on `Bip001-Prop1`) and the `SBHair` mesh. A ragdoll runs on the `Ragdoll` profile (object type `PhysicsBody`), so they shove her own bodies every frame. The patch appends an Ignore entry for `PhysicsBody` to their raw collision responses; nothing else in the blueprint changes. Outfit or hair mods that replace this blueprint will conflict.

- **hit bone**: vanilla keys leave `bOverrideHitBone` false, so every death pushes `ImpulseBoneName` (Spine1). Ragdoll key code (`0x14245eb70`) takes the bone from the hit info when the flag is set and falls back to `ImpulseBoneName` when the hit has none. Eve's keys set it, so a leg hit folds the leg and a head hit snaps the head. Explosions keep the centre push.
- **outfits**: every outfit mesh that `EquipmentTable` can put on Eve (SkeletalMesh on her body skeleton; hair excluded) gets its physics asset patched. `assetkit/outfits.py` finds them, and `build.sh` runs it before patching.
  - **physics**: vanilla physics assets let Pelvis collide with both thighs although their capsules overlap by ~10 cm at rest. The solver pushes them apart every frame, which was the flinging. Almost every other pair is disabled, so limbs pass through each other. The patch makes bodies collide across five groups (torso, each arm, each leg). Pairs inside a group never collide: some outfits ship with neighbouring torso bodies colliding, and CH_P_EVE_09 spun from it. Pairs closer than 0.5 cm in that outfit's bind pose stay disabled, for every body. The accessories (hair, cape, breast and hip jiggle, neck pieces: every body outside the five groups) collide with nothing of hers, only the world (`accessory_collision`, off): CH_P_EVE_09 still had 114 accessory pairs colliding with her main bodies, the hip and breast bodies inside her torso shoved the thighs and arms every frame and the cape and hair got pinned between her and the floor, so she shook on the ground (pelvis turning 150-300°/s while lying still). With `self_collision` off (the default) every pair of her bodies is disabled as well, the groups too: an arm or leg landing on her own body set every joint jittering until each part found the floor, and the runtime switch below didn't stop it. Limbs may pass through her. These pairs are measured per asset from the mesh's reference skeleton and the body capsules, and for `CH_P_EVE_30` the result matches the hand-checked list. `LinearDamping` drops from 5.0 to 0.2: at 5.0 she fell at about 2 m/s and looked floaty. `AngularDamping` drops from 10 to 1 (`body` in the spec; a body that has no such property gets one): she toppled and turned as if under water. Some outfits (25 physics assets, e.g. CH_P_EVE_09) give twist or helper bones a body, as the anchor of a cape or skirt chain, but no constraint to anything above it. In gameplay those bodies are kinematic, so it never shows; in a ragdoll they fly free and drag the skin weighted to them, which bent and stretched her arms. Each of them gets a locked joint to the nearest ancestor body, framed at the bind pose (`outfits.lock_orphans`, on by default). The main joints bend about their twist axis (PhAT framed them as hinges) within a range centred on a zero of the asset's own, as soft limits: knees went 19° past straight, elbows 24°, hips bent forward only 30° and back as far, the spine 5° a joint, and under a push the soft limits gave way further. Each joint gets the range of `outfits.ranges` (degrees its far end may swing towards her front and her back from the bind pose: knees 140 back and 3 forward, elbows 145 and 3, hips 110 forward and 20 back, shoulders 120 and 40, each spine joint 15 and 8, the neck 25 and 15). `outfits.py` measures each asset's zero at the bind pose and turns the joint's frame 2 about its twist axis to centre the range there. The shoulders and hips also get `inward` / `outward` (their sideways swing, Swing1: shoulders 25° in and 50° out, hips 15 and 45, from the bind pose; see the physfix paragraph below). `swing` overrides cone limits (none now: it spread the hips ±30°, which crossed her legs), and `hard_limits` turns every limit of the main joints hard (`bSoftConstraint` off). A main joint's motion limited to 0° (CH_P_EVE_09's pelvis-to-spine cone) is locked instead: a hard limit with no range shook it every frame. `locked_joints` locks joints whole (none: locked clavicles hung both arms rigidly off the light neck and the spine buzzed instead). Any motion limited under `lock_below` (6°): the 5° clavicle cones, shoulder roll, hip sideways swing, neck and pelvis-to-spine twist sat at their hard limits all the time and every joint buzzed, even in mid-air. These bodies are kinematic in gameplay, so only ragdolls change.
  - **cpuaccess** (off, `outfits.cpuaccess`): sets `bAllowCPUAccess` on LOD0 of every outfit mesh, for a game without `sbslice.dll`, which keeps every skeletal mesh's LOD0 CPU copy itself. Repacked copies of outfit mods' meshes (SBPR's) crashed the game's async loader on some outfit changes, so the meshes aren't shipped.

  With `cpuaccess` on, every patched mesh is a full copy in the outfits pak (all 134 make it about 560 MB compressed). An outfit mod's mesh is patched in its own version, see [Other mods](#other-mods). Use `outfits.only` / `outfits.skip` (mesh-name globs) in the spec to trim the list, or set `outfits.enabled` to false to drop all of it. Meshes and physics assets that don't round-trip byte-exactly through UAssetAPI are skipped and listed in the build log. `ClothingAssetCommon` exports stay raw bytes, because UAssetAPI loses cloth LOD data when it writes them back.

Scripted deaths are left vanilla: swimming, ocean, sliding, desert hole and grinder. The laser and balance beam deaths get the injected ragdoll key above, and `main.lua` cuts them along the beam (`sw.laser_hit`: `SBLaserComponent.LaserSegmentArray`, each segment's `StartPosition` / `EndPosition` / `HitActor`; the components kept as they are made, `newobj.lua`, and seeded with the characters once a level, not looked for at the death: that `FindAllOf` was 35 ms of the death's cut frame).


The living right-arm cut’s dropped sword copy waits until the real sword has stayed within 40 cm of Eve’s head for 0.3 s before flying back to her hair. If the real sword moves away during the 0.6 s flight, the copy returns to its starting position and original world scale, with its physics and collision restored; a completed return removes the copy and shows the real sword. `ue4ss/test/run.sh sword_return` covers a false approach, an interrupted flight and a completed return.

## Build / install

Needs `git`, `cargo`, `python3` and `curl`. The .NET 10 SDK is fetched into `.cache/` if it is missing. Point `SB_GAME` at another install if needed.

```bash
./build.sh --install
```

```bash
./build.sh --uninstall
```

`./build.sh --install` installs the core pak only; `SB_OUTFITS=1` adds the outfits pak (a dev reference: the in-game fix leaves an asset it patched alone).

### Other mods

The build reads every container in `Paks/~mods` (subfolders too) over the game's own, in the order the game mounts them (paths sorted case-insensitively, a later one wins), and patches what they ship in their version: a nude or outfit mod's mesh and physics asset, a face mod's face, a mod's `EquipmentTable` and the new meshes it points at. So an outfit mod's look stays and gets the cut and the ragdoll fixes. Packages taken from another mod are listed in the build log and `dist/sources.txt`. Rebuild (`./build.sh --install`) after adding or removing such a mod: the paks hold a copy of what they patched, so a removed mod's outfit would stay, and a new one would load unpatched. A mod that loads after ours (its file name sorts after `ZZ_StellarBladeGore_P`) wins in game over what both replace; the build names it, and renaming it to sort earlier fixes that. Mods that only replace textures or materials don't touch anything of ours. Encrypted containers can't be read and are skipped with a note. Mod containers of an older TOC layout (`DirectoryIndex`, as Eve with no Clothes on ships; the game's are `PartitionSize`) are read alongside the game's: `assetkit/patches/retoc-mixed-toc.patch` lets retoc mix layouts that share the chunk IDs and package format (everything up to `PerfectHash`). An outfit mod's mesh exported without a physics asset (Eve with no Clothes on's `CH_P_EVE_InnerSuit`) still gets CPU access (the build log names it), and in game `physfix.lua` gives it the outfit's own `<mesh>_Physics` (patched by the data mod), else `tune.physfix.fallback` (the base body's): without one she has no bodies and can't ragdoll at all. A given asset is the component's own and can be dropped again (gore's undo re-makes the bodies from the mesh's asset, which is none): `physfix` gives it again on the next tick she is animated (`lost the physics asset it was given`), and every script asks `physfix.asset` (component's, mesh's, else the given one) for her physics asset. Mods only ever change our input: the cache key covers their containers.

Without the outfits pak (the release has none) every outfit is caught by `physfix.lua` when she puts it on: the log says `physfix: outfit ..., physics ...: NOT patched, LOD0 CPU access false`. Without CPU access it may not be cut (gore logs the DLL's `no CPU copy`), and she just ragdolls; the flag isn't the whole story (InnerSuit read false and still cut). sbslice.dll now forces it at load (it patches `USkeletalMesh::NeedCPUData` to answer yes for LOD 0, `cpu copy LOD0 on` in the `sbslice.dll ready` log line), so meshes loaded after UE4SS starts keep their CPU copy without the data patch (confirmed in game: SBPR's outfits cut with no Outfits pak), and the log then says `LOD0 CPU access kept by sbslice.dll` instead of warning. Its physics gets what can be done from the script: the main bodies' damping (in the asset's defaults, bodies re-made while she is animated), hard joint limits, the torso's jiggle bodies (breasts and other leaf bodies under the pelvis, spine or neck) set to collide with nothing (`UBodySetup.CollisionReponse`: inside her chest they shoved her arms and torso every frame and she buzzed on the floor, as CH_P_EVE_30 did with no Outfits pak while CH_P_EVE_10, which has none, lay still), and a locked joint for each loose body (twist and cape anchors with no joint: CH_P_EVE_29's thigh twists carry her upper thighs' skin), the build's own: `physfix.add_joints` makes a `PhysicsConstraintTemplate` framed at the bind pose (`outfits.py`'s `orphan_joints`) and the DLL's `j<asset>,<joints>,<first joint>,<new>...` appends it to `ConstraintSetup`, found as the asset's TArray of that length whose first element is that joint (without the DLL, a locked constraint component while she is ragdolled). Self-collision stays off for such an asset. The rest of the data patch's physics is made in game too, so such an outfit ends up as the build would have patched it: the main joints' twist ranges are re-centred on her bind pose (`tune.physfix.ranges`, the spec's `outfits.ranges`; `sbslice.dll`'s `b<mesh>,<bone>,...` composes each bone's bind pose from the mesh's `FinalRefBonePose`, and `physfix.joint_ranges` is `outfits.py`'s `joint_limits` step for step), motions limited under `lockBelow` (6) degrees are locked, the hips' sideways swing is set (`swing`), and none of her bodies collide with each other (`collisionTable`), but while she is alive the vanilla asset's pairs that aren't two main bodies (`physfix.alive_pairs`): her ponytail simulated on her own mesh (CH_P_EVE_30's `Ab-TL-HairB01`-`04`) collides with her head, neck, back and arms there, which holds it on her back and in her hair pin; filled in, it sank through them into the pin and jittered (playtest; F8, which stopped those bodies simulating, put it right). The DLL's `L<asset>,<bodies>` lists the pairs a table lets collide (`ok colliding N a,b ...`), read once per asset before its first `t`; each death's table (below) leaves them out, so in the ragdoll her hair touches nothing of hers as before, and a cut on her alive (F9, Lose Arm at Low HP) no longer turns her `PhysicsBody` response off when the table is filled (its main bodies are apart already; off, the ponytail sank again). The collision table (`CollisionDisableTable`, a `TMap<FRigidBodyIndexPair, bool>` the physics scene reads by pointer when her bodies are made) isn't reflected, so the DLL's `t<asset>,<bodies>` finds it by its contents: the first TSet from the asset's start (the scan reaches past its end, where the next asset may keep a table of its own; later assets are looked at only at the offset found, `+208` in this build) whose every element is a pair of body indices sitting in the hash bucket the pair's hash puts it in, with every bucket's chain reaching exactly its elements. That proves the layout and the hash (`a + 23 b`) before anything is written; the new arrays (every pair) come from GMalloc and the old ones go back to it. Without the DLL the torso's jiggle bodies get "collides with nothing" instead (`quietJiggle`). `native/sbslice/test/run.sh phys` holds all of it to the build: `test_phys.c` checks both commands on made-up data, and `phys/parity.py` runs `physfix.lua`'s fix on every vanilla outfit's physics asset (made up from the build cache's JSON) and compares every value it leaves with the build's patched asset, the loose bodies' joints frame for frame (71 of 71). `tune.physfix` switches each part. Her arms collide with her body and with each other (`physfix.armCollision`): each death the DLL writes the collision table again (`t<asset>,<bodies>,<a>,<b>...`: every pair but the ones listed, `physfix.arm_table`), leaving out the pairs of her upper arms, forearms and hands against `Pelvis`, `Spine`, `Spine1` and `Spine2`, each arm's bodies against the other's, her forearms and hands against her thighs, and each leg's thigh, calf and foot against the other's. A pair whose bodies are within `armGap` (2) cm of each other as she goes limp stays in the table, since bodies that start overlapping fling apart; the gap is between the bodies' own shapes (each `AggGeom` sphyl, sphere and box as a capsule, placed at the bone's world transform), as `outfits.py`'s `body_capsules` measures them (the bind pose leaves out only each upper arm against `Spine2`). Measured between the bones with a 22 cm margin, the upper arms never collided and the forearms mostly didn't (playtest 43). The mesh then blocks its own `PhysicsBody` channel, switched off and on so the engine filters her body pairs again by the new table (log: `arms: N arm pairs collide: ...; M touching at the start, left out (...)`). A cut keeps it (the table keeps every other pair apart, a broken joint's too); a split keeps the pairs whose bodies are both on her half, the table written again with only those and the channel switched off and on (across the waist: her arms and chest, not her hips), else her bodies stop colliding with each other as before (`split: her arms ...`). Each cut adds a piece's main bodies (at and below the cut bone) against the rest of her main bodies (`physfix.piece_collision`, `pieceCollision`), by the same gap rule where she is cut (the piece's root and the body it came off stay apart), the DLL's table taking up to 1024 colliding pairs (`PT_KEEP`); when she gets up (and when her cuts are undone) the table is written with no pair colliding but her hair's (`collision pairs reset`), and a piece cut off her standing pairs with nothing (the rest of her is kinematic, moved by the animation: her thigh threw the hand hanging beside it off at 2500 cm/s); log `cut piece <bone>: ...`. Split halves don't collide with each other (the copy ignores `PhysicsBody`): each half keeps the other half's bodies, freed and invisible, right where the other half's real ones are, and a component's bodies can only be switched on or off together from the script. The shoulders' and hips' sideways swing (Swing1, about frame 2's Z, the engine's default ±45° round her A pose, where the arm hangs 30° out: an arm folded 15° into her chest) is set by the range too: `inward` 25 and `outward` 50 degrees from the bind pose for the shoulders, 15 and 45 for the hips (the hips' ±30° crossed her legs; the spec's `swing` override is gone), Swing1 their half sum, and frame 2, turned for the twist, turned again about its Z by the bind pose's swing plus half their difference (`physfix.joint_ranges` and `outfits.py`'s `joint_limits`, held equal by the parity test, `PriAxis2` too).

[Customized Ragdoll](https://www.nexusmods.com/stellarblade/mods/3884) (`customrag.lua`, `tune.customizedRagdoll`): a Blueprint-only pak. Its `death_anim_p` replaces Eve's death animations (`Result_Stand_Dead`, `Eve_Stand_Dead2/3`, `Eve_Dead_DownFaceUp/Down`, `Eve_Dead_Explosion`) with copies carrying an `AN_RagdollManager` notify at 0.008 s; its `death_expression_p` holds the Blueprints under `CH_P_EVE_01/Animation/Facial/`. The notify (`Received_Notify`) finds an `A_RagdollManager_C` (`GetActorOfClass`) or spawns one attached to the player, and calls its `Process(Character)`. `Process` returns at once when the manager is `Active`; otherwise it keeps her `PhysicsAssetOverride` (`OGPA`) and her Visibility / PhysicsBody responses, records every non-hair socket's linear velocity, `SetPhysicsAsset(Anim_Eve_Dead_DownFaceDown_Physics, true)`, sets Visibility to Block and PhysicsBody to Ignore, simulates every body below `Root` with a `PhysicalAnimationComponent` (orientation and position strength 10000, its multiplier blended from 1 to 0 over `BlendTime` with `Blend` on, by `1 - k^2`), re-applies the recorded velocities as impulses clamped to `ForceLimit`, `EnableInput`, adds `CM_Ragdoll_C` to the camera manager and sets every `UserWidget`'s render opacity to 0 (kept in `WidgetMap`); 3 s on, any key in its `Key` array (`Revert`) puts all of it back. Its other keys ([ ] \, with Ctrl and Shift) step `ForceLimit`, `BlendTime` and `Blend` into the `CustomizedRagdoll` save slot (`SG_RagdollManager_C`). `CM_Ragdoll`'s `BlueprintModifyCamera` reads only the manager's `Active` and `Mesh`: the view at `Bip001-Pelvis` minus 200 cm down the view rotation, sphere-traced (2 cm) from the pelvis with her and her child actors ignored. The physics asset swap undoes `physfix.lua`'s and fights the cuts, so with the support on the mod spawns the manager itself while she lives (once a second from `hm.alive_watch`: `BeginDeferredActorSpawnFromClass`, attached, `Mesh` her mesh, `BlendAmount` 1 so its tick has nothing to blend, `Active` true, so the notify's `Process` does nothing), adds `CM_Ragdoll_C` in `camera_hold` instead of the held camera (disabled while photo mode is up) and removes it in `camera_release`. Its input stays off (no `EnableInput`). The death screen: `screen.hiding` false (the game's timing), `screen.revive` sets the death and revive screens' render opacity to 0 while not `screen.shown` (`rv.ghost`, put back as the death ends), and Shift+F4's first press shows them (`screen.fade`). Both classes not loading (the pak not there) is told once and the Free Camera is used.

`ue4ss/install.sh` keeps other UE4SS mods: ours are turned on by their `enabled.txt`, and it only takes our old lines out of `mods.txt`. `--uninstall` removes this mod's script and DLL and leaves UE4SS; `--uninstall-all` removes UE4SS too.

The script and DLL: `./ue4ss/install.sh` (see [UE4SS script](#ue4ss-script)).

The build pins and fetches [retoc](https://github.com/trumank/retoc), [UAssetAPI](https://github.com/atenfyr/UAssetAPI) and the [SB header dump](https://github.com/a5tronomy/SB), generates `.usmap` mappings from the headers (`assetkit/mkusmap.py`), extracts the assets from the installed game, patches them (`assetkit/patch_shows.py`), repacks `dist/ZZ_StellarBladeGore_P`, `dist/ZZ_StellarBladeGore_Face_P` and `dist/ZZ_StellarBladeGore_Outfits_P` (`.utoc`, `.ucas`, `.pak`) (UE4.27 TOC layout, zlib blocks through `assetkit/patches/retoc-compress.patch`; `SB_COMPRESS=none` stores them raw) and reads the result back to verify it (`assetkit/verify.py`). The output lands in `SB/Content/Paks/~mods/`.

The source side (extracted packages, their JSON, the outfit scan) is cached in `.cache/work` under a key of the game's and the other mods' containers, the mappings, the tool sources and revisions and the spec's outfit selection, so later builds only patch, pack and verify; the first build after a game update extracts everything again. Outfit meshes never go through JSON: `sbtool meshinfo` reads what the outfit scan needs, `sbtool cpuaccess` sets the flag in place and `sbtool checkcpu` verifies that nothing else changed. sbtool's batch commands run `SB_JOBS` packages at a time (default: half the cores), and retoc compresses blocks in parallel.

Tuning: edit `mod/ragdoll_spec.json` (field names as in `USBShowRagdollKey`) and rebuild. `DirectionType`: 0 Static, 1 HitDirection, 2 TargetToSelf, 3 HitLocationToSelf, 4 Velocity, 5 StaticLocal.

Since 0.4.1 the script does nothing for its first 10 s after the lobby's player start (`screen.START_S`): her tick hook goes on at `ClientRestart` but returns at once, and the new-object watches (widgets, the photo mode pawn, characters: `newobj.lua`, below) are registered on the first tick after it (`screen.late`). A launch crash in the audio mixer's thread, as the lobby loaded, kept coming with the mod's sound loading put off.

## Gore

A cut opened before the death show can lose its broken skeletal constraints when `physfix.start` assigns an asset and rebuilds bodies. `physfix.remakes` tracks successful rebuilds; after death setup rebuilds the current mesh, `gore.rebreak_cuts` re-breaks opened cuts on that mesh, using their original kept-chain exclusions. It does not rerun cut effects or reapply impulses. `sim_animdeath` tests the rebuilt and unchanged paths; `sim_lemi` checks the actual asset-assignment counter. In a grab the head now waits for her death with the body and split (`blade_decision.early_waits`, covered by `sim_grab_cut`): a beheading opened under the finisher read as reattached once the show moved her on. Slow motion on death (`death_slowmo.lua`, `tune.deathSlowmo`, on by default) runs the game slower for as long as it is set to (defaults 0.15 for 2.5 s, no caps), then restores full speed; Esc's fast forward stops it first, and recovery and level reloads always hand time back. The mod's own gore and voice pitch down with it (the game's sounds and music keep theirs), and the ragdoll's clock runs on game time so its speed governors stay true in slow deaths. `sim_death_slowmo` covers the window, validation and handoff; game feel still needs a playtest. `gore.noBursts` (off by default) keeps fresh stumps dry: the stump spurts, the smash blood clouds and the split seam's hit burst are skipped, while splats, drips, pools and meat stay. `enemies.forceGrabs` (off by default, experimental) renames what each enemy close or lunging attack branch plays to its tree's own grab skill (verified against the shipped trees: grab branches live behind effect checks like `M_Raven_BetaGrabCheck` with dice rolls, distances and cooldowns around them; the tree itself is untouched, so selection still follows range and phase). Trees without a grab skill (the Elder's grab lives in the fight's script, not its tree) and dodges, guards, ranged shots and get-ups keep theirs; the saved originals still feed Hits After Death. Clawed enemies with no weapon meshes are judged by name (`smash.species`): the stalker's swipes cut instead of smashing. `sim_force_grabs` covers the pick, swap, late-tree catch-up and putback, `hit_classification` the species judgement; whether renamed branches play out fully in game needs a playtest. Her sword flies with the death now (`active.weaponThrow`, 400 cm/s along the killing blow on top of her hand's speed; 0 drops it by her side as before), covered by `sim_sword_throw`; where it lands still needs a playtest. Manual slow motion (`manual_slowmo.lua`, `tune.manualSlowmo`, off by default, experimental for now) runs the game at the death slow motion's speed till the Slow Motion key (Y) again; a death's own window or fast forward owns the clock while it runs, and the ragdoll clock and our sounds follow. Motion blur is held off while slow (dilation smears the velocity buffer) and her hair's simulated bodies are kept awake (asleep under dilation they freeze mid-sway and read as floating). `sim_manual_slowmo` covers the toggle, validation and reset, `sim_slowmo_pp` the blur volume, camera override and boom de-lag; game feel still needs a playtest. Trail speeds (swing selection, follow-up hits) divide by game time, so slow motion stops hiding fast swings; the pause detector compares against the expected slow speed instead of full speed. The reported boss-finisher behavior and whether the rebuilt bodies preserve their separated positions remain unverified in game.

For the reported 0.6.3 Brute death crash, `sfx` reacquires sound cues by path on each play and plays both smash layers in the same update, as before 0.6.3; effects and floor materials retain guarded caching. This is an A/B candidate, not a confirmed fix: the crash was on the audio mixer thread immediately after a smash sound, but an older startup crash reached the same address even with mod sound loading delayed. Restoring same-update layers can increase frame hitches. `sim_smash_effects` checks both layers and the sound lookup path; it cannot reproduce the native mixer crash.

The game's own dismemberment (`ASBCharacter` slicing, `0x142129c30`, used by the player's sword on some monsters) can't work on Eve. It copies the cut region into procedural meshes and hides it on the original through a vertex-colour override plus the material parameter `DissolveMaskRadiusFactor`. Only the gore master `MA_CH_DamageGore` has that parameter, and cooked materials can't be given one. So the cut is made in the skinning instead, on her own mesh with her own materials.

`native/sbslice` is a tiny freestanding DLL (clang + lld, no MSVC; `native/sbslice/build.sh`). It loads as a UE4SS C++ mod and waits for `gore.lua` to send it the address of the unused cheat UFunction `SBRewardGroupDropFormationPathLocation`. It then swaps that function's native thunk for its own handler, which takes commands through the cheat's string parameter. The exe has no ASLR.
