Every engine address it uses (`resolve_addresses`, as it loads) is checked by its bytes at the Steam build's address first, and looked for by byte pattern across the code section (`.trace`) when they differ, the globals read out of the found functions (GMalloc, the photo manager and config getters, GUObjectArray from `FWeakObjectPtr::Get`); its answer says `addresses: steam`, `found` or which are missing, and with any core one missing it refuses. The thunk is matched by its address, else taken from the UFunction's `Func` (+0xd8) when that points into the code section. Its one code patch is `NeedCPUData` (every skeletal mesh keeps a LOD0 CPU copy), checked against the expected bytes first. `sbslice.rc` gives the DLL a version resource (version from main.lua, built by `llvm-rc`): a bare DLL with no CRT, resources or signature was deleted as a false positive by a few antivirus scanners' machine-learning verdicts.

How a cut works (`c<component>,<lod>,<bone>`, a plane point and a normal):

- **Split.** For a plane through bone B, the DLL reads LOD0's positions, indices and skin weights (CPU copies) and skins every vertex in the current pose. A vertex weighted to B's subtree and in front of the plane joins the limb and keeps only limb bones. Every other vertex keeps only body bones. A limb hanging free of the cut goes with B whichever side of the plane it hangs: a bone of B's subtree whose joint and its parent's both lie more than 3 cm in front of the plane, and everything under it (the arms on a waist cut). By position alone, a hand hanging down past the waist stayed on the legs, and her gloves and the suit's cuffs floated where she was cut.
- **Seam.** No vertex is shared across the cut any more. Every vertex of a triangle that crosses the plane goes 100 % to a sink on its own side: a helper bone near the joint that sits where the bind pose puts it relative to its parent, preferably a leaf with few vertices of its own, chosen per mesh section. The section has to contain the sink, because the engine drops weights on bones a section doesn't have. Before each cut `gore.lua` sends `n<component>,<bone>,...`, the bones that may never be a sink: the main skeleton (`Bip001-*`; the pelvis, a clavicle and the head had been picked, and a sink gives up its weights everywhere), hair, capes and breasts (they swing, and the seam swung with them) and accessory bones (`Ab_*`). For a cut through the spine it adds everything under the clavicles and thighs: they swing on their own, and the bind pose's arms hang at the waist, where a strap's seam had collapsed onto a triceps helper. For a limb or head cut it adds every limb but the one being cut: a thigh cut in a fall, her hand resting by the hip, took `Ab-L-Elbow` for the hip's seam, which then rode the flailing arm as a sheet from the hip to the elbow (`ELBOWHIP=1` in the harness puts the elbow on the hip joint and fails if any sink is on another limb). For a limb or head cut it also sends the bones with a physics body (after `fffe`), and the seam then follows the stump: a sink must ride the stump's own body or the one next to it (two bodies away scores ×64), a seam whose sink would ride another body borrows a still helper of the stump's body into its section, and the seam collapses to the middle of the opening, 2 cm inside, instead of the helper's origin (a thigh's seam on a chest helper 44 cm away was drawn up to it as a sheet). Inside means past the rim itself (never on the other side of the plane, and also for a rim further than `SINK_DEEP` in) where the rim lies further in than the plane: the cap sits on the rim, and on coarse limb skin (CH_P_EVE_01 to 05) the rim lies up to 5 cm in from the plane, so a point 2 cm in from the plane stood out past the cap with the seam drawn through it. Bones the watch saw moving stay out, and so do the helpers hanging under one, still as they are against it: `Ab-R-Side` hangs under `Dm-R-Side-Point`, an aim helper the animation points at the arm, and with the shoulder's seam on it, the seam chased the flying arm in an animated death, a sheet from her shoulder to the arm (CH_P_EVE_29; `DRIVEN=1` turns the shoulder's helpers with everything under them, and fails a sink under one). Moved bones stay out except as a last resort for a seam whose only other choice is two bodies away (`HIPMOVED=1` in the harness turns the hip's correctives), or one with no other choice at all: after a long watch had seen all of InnerSuit's helpers move, a waist cut left one side's seam on its old weights, stretched between the halves (`ALLMOVED=1`). A cut through the torso sends no bodies and keeps the sinks where they were. It sends the rig's `Dm-` drivers and every helper under one (before the main skeleton) after `fffd` instead: they count as moved, so a seam takes one only when its section has nothing else, after the helpers the watch saw move, and the last resort borrows one into the section if need be. On CH_P_EVE_30 a waist cut's lower seam had gone to `Ab-L-AX2`, under `Dm-L-AX2` on the pelvis, and that half's opening stretched 3.5 m after the flying upper body; with the hip's helpers moved the harness's seam went to `Dm-Spine-bland` and tore 123 triangles. Now it goes to `Ab-L-Venter` on the pelvis or the spine's AX3 family (`ARMSAFTER=1` cuts both upper arms off the flying torso after the waist, as that death did, and the waist runs fail a new sink under a driver). A split sends them too, and its point and band sinks try every band without a driven helper before one with (`side_sink`). After `fffc` it sends the drivers of the side whose limbs the half lets go (`gore.M.side_drivers`: the limb a driver follows is read off the name of the topmost `Dm-` bone over it, `-L-`/`-R-` with an arm word (Trape, Shoulder, Pectro, ...) or a leg word (Thigh, Hip, Knee, AX2, ...); not a breast's or a cloth's): the animation moves them after those limbs as they fall free, and the half's skin that hung partly on them (`Dm-R-Trape`'s `Ab-R-AX1` and its gear) stretched out after them as cones. The half's kept skin lets go of them as of the limbs (`limb[]`). The harness's split moves them with the dropped limbs and fails a kept vertex that follows (`OLDDRV=1`: not sent).
- **Only the body.** A section that doesn't go round the cut (the same test as the caps below: straps, plates, pads, a cape, a loose sleeve) gets no seam. Each of its connected pieces that crosses the cut goes along whole with the side holding at least 85 % of its weight; one tied to both goes whole with the side holding most of its vertices (a strap over the shoulder weighted 2:1 to the arm but lying on the chest stays on the chest), so nothing of it can stretch. It used to vanish whole into a sink, which read as the cut taking a piece of her outfit. Pieces over 4000 vertices or reaching more than 40 cm from the cut are cut like the body instead, unless at most 15 % of their vertices lie on the other side (CH_P_EVE_09's cape, 9 % of it past an arm cut, had a seam torn across it). A seam through them had collapsed onto whatever bone their section had, often far off or swinging, and stretched across the body. A piece reaching more than 6 cm past the plane on both sides, with over 15 % of its vertices and 3 % of its weight on each, is cut too: sent whole, a waist cut's belt straps stood up off her hips like horns and a harness piece reaching 16 cm down her hips went with the upper body. So is a piece with over 15 % of its weight on each side reaching more than 4.5 cm past the plane both ways, however few of its vertices lie over it, as long as those stay within 12 cm of the plane: CH_P_EVE_29's side panels under her arms (22-30 % of their weight on the upper body, 8-12 % of their vertices over a waist cut) went whole with the legs and stood up 6-8 cm off her hips, stretched and open, while the cape past an arm cut (its few vertices there hang 67 cm down the arm) still goes whole. And so is a piece whose few vertices past the plane reach more than 12 cm down the other side, most of them on that side's bones, unless it has weight on a chain the limb cut keeps on the body (the cape): CH_P_EVE_14's leg laces, 14 % of their vertices 62 cm down a cut thigh, went whole with her body and lost the leg's weights to `Bip001-Spine`, the knee's skin stretched from the severed leg to her. Such a seam takes the sink nearest itself, borrowed into its section's BoneMap when the section has none of that side (CH_P_EVE_09's harness knows only `Bip001-Spine` on the hips). The log shows `pieces=` (how many went whole).
- **Skirts on the thighs.** A thigh cut keeps every skirt chain with no physics body on her (`gore.skirt_chains`: the tops of the `*Skirt*` bones whose parent isn't one, sent in `g` with `0x8000` on the index, `KEEP_RIGID`). CH_P_EVE_37 (Black Rose) hangs its front and side panels (`Ab-*-SkirtD/O/E`) off the thighs' twist bones, animated only, so the leg took them along and the skirt stretched from her hips to it. Unlike a kept cape, their bones stay the limb's (they still follow it): only their skin stays on the body, and gives its weight on the limb up to the stub's carrier, held where it hangs at the cut. The chains on her pelvis and her other leg count too, for their skin partly on the cut thigh.
- **Collapse.** The sink's inverse bind matrix becomes a zero scale at its own origin, so the seam collapses onto the sink's live position whatever drives it (animation or physics). A stump's seam (a section that goes round the cut; a cape or a strap that crosses somewhere else keeps the sink it was given) is then held on the bone its rim rides, `bb` / `lb`: the weights stay on the helper, since collapsing the rim's bone would take the whole limb or the torso, and every frame while a piece is off (`H` from her tick, no result file — that file is what made a per-frame command stutter) the collapse point is rewritten so it stays at the opening in the rim bone's frame. That point is `SINK_IN` (2 cm) in from the middle of that side's rim, all its sections (a piece that doesn't go round the plane point: its own section's middle, on the plane when within `SINK_DEEP` of it). It used to be 2 cm in from the plane at the bone, but a thigh's rim lies up to 3.4 cm in from the plane and 6 cm off the bone, so the point sat in front of the cap and drew half the suit out over it. The harness fails a cut whose collapsed seam reaches within 1 cm of the body cap's centre (`bc=`). A thigh twist, the spine's `Ab-AX3T` or a knee driver keeps being animated after the cut, and the seam point used to swing off the opening as a cone, the cut face's black cavity. The harness shoves a leg stump's sink 30 cm and twists it, and fails if the seam moves over 1 cm; it then shoves the rim's bone and fails if the seam doesn't follow. Triangles across the cut become lines, and the ring next to them closes the opening. The sink's own vertices move to their strongest other bone first. Weight that leaves any bone (a sink, a helper the cut drops) goes to that bone's nearest ancestor that stays, else to the strongest other bone: a helper without a body rides its parent at a fixed offset, so the parent skins the vertex where the helper did. Moved to the strongest bone, a split's shoulder skin went onto the chest and tore off the clavicle by up to 23 cm once she moved. On a limb or waist cut, a body-side vertex whose bones all go with no kept ancestor in its section's BoneMap gets that ancestor borrowed in (`stuck=` counts what still has nowhere to go): left as it was, CH_P_EVE_14's jacket hem under a waist cut flew off with the upper body. `HideBone` can't do this: a hidden bone renders at its parent's bind origin and stops updating, which stretched the severed limb back to the cut.
- **Apply and report.** The weights go to the engine's own `USkinnedMeshComponent::SetSkinWeightOverride`. The DLL reports both openings, fitted to the rim vertices in their new pose: centre, axes and an ellipse in the cut plane. The cap belongs on the limb, not on what is strapped to it, so rim points are grouped by mesh section first. A section whose points go round the opening (11 of 16 directions) is a layer of the limb. The innermost layer and the ones sitting tight on it (skin, a body suit) get the cap; straps, plates, capes and loose sleeves are left out and close up in their own material. A section filling a hole of 3 directions or more in that core is the same skin split in two (InnerSuit's limbs and waist are two sections, one each side): it joins when its radius is within 1.1× the core's plus 0.5 cm, or within 2.5× when its rim runs on from the core's (a point within 1.5 cm of one of the core's), since on a leaned cut's oval the section at the long ends lies further out however well centred. Directions and radii are measured round the opening's own centre (`core_centre`), not the cut's point on the bone: the middle of the box the layers span in the plane, found again from there until it settles (from the median of all the points when nothing goes round from the bone). From the bone, an opening it sat off the middle of read its near section as the inner layer and the far one as an outer one: on the skin suit the cap covered the near half alone (a 3 × 0.7 cm cap on her waist, round a spine bone near her back), the other half's seam showing round it as skin-coloured wedges. The hanging pieces' test (Only the body, above) measures round the same centre. With no layer at all, every point counts. Of what is left, points further than 1.6× the median from that centre are dropped too. The log line shows the grouping (`bsec=section/points/directions/radius`, `bcore=` the kept sections, `l...` for the limb side). `bb=` and `lb=` name the bone each side's rim rides (the most weighted one over its rim vertices, sinks left out): the caps, stubs and bleeding ride it, not the cut bone's parent, so they stay on the stump's skin (a thigh's parent is `Bip001-Spine`, while its stump hangs on the pelvis helpers, and the stub slid to the edge of the stump as she moved). On the piece's side a helper under one of the rig's drivers (a nocarry bone with no body of its own, between it and the cut bone) hands its weight to the bone above the topmost such driver: the animation keeps pointing the drivers at her body, and the left arm's cap and bone rode `Ab-L-Deltoid` (under `Dm-L-Deltoid-A`) straight down off the flying arm. Only on her, with her bodies sent (an enemy's every bone is nocarry).
- **Open.** The sinks are recorded but collapse only on `k`, a few frames later. Collapsing them under the old weights would pull every vertex those helpers moved onto their origins until the GPU has the new weights.

The command is `c<component>,<lod>,<bone>[,<mode>,<radius mm>[,<sink>,<remap>]]`. Besides the plain cut (mode 0) there are three modes (3, 5 and 6 were the old wounds: a hole, a dent and a rupture, removed with them; a hit she lives through now leaves a mark, see Hits she lives through). `m<component>,<lod>,<reach cm>` asks where a ray from LocX/Y/Z along DirX/Y/Z meets the skin, changing nothing (`hit=`, `n=`, `bone=`, `out=`):

- **Whole (1).** Every vertex follows B's subtree; no seam. Eve's face is its own mesh, weighted to the head and the neck, so a beheading used to move all of it onto the head (cut in Plane mode like the neck, 8300 of its vertices vanished into the stump and the head came off faceless). Whole, the head took the face's whole neck along, down to her collar, so it came off with a long neck. Now the face is cut like a limb (`tune.gore.faceCut`), at the head bone (`headAt` 1.0 of the way from the neck bone; it was 0.7). Its eyes, teeth and brows know only the head bone, so the head side's seam closes on `Bip001-Spine1`, a bone no face vertex uses, made to follow her head from the collapse on (the `<sink>,<remap>` fields: a borrowed sink on MODE_CUT's limb side, on MODE_SIDE's vanishing side). The face's neck stays on her, and her stump's cap goes on the face's rim, unless the body's goes all round her neck and is wider (a high collar). Most bodies end under the plane or just past it (CH_P_EVE_30's neck at 0.88, CH_P_EVE_29's collar round the back only). Her chin's underside is only 1-3 mm above a plane at the head bone, so a nod or a leaning blow ran the plane through it and left a slice of chin on the neck. `gore.lua`'s `clear_jaw` moves the plane down its normal until 22 points of her jaw and skull base (`JAW`, in the head bone's space: the lowest vertices only the head moves for any plane within 60 degrees of the neck) are `jawClear` (0.5 cm) above it; past `jawDrop` (3 cm) it leans the plane back toward the head's own axis first, along which 0.5 cm is enough. On the real face mesh in 768 poses (nod -30 to 40 degrees, roll 20, the plane leaned up to 35 degrees every way) no vertex only the head moves ends under it. The harness cuts the real face mesh (`HEAD=1 FACESINK=1`) at 0.5 (the lowest the clearance takes it), 0.7, 1.0 and 1.2 and flings the head off: nothing stretches, nothing of the head stays behind. Past about 1.2 the plane runs through her jaw.
- **Plane (2).** The limb side is what is in front of the plane and within the radius of its axis, whatever the weights. The head's subtree is the head alone (the clavicles hang off the neck) and the outfit skin has no head bone near the neck, so the neck is cut by position, mid-neck.
- **Side (4).** Half of a split, at any angle: what is in front of the plane stays, what is behind it goes. Kept skin on a bone the half gives up (a sink, a let-go limb's driver) goes to that bone's nearest kept ancestor, borrowed into the section's BoneMap when it isn't in it; before, a section with none of them in its map put it on the section's first kept bone by index, and CH_P_EVE_01's outer suit (14,659 vertices on 11 shoulder and hip helpers, no main bone) put 4,103 hip vertices on the dropped side's `Ab-L-AX1T-BKWING`, which then followed the flying arm. A let-go limb's drivers stay let go when the kept head's skin hangs on them too (that suit's collar is on the shoulders' AX1T helpers). It isn't collapsed into points: the first version did that with one sink per section, so the whole other half, legs included, went into one spot near the chest and every triangle along the midline stretched from the hips or the neck to it. Now the torso just behind the plane (5 cm) is flattened onto it, and what lies deeper collapses to a point on the plane in its band (flattened, a slice across the waist would spread the shoulders out past the opening). Its sinks get a projection instead of a zero scale: inverse bind matrix, then onto the plane (2 cm inside the kept side, behind the lids; at 0.3 cm the sheet came out in front of the lid wherever she bent) in the sink bone's frame as posed at the cut. Each vertex lands where it was, only pushed onto the plane, so the midline closes without anything reaching across. The sheet is banded the way the lid is cut into pieces: each vanishing vertex goes to the band of the spine bone it lands nearest to (Lua's `SPLIT_RIDE`, sent after the limbs; each half sends only the spine bones it keeps, `SPLIT_RIDE.of`: the copy's head breaks free in its ragdoll and spun at up to 8000 deg/s, and the lid and sheet that rode it rose from the body in tall dark sheets), and a band's sinks must ride that bone (the first main bone up their chain; under a clavicle or a breast they swing on their own). A bone no free helper rides (CH_P_EVE_09's Spine and Spine1) gets no band. A flattened vertex is weighted between the two bands of its section it lies between, by where it sits along the spine, so the sheet bends like skin. With four bands along the opening and whatever sink was nearest, a pelvis helper carried 43 cm of the sheet up to the ribs, clavicle helpers swung with the arms, and once she bent the sheet tore between bands and came out through the lid (the playtest's stretch probe: thousands of stretched triangles, all on the split's own sinks). A limb never splits lengthwise: Lua sends `g<component>,<gone roots>,0,<kept roots>,0,<ride bones>[,0,<dropped chains>]` (each arm and thigh by the joint it hangs from, shoulder or hip: by the middle of its first segment, an arm hanging past a slash across the stomach went with the legs and was cut off at the shoulder), the kept half's limbs stay whole wherever they lie, and the other half's limbs collapse to a point on the plane where they joined. Accessory chains off the torso or neck (the top of each chain of 2+ physics bodies outside `Bip001`, not under a limb or the head) go the same way, each to the side where its bodies lie on average: kept whole with the kept roots, or dropped whole (each vertex mostly on it collapsed to a point in its band). Halved, the neck chain's swinging half stretched to the flattened sheet across the opening. Kept skin lets go of every helper past the plane and on it (within 1 cm), so the other half's sinks never pull on it, and the halves no longer need re-cutting. Both halves are components of one mesh asset, so their sinks are shared: a vertex the vanilla skin puts wholly on a helper the other half has just made its sink is reweighted like any other (it used to count as an earlier cut's seam vertex and was flattened with the other half: CH_P_EVE_09's neck, on `Ab-NeckSub`). A section with no helper to flatten onto (CH_P_EVE_09's neck lace knows only the neck and the spine) borrows the half's nearest one into its BoneMap, like the face below. The opening runs head to crotch and isn't convex. The DLL traces it as one outline (`s0p=`, up to 200 points): the rim points and the mesh's own cross-section with the plane are drawn on a 0.8 cm grid, the outside is flooded from a corner, the inside filled and its largest piece's border chained; a fill much smaller than its outline's area means the outside leaked in through a gap, and the gap closing is doubled (1.5 cm up to 12). It is also doubled while the outline is under 40 % of the ellipse the rim's points spread over: through gaps wider than the closing the outside flooded round most of the torso on CH_P_EVE_06, 19, 28, 43 and 59, and the scrap left (180-380 cm² of a 1000 cm² opening) was taken as the opening. With none big enough, the biggest outline found is sent. Bands along it only pick the sinks now. `h<component>,<mode>,<head bone>` (point and normal: the neck plane) goes before a half's split cut: Eve's hair (`CH_P_EVE_Hair02`: a root and its hair chains, no body bone) can't be halved, so the head never is. Lua puts the neck plane at the neck's base. Mode 1 keeps everything above it whole within `NECK_BASE_R` (7 cm) of the neck axis up to halfway to the head bone, and `NECK_R` (10 cm, her collar) above, and closes the strip under it on the far side into the neck (halfway up the neck, a split leaned 2 cm beside it took the far side's lower neck; 10 cm wide at the base, it kept the far trapezius, which stretched to where the dropped arm collapsed); mode 2 lets the head go into a sink at the neck and sends a second opening, `n` (`np=`). Its kept skin keeps nothing on the head's subtree: that weight goes to the head's parent, the neck, as a gone limb's does (557 of CH_P_EVE_10's neck vertices hung partly on the head, Neck 204 / Head 51, and stretched after it). No sink may be a bone the bone watch saw move while she played (an animation-driven corrective such as the `Ab-AX3` family would carry the flattened sheet with it), nor one the head's or the neck's skin hangs on (sinks are the mesh asset's, so the other half's head would let go of it), and such a bone is never one the kept skin drops, wherever it lies (`Ab-NeckSub` swings past the plane in a pose). The answer adds `proj=` and `point=`. `<sink>` forces a sink bone for sections that have nothing to flatten onto: Eve's eyes, teeth and brows only know the head bone, so the face borrows `Bip001-Spine1`, which no face vertex uses. The DLL adds it to the section's BoneMap in place when the array has room (an engine array always has room for 8 entries); a fuller one is swapped for a copy with room for 512, put back on undo. Borrowing only in place had left a waist split's copy with 10617 vertices of the upper body standing on the legs, its sections full and, on `k`, points the component's `MasterBoneMap` entry for it at the master's bone `<remap>` (the head), so the face's vanished side flattens in the head's frame. An F11 / F5 / odds split leans its plane from the crotch to `tune.gore.splitBeside` (2) cm beside her neck, so the head stays whole on her half and the cut runs between her breasts (blade splits follow the blade). Lua sorts a split's arms by the shoulder joint (`SPLIT_LIMBS`), not the clavicle by the neck. A dropped limb's point rides the spine bone nearest where it joined, else any spine bone (never flattened onto the plane: a flat hand).

`p<src>,<dst>` copies one component's pose onto another on the same skeleton, for the split's copy.

`y<src>,<lod>,<dst>,...` puts src's weights (its override as the GPU skins it, else the mesh's) on other components of the same mesh asset: another mod's see-through copy of her mesh, and a split's copy of a body cut before. Each destination becomes a co-owner (`also`) of src's sinks, so its own cuts leave the seams on them: cut from the mesh's own weights, the copy of a body whose arm was off put the stump's seam back on the upper arm and clavicle, stretched out to the flung arm. A destination whose MasterBoneMap entry matches one src borrowed (the face's seam bone, made to follow her body's head) gets the same entry, applied with src's on `k` and given back when its weights are cleared: Ultra Violence's face blood copy follows her body, the face's own master, and its neck-to-head seam stayed on the borrowed bone's own transform. `gore.copies_find` takes copies on the cut mesh's pose or on its master's. `S<comp>,<lod>` saves a component's weights (its override, or none) and the sinks and borrowed BoneMap entries there are; `R<comp>,<lod>[,<other>...]` puts them back after a split that failed on a body cut before: the weights as saved (or the override released), the sinks and BoneMap entries made since for it and the others named (the copy) dropped, a collapsed one's matrix put back first, and the co-owners as they were (`ok back, dropped N`). `x` there would have taken the arm cut with the split (the sinks are the asset's: an `x` on the copy restores them all). `S<comp>,<lod>,1` forgets the save once the split held; `r` forgets them all. The harness (`ARMSPLIT=L|R`, in the `split` group) cuts that upper arm (`ARMAT` along it, 0.4), flings it, saves her, copies her weights to the copy, splits beside the neck and checks both halves as usual (the arm cut's sunk vertices are the baseline), then collapses, restores and checks her weights, her sinks, the co-owners and every other inverse bind are as before the split.

`s<component>,<lod>` is the stretch probe: it skins every vertex exactly as the GPU does (the component's pose, its overrides) and answers `ok stretched=<n> of <triangles> hidden=<n> longest=<cm> bones=<bone>:<count>:<cm>;... shown=<bone>:<count>;...`, the triangles with an edge more than 3x and 15 cm longer than at bind (`hidden`: those wholly on sinks with under 1 cm² left; a split's flattening sinks only squash the skin onto the plane, so a sheet stretched between two bands still shows) and the bones they hang on most (`shown`: counting only the triangles that show). `gore.lua` logs it with bone names (`split stretch, her half / copy` at 0.5 and 2 s after a split; with `probes`, `cut stretch, <bone>` at 1 and 5 s after a limb cut, and for a beheading the face's, `cut stretch, Bip001-Head (face)`). A component on a master pose is skinned through its own `MasterBoneMap`, so the face's borrowed `Spine1`, remapped onto her head, is skinned where the head is. With a third field, `s<component>,<lod>,<n>`, it also writes the skin as posed to `probe<n>.obj` next to the log (component space, cm, `vb <bone>` after each vertex: its strongest bone): with `probes` on, a split's halves at 2 s (`probe1` her half, `probe2` the copy, whose attached caps and stubs are logged with where they are and their scale) and a cut at 5 s (`probe3`), to see what a half shows in game.

In every mode, a limb-side vertex in a section that can't follow the limb, and every limb-side vertex of a section that has seam vertices on that side but no limb sink, vanishes: it joins the body side's seam and collapses with it, which beats a triangle stretched from the flying piece to the body. The log shows `vanished=`.

`x<component>,<lod>` releases the override, restores that mesh's matrices and the component's borrowed `MasterBoneMap` entries. `r` restores every collapsed matrix and borrowed BoneMap entry, since they live on the shared mesh asset. Those records (the sinks, the grip's eased bones, a regrow's matrices, the borrowed BoneMap entries) name the asset by its address, which a level reload may free: each asset recorded is noted with its `GUObjectArray` index the first time a command sees it (the frame after its cut, worn), and every command first drops the records of a noted asset no longer alive (`assets_prune`, the liveness check of `G`), writing nothing; `r`'s answer then ends `gone=N`. Before, the next `r` after a reload wrote into the old level's freed assets. An asset not noted (the object array unreadable, as in the harness) is written back as before. A BoneMap that had no room for a borrowed bone gets a bigger array from the engine's own allocator (`GMalloc`), so the engine can free it when the mesh goes; one from `VirtualAlloc` corrupted its heap and the game crashed on quit (Alt+F4: `MallocBinned2 Corruption Canary`). A swapped array the restore puts back is freed through `GMalloc` too. A borrowed bone also joins the LOD's `ActiveBoneIndices`: the GPU skin builds a matrix each frame only for those (the sections' own BoneMaps and their ancestors), so a helper with no skin of its own (`Dm-R-Thigh-point`, `Ab-AX3` on CH_P_EVE_10) skinned whatever its sink or stub vertices with leftover matrix data, and they flew across the screen somewhere new every frame. The array isn't in the exe's symbols, so the DLL finds it in the LOD's render data by its contents, once (`active=<offset>/<bones added>` in a cut's result; `0/0` = not found, and then no bone outside it is ever borrowed). The harness keeps the same array and fails a cut that leaves any vertex on a bone outside it (`NOACTIVE=1` hides it from the DLL).

`ue4ss/StellarBladeGore/Scripts/gore.lua` drives each cut:

1. Every killing blow cuts, whatever hit her (a Gorilla's fist cuts like a blade). A killing hit the game reported (see the split below), or an enemy's weapon swing seen at her death, is decided by its blade alone (`gore.blow`); otherwise it rolls what the blow does (`tune.gore.death`: a limb, the head, the torso, or a split when the blow came down from above; the split is described below). What comes off flies the way the blow went (from the attacker through her, else the swing, else her death push): a piece at `deathThrow` (280 cm/s, no `legThrow` on a leg), a split's cut off half at `splitFly` (350), every body of it (`piece flies with the blow` in the log). It picks the part nearest the hit and forces LOD0 while she is cut (the face too, for a head).
2. Two ticks later it cuts at `tune.gore.cutAt` along the bone, and the next tick places the caps hidden (in the cut's own frame they made it stutter longer). The openings are where the cut found them, so each cap and stub is first moved along with the bone it rides since the cut (its world transform noted at the cut): in a fall the arm had moved on by the next tick and the stump's cap hung well off its end. The bone lists the cut sends (no sinks, no carriers, the other limbs) come from her outfit's bone tree, read 40 bones at a time while she plays. Detaching a kept chain, and the kept/gone bone indices of a cut, resolve off that cached tree instead of one engine query per bone (19 ms of Lua on a limb cut's chains stage, November 4 13:17-13:18 session), falling back to the engine where the tree isn't built; the assembled nosink/nocarry/bodies/drivers list of a cut's `n` command is cached per mesh asset and cut shape. Command content is unchanged. `sim_cutlist` checks the tree result against the engine loop with zero engine calls on a warm tree. On a death the plane leans until it contains the killing blow's direction (the death key's impulse: attacker to Eve, plus some of the hit direction; the game exposes no blade swing), at most `blowTilt` degrees off square; then a small random tilt. Accessory chains that hang off the limb's root bone stay on the body: a chain of at least 3 bodies of its own (not `Bip001`), anchored on that bone, whose top joint lies over 3 cm off the bone (twist bones lie on it). CH_P_EVE_09's cape hangs off the upper arm's twist bone half way down the arm, and part of it went off with the arm. A twist bone (`-Tw<n>`) is never a chain top, whatever the pose. A chain whose bodies all lie along the limb (none past its end), reaching no further from its top than the limb is long and not named `*Cape*`, is a strap on it, not a cape, and follows the plane whole (the reach and the name don't depend on her pose: with her arm out or up CH_P_EVE_09's 70 cm cape hung across the arm, within its length, and was taken for a strap; `test/sim_cape.lua`): kept on the body and hung from the body above (as `chains = "body"` below) when its top is behind the plane (`cut <bone>: <chain>, a strap over the plane, stays on her` in the log), else not named and off with the limb. CH_P_EVE_14's leg straps hang off the hip 5 to 36 % down the thigh; cut off as pieces of their own they flew 2 m off and their skin tore off the leg. `gore.lua` names them to the DLL (`g<comp>,0,<chain roots>` before the cut; sent empty otherwise), which leaves every vertex mostly on them on the body, on their own bones. Then (`chains = "detach"`, the default) each chain is cut off as a piece of its own: a second cut on the chain's top bone, the plane through the joint it hangs from and facing down the chain, so all of it goes; for that cut the chain's own bones may be sinks (its seam is its own top) and the cut limb's may not (the limb is gone: an earlier cut's biceps sink had pulled the cape's seam off with the arm). When the limb comes off, the chain's joint to it breaks and it falls by itself (`<chain> cut off on its own` in the `off` log line, `cut <chain> off <bone>: ...` for its cut). Kept on the body, the arm's 80 cm cape hung straight down from the shoulder with no arm under it. With `chains = "body"` each chain instead lets go of the limb and hangs from the nearest body above it (the clavicle, the pelvis) on a `PhysicsConstraintComponent` ball joint where the chain starts (40° swing, 20° twist), destroyed on recovery (`<chain> kept on <bone>`). `test/real/run.sh` with `ARM=1` cuts the left upper arm of the real mesh and the cape off it, then flings both apart: every cape vertex must end up on the cape's own bones, no forearm vertex may stay, and no stretched triangle may show (`KEEPCAPE=1` keeps it on the body, `WITH=1` sends it whole with the arm, whose shoulder seam shows, `NOKEEP=1` the old split cape; `CAPE=<root,...>` names other chains, CH_P_EVE_14's leg straps `Ab_LLA01,Ab_LLB01` with a thigh cut, and `DBGFORE=1` prints the limb's vertices left on the body). `WAIST=1` cuts across the waist as F9 does: no arm vertex may stay on the legs, no outfit vertex mostly on the upper body may stay 6 cm over the cut (the straps' horns) nor one mostly on the hips go 6 cm under it with the upper body, and with the upper body flung off and its arms swung no stretched triangle may show. A limb bone with no body of its own (CH_P_EVE_21 has no thigh bodies: its calves are jointed straight to `Bip001-Spine`) has no joint to break, so the joints from the limb's top bodies to the body outside it break instead (`no body on <bone>: broke ...` in the `off` line). While a piece is off a living Eve her capsule ignores physics bodies and her mesh ignores pawns: the upper body of a waist cut landed inside the capsule and jiggled as it was shoved every frame. Her hair (`SBHair`, which blocks PhysicsBody, her mesh's type with the ragdoll's profile) ignores PhysicsBody meanwhile too. The piece's settling (active.lua's run with `piece`) re-masses, watches and puts to sleep only the piece's own bodies (`own`: the bodies under a severed bone): her mesh simulates more than the piece alive, her ponytail on outfits that hang it off her head as bodies (CH_P_EVE_30's `Ab-TL-HairB01-09`), and put to sleep with it, the ponytail hung where it was as she moved on and stretched into the air in photo mode's poses till her death. A stub on a limb's body stump stays at the opening's centre (only cuts through the spine or neck move it towards her back; a thigh's parent is `Bip001-Spine`). Her back is read off the spine bone as noted at the cut, the frame the opening is in: read live when the stubs were placed, a flying head had turned up to 120 degrees on, and its stub sat off to the side by the ear. The skin kept between the joint and the cut can't stay on the cut bone (it leaves with the limb), and put on the parent it snapped back towards the bind pose: an upper arm hanging by her side left a stump swung out with its opening a sliver 4 cm off the cut, so she looked hollow there and the bone stub sat in the sliver. That skin now rides a carrier: a spare helper on the cut bone's parent (`Ab-L-Shoulder1` on the clavicle for an arm; only on the parent itself, else none and the stub stays on the parent as before: a calf's stub carried on the spine stayed behind as the thigh swung), borrowed into the sections that lack it, whose inverse bind matrix becomes the cut bone's as posed at the cut, seen from the helper. It must hold still against its parent at the cut (within 3 cm and 6° of its bind pose) and have held still all along while she played: gore.lua sends `w<comp>` every 5 ticks while she is alive, the DLL keeps each bone's furthest slide and turn off its bind offset, and a bone looked at fewer than 30 times or ever off by more is not a carrier while one that held still is (the cut's result says `watched=<looks>`). With none, the one that moved least is taken if it stayed within 8 cm and 15° (`carrier=<bone>~`): over a long session the watch saw every helper on her spine move a little, the stub stayed on the parent as the bind pose has it, and a standing thigh's skin coned down past the cap (CH_P_EVE_20_TypeB; the harness's `POSED=1 LOOSEMOVED=4`). With none at all the result names the nearest miss, `nocarrier=<bone>:<cm>:<half-turn cos>`. The thigh's parent is the spine on her rig, and the spine's `Ab-AX3`, still at the cut, is a corrective the animation drives: it flung a thigh's stub (5365 vertices) all over the screen as the leg flew. It may not have a physics body of its own (gore.lua reads them off her physics asset): a calf's stub went on `Ab-L-Hip`, the hip's jiggle body, which swings on its own constraint. It may not be one of the rig's `Dm-` drivers (gore.lua sends those after `ffff` in the `n` command): `Dm-Spine-bland` blends Spine and Spine1, and on a waist cut it dragged the hips' stub after the flying upper body. `WAIST=1` drives it half way after the upper body and fails if any of the legs' skin moves; `ARM=1` turns the parent's subtree 30° and fails if any carried vertex stays behind. Enemies get no carriers (gore.lua names all their bones): their mesh asset is shared with the live enemies of their kind. The stump stays exactly as it was and moves with the parent (`carried=<vertices> carrier=<bone>` in the cut's result). `ARM=1` also checks the body's opening, posed with the cut's weights after the collapse, still goes round the arm (at least 12 of 16 directions; it was 3 to 4); `ARMBONE`, `ARMEND` and `ARMAT` cut another limb bone or elsewhere along it, `DBGARM=1` prints the opening's coverage and the skin each section keeps.
   A head cuts the face whole first, then the neck by position 0.7 of the way from the neck joint to the head (4.1 cm over the joint; 0.5 sat by the shoulders, and before that it was 1.8 cm over and 2.9 cm under the joint); the face reaches down to 1.2 cm under the head's joint and goes with the head, whose cap sits on the face's neck.
3. Two ticks after that it sends `k`, breaks the ragdoll constraint at that bone, so the limb's bodies carry it away (on a living Eve too, for F9), and gives every body of the piece a push (`throw` away from the body; `deathThrow` along a killing blow), climbing at most `throwUp` (0.25, about 14 degrees). Its blood splat and sound go out the next tick, its bleeds the one after, each out of the opening's frame. A killing blow's cut is chosen the frame after the death's own setup.
4. It shows the caps. Each is a thin domed lid built as a procedural mesh on the opening's outline (the convex hull of the trimmed rim points, which the DLL sends in order), so its edge sits on the skin. If the procedural mesh can't be made, a flattened sphere fitted to the ellipse is used instead. With `capMatte` on (off by default: the stumps looked too rough) they use the meat's own textures (`T_A_Meat_01_A`/`_N`/`_ORM`) on the game's character gore master `MA_CH_Standard_Gore`, its `Roughness Min`/`Max` held at 0.75/1, `Metallic` 0 and `OpacityMask Intensity` 1 (the master dithers by it); a split's lids stay on the meat material (`capMatte.splitLids` false: on the master they didn't look solid): the meat material's shine is baked into its ORM texture (it has no roughness parameter) and the stumps looked wet and mirrored the room. The textures are read back; when the colour one doesn't take, and with `capMatte` off (the default), both use the game's meat material (`MI_A_Meat_01_1`, `capMaterial`), each through its own instance whose emissive `BloodColor` is turned down to a faint dark red (`capBloodColor`; the effect material's own 10, 2.4, 0 glowed bright orange), and are attached to the bone of that side. The game has no human cross-section; `MI_CutDamage`, used before, is a monster dissolve material and looked like a broken glossy texture on a lid. The stumps' bone and tubes are Tachy's, on 0.3.0's meat lids (`tachyLids` false, the default: on the procedural lids her flesh read pale and washed out whatever its parameters, playtest; with it on, the caps take her flesh disc as below, and with `tachy_core.lua` unreadable there is no bone; the game's human `GM_Brokenbone` is gone from the stumps, kept only for a split's `splitSpine` pieces, off): the game made one Eve-model body cut open, her arm on the beach after the Brute (`CH_NPC_TachyNPC_ArmDamaged_Body`, a `damage` section on `MI_CH_NPC_TachyNPC_Destroyed`). Its texture holds a flesh disc ringed with skin and the cross-sections of a metal bone in a gold socket and torn tubes. Each cap takes its own instance of that material (`tachyFlesh`: its flesh is near black red, sRGB 35, 0, 0, and near mirror smooth, and in game the reflections turned it grey, so her own values: her master's defaults (`Brightness` 1, `Base Color Tint` white, `Metallic` 1 times her texture's, `AOMultiply` 1, `AO Tint` white, `Desaturation` and `Contrast` 0, read out of `MA_CH_Standard_Gore`'s cached parameters) and her instance's (no glow), with `Roughness Min` 0.9 for her 0.3 (her texture's own roughness is near mirror smooth), `AOMultiply` 0.5, and a crimson glow (`EmissivePower` 8, `EmissiveColor` 1, 0.02, 0.05 times her texture's red, through `Emissive_CH`'s red mask): at any `Brightness` from 1 to 10 the flesh stayed pale in game, the room's light off it over its dark red, and the glow is its own colour whatever the light (at `Brightness` 10 its red blew out to salmon pink) (a blue-leaning tint and 0.55 left a cut-off head's cap washed grey), and `AOMultiply` with `AO Tint` 0.3, 0.03, 0.02 hold back the room's light and reflections, which still turned flesh and bone grey in the dark (`tachyFlesh.ao`, `.aoTint`; the core keeps her material as it is: its metal reads as metal only by the room's reflections, and dulled with the flesh it looked like pale dirt), and the flesh glows its own colour (`tachyFlesh.emissive`: its colour texture set as `Emissive` and `Emissive_CH`, `EmissiveColor`, `EmissivePower` 8; the F3 menu's Stumps tab sets these on every stump already out, `M.reflesh` over the caps' components, each kept with its actor's address and touched only while that address is still one of the cuts' caps or a pool lid (by address: UE4SS hands back a new Lua object for the same actor on every call, so a comparison of the objects never matched), its material 0 if it is still an instance of hers (kept by path, the instances and then the components were never found again); all read back in the log), as lit only by the room it stayed grey-blue and is built by `tachy_lid`: fifteen rings from the rim in to the centre, 72 points round each (the outline's, with more put between them), at `tachyCapScale` (1.0) of the outline (at 1.05 it ran over the cut's edge; outline points over max(2 cm, 0.35 of its median radius) off the plane are left out first, with `tachyLids` only, `M.flat_outline` (on the meat lids it left out up to half an arm's rim): a harness strap counted as going round an arm drew the cap down it as a kite), each point mapped onto the disc by its angle and its share of the way in (any outline fills the disc; the painted skin ring is left out, the cap's edge sits on her own skin) and raised by the dome (`tachyDome`, 0.2 of the old one: it read as a cover, not a cut, and flat the cut's closed skin stood through it), a swell just inside the skin and the flesh's relief (`relief` in `tachy_core.lua`: the texture's brightness less its broad shading on a 48 x 48 grid, dark creases down and fibre bundles up, with two octaves of smooth value noise on the disc for finer tears (`M.tachy_noise`, the same on both faces of a cut), up to 0.8 cm high at `tachyRelief` 1 (`r` x 0.12; deeper read too lumpy) and its creases at most a fifth of that down (creases under the cut sank where the limb's closed skin hid the flesh), faded in over the outer tenth, under a core at the centre, and within 1.2-2.7 cm of where a spine bone stands), its normals from the faces; the stub is her core (`tachy_core.lua`: the section's 255 core vertices, read off the mesh with CUE4Parse, in her disc's radii) built as a procedural mesh on a pool lid, scaled to the opening's mean radius (at most `tachyCoreMax`, 6 cm; a leg's `tachyCoreLeg`, 1.4, times that) and standing on the cap's dome. Both openings of a cut share one frame (`M.pair`: the body side's x and y): the cut piece's lid and core are the stump's mirror image (the core mirrored, its triangles turned round), so the bone and tubes on the arm line up with those on the shoulder; each opening had its own frame before and the two faces didn't match. The frame's x is her back on the cut's plane (`back_on`, from the spine bone as posed at the cut), not the DLL's rim axis, which lies any way round per cut and stood the bone at a random place on each stump; on her right's limbs y goes the other way (`M.side_of`), so her two thighs' or arms' cores mirror across her midline (`test/sim_frame.lua`). Her bone is painted ivory on her texture (metal mask 0.01-0.04) and read as pale dirt; with `boneSilver` (off by default: in game it read crimson, playtest) its end takes a tube end's tile (u 0.02-0.17, v 0.40-0.56) and its shaft the metal strip along the bottom (u 0.05-0.70, v 0.82-0.93, mask 0.95), each scaled, not turned, so her tangents stay right (`M.bone_uv`). The core is her material on its own instance (`tachyCoreLook`: `Brightness` 0.8, `Desaturation` 0.7, `Base Color Tint` 1, 0.8, 0.78, a dark grey near the flesh; hers read near black in the dark, `Brightness` 1.8 too light). A waist's spine bone is `spineBoneWaist` (1.6) times wider and no taller. Every core is sheared upright (her bone leans 14 degrees off her disc's normal, `lx`/`ly` from its shaft's lowest and highest quarters; it looked bent) and stands along the cut bone (`o.axis`: the part's joint to its `to` joint, out of each opening; within 50 degrees of the cut's normal, its foot still on the lid), and a leg's leaves out her gold socket (the two flat pieces low on her disc). A spine or neck cut (neck, waist) gets her metal bone alone (`M.tachy_bone`: the core's largest piece, its shaft, and the pieces whose middles lie within half its reach, its end; the tubes and the flat disc by them are left out), its shaft `stubBone` (0.3) of the opening's narrow width and at most `spineBoneMax` (3.5 cm) thick, `spineBoneOut` (1.2) times its own height out of the flesh, where the spine runs (`SPINE_BACK` 0.55 of the way to her back at the waist, `NECK_BACK` 0.5 at the neck; across her, midway between the outline's sides), its cap mapped onto a plain patch of the disc (`TACHY_PATCH`, as a split's caps: the core's gold socket and tube holes are painted on the disc and showed through without the core over them): the arm's bone and tubes there read as mushrooms (playtest). A split's lid pieces stay on the meat (their UVs tile). On the spine, neck, head or pelvis the stub sits towards her back (`SPINE_BACK`, 0.55 of the way from the opening's centre to its rim), where the spine is, not in the middle of the opening. Her back is the cut spine bone's own -Z (every Bip001 spine bone's, within 15 degrees); it was worked out from her hips and shoulders, which after earlier limb cuts lay wherever those limbs fell. Every cut (F9, F11 too) also raises her skin's own blood layer: Eve's skin master (`MA_Skin_EVE`, `MF_SkinBloodFX`) reads `BloodFactor` and `BloodOffset` from the game-wide `MPC_PCDirtyOffset`, so `bodyBlood` ramps them over 1.5 s and puts them back on recovery, or on the respawned Eve's first tick (the collection outlives her: the respawn kept the blood on her) (`body blood:` lines show the values before and after). It covers all of her, not just around the cut. Her bleed and hit blood (`MA_A_DefaultBlood_01`, `MA_A_GpuBlood_01`, the effects' colour curves are white) take their colour from the game-wide `MPC_BloodParameters` (`DefaultBlood` 0.15, 0, 0, `DefaultBlood2` 0.23, 0.05, 0, `EmissiveBloodMinValue` 0.15, read out of the asset), and sprayed bright red; `bloodTint` sets those to a dark crimson before every bleed or hit blood is spawned (each level has its own copy of the collection), the game's own blood on her with them (`blood tint: ... was ...` logs the values found, once). While she plays, the cuts' assets are loaded ahead and kept, one a look (effects, decals, materials, then sounds after 30 s; early sound loading was suspected in an audio mixer crash at exe+0xb4b8817, but the same crash recurred before this mod started its tick, so its cause remains unconfirmed). A run of slow frames (4 within 1.5 s) while the mod's own tick stays short logs `fps dip:` with what the mod has out (caps, pool lids, floor decals, bleeds, effects, cuts), at most every 10 s. **Floor blood**: every bleeding opening of a cut (F9 and F11 too) is followed for `poolTime` (12 s). While it moves (she falls, the piece flies) it drips small splats every `dripEvery` (18) cm; where it comes to rest a pool spreads under it, fast at first and slowing (`poolGrow`, 5 s to 63 %), as large as that part bleeds (a neck or thigh about 55-60 cm radius, a forearm 30, each half of a split 90), and only over open, flat floor (the decal box, 8 cm deep, smeared a pool spread to a wall down it): `gore.wall_room` as it starts (level traces in 16 directions at 3 and 10 cm, 10 % short of the nearest surface steeper than 45 deg), `gore.ring_ok` before each next 6 cm ring (16 traces down onto it: floor facing up within 25 deg, within 3 cm of the pool's plane; two rings a pass for all pools). The decals are the game's own floor blood from the cinematics (the parking lot's `MI_D_NoiseBloodDecal_03/04`, drips and splats too: the Raven cinematic's `MI_FloorBloodDecal_5` drips were a brighter red than the pools), traced down onto the floor under the opening and turned to its slope; her meshes take no decals meanwhile. They don't flow: on a slope a pool is still a round stain. At `maxDecals` (150) drips and pools, the oldest drip is removed for the new one, a pool only when no drip is left (the oldest one no opening still spreads; `gore.decal_room`): refused at the cap, an arm cut off her alive dripped 58 times as she fought on and her death left no floor blood. A death without a cut keeps the single pool under her pelvis. A limb cut while she stands gets the death ragdoll's collision profile on the mesh until she recovers: her living mesh has no physics collision with the world, so the piece fell through the floor.
5. It plays the game's own gore sounds (`sounds`, `soundVolume`, through `GameplayStatics.PlaySoundAtLocation`, with no owner). Each kind has a few sets of cues from one family (`SFX` in `gore.lua`): a cut is `Mon_slice_Big_Cue` or `Mon_slice_Small_Cue`, sometimes with `hit_Linkskill_Blood_01_Cue`; a beheading, leg, waist cut or split (big) is a slice with `PC_LinkAttack_Boss_Gore_Splash_Cue` (or with the blood cue). One set plays per event, at random but never one with a cue that played in the last 3 s (a cue still playing drops its next start), at pitch 0.92-1.08. A limb cut logs `cut stretch` (the stretch probe on her mesh) and `cut chains` (how far each kept chain's bodies hang from its anchor) at 1 and 5 s.
6. It spawns the blood: a splat on the floor under the cut (`gore.splat`, a decal of the pools' blood, `splatSize`; the game's splat effect `NS_Hit_Flesh_Decal_Strong_01` put a brighter red down than the pools), a bleed from each opening for `bleedTime` seconds, attached to that side's cap (so the severed piece bleeds from its stump and not from its middle) and pointed out of the opening along its normal. The current bleed is the 0.5.5 blood-only Niagara system on each stump until the end of `bleedTime`; hit-particle spurts and `NS_A_BloodLoop` remain parked. A living low-HP arm cut uses the same attached pulsing stump effect on both openings, and retains the splat and floor drips/pools. The `bleed: opening faces ...` log line shows the component's Z and X axes, though the visual emitter direction still needs a playtest. `NS_Blood_Flooded_Attached`, the bleed before, locks its emission to a fixed axis (`SBLockAxis`) whatever it is turned to, so every spurt went the same wrong way. Last, floor blood: each opening drips while it moves and pools where it comes to rest, and a death without a cut pools under her pelvis once she settles. The pools are the parking lot cinematic's puddle decals (`MI_D_NoiseBloodDecal_03`/`_04`, one solid blob each), traced onto the floor; the Raven cinematic's (`MI_FloorBloodDecal_1-4`, a sheet of splats under a starburst mask) and the game's looping floor effect read as stretched streaks. All of it is the red blood (`MI_A_DefaultBlood`, the common decal): the hit and splat had been the monster ones, the Naytiba's glowing orange liquid.

A **split** (`death.split`, only when the killing blow came down from above) cuts her in half top to bottom along the blade. The split comes first and the fall after it: from the moment it starts, her ragdoll is held where it is (gravity off, every body's velocity zeroed each tick, the muscle tone kept at full strength, at most 1 s), and when it opens both halves get back the velocities her bodies had (the death key's push, her momentum) and fall (`held still for the split`, `released after ...` in the log; the hold matches her mesh by address, as UE4SS hands out a new wrapper each call, and F11 never held before that). It used to start falling first and come apart a moment later. On an animated death (`active.animated`) her fall plays on while the split is cut, and she ragdolls as it opens: both halves come apart by physics at the speed the animation gave each body (two frames of it at least; `split open (death): ..., she ragdolls now` in the log). She used to ragdoll the moment the split was rolled and hang frozen in it for the ~0.4 s the cut took, so the ragdoll showed before the cut. With `splitAnim` above 0 both halves play on with her fall as one body (the copy master-posed to her mesh) for that many seconds after it opens first. The split brings its own blood: no hit splash at the death, and the seam bleeds `splitBleedDelay` (0.3 s) after it opens, once the halves have come apart (the hit's blood had come first and read as blood, then a split). `main.lua` hooks `SBNetworkPlayerController:ClientBroadcast_SkillHitResult`, the game's per-hit message with the swing's direction, the attacker's forward and a left-to-right / right-to-left / front attack direction, and keeps the hits that land on Eve (all logged, to calibrate the direction; the first 12 elsewhere too). Its parameters are read in the order the SDK dump declares (`inTurn, inSkillGuid, inSkillResultID, inCasterGUID, inTargetGUID, inSkillHitResult, inAttackGroupName, inHitResult, inHitLocation, ...`); an earlier version read them shifted, so no hit ever matched her. A death within `hitWindow` (0.6 s) of a hit whose direction points down by more than `splitDown` (0.6) may split; the plane holds the swing and the attacker's forward, made to contain her spine. `gore.lua` then:

1. adds a copy of her body (same mesh, materials and transform, not ticking, hidden);
2. copies her pose onto it, decides which arm and thigh go with which half, and cuts both bodies in Side mode, one keeping each side of the plane. Her half keeps the head whole, with her face and hair untouched on their own animation; the copy's neck closes (the `h` neck plane above). Each half gets one flat lid over its whole opening and the copy a domed one over its neck, her half one over the strip of neck it lost. Where the plane runs along the spine, each lid shows it halved: one `GM_Brokenbone` piece per spine segment (pelvis to neck), flat in the plane from joint to joint, towards her back, riding the segment's lower bone (`splitSpine` (off), `splitSpineWidth`, `splitSpineFlat`; `N spine pieces` in the lid's log line). The flat lid is the traced outline ear-clipped, with a skirt reaching `splitLidGrow` (0.4 cm) past the skin, `splitLidPush` (0.2 cm) out of the half, both faces; Outfit pieces on a half's pose (sleeves, gloves, suit parts) hide the bones of the limbs that went with the other half (`HideBoneByName`, PBO_None): one spanning both sides stayed whole and followed the dropped limb, a third arm on the half. The lid is one piece whose vertices each ride the two spine bones they lie between, blended by where along them they are (as the flattened skin behind it is: sbslice bands that sheet by the same bones, the head left out of both, and blends each vertex between its two bands' sinks the same way; a band with no free helper riding its bone, Spine and Spine1, gets a helper of another band held on its bone every frame by `H`, `bandheld=N` in the cut's line. Before, such a band went to its neighbour, a whole side's sheet could lie on one bone, `bands=1/ride`, and it came out through the bending lid), and move every tick for as long as the lid is on her (`splitLidFollow`, 0: no limit; after a fixed 12 s a ragdoll nudged later left it off her), skipped while nothing moved against the lid, so it bends with the ragdoll (`splitLidSkin`; `split lid on ...` in the log counts the lids bending). A rigid piece per spine bone, as before, cracked into shards as she bent. Halving the head had made the hair freak out (posed by the body, its chains lost their own simulation) and left one half bald or with a whole head;
3. moves every component of hers that is posed by her body or rides a socket on the lost half (gloves, suit pieces, accessories) onto the copy, so nothing floats where she was cut (`split: N pieces moved`; put back before the copies go). Her own lid and spine pieces stay: they stand out of her half over the plane, and moved onto the copy they left her half open and tore off the copy in shards. Whatever rides the head (hair, hairpins on `SC_HairACC`, earrings) stays on her;
4. collapses, gives the copy the pose she is in now and its own ragdoll (her physics asset, the Ragdoll profile, no collision with her), her velocity, and shows it. On each half, the limbs that went with the other half come off and fall free, invisible, at their own mass (the copy's head goes the same way). At 0.02 of their mass they were feather-light chains that shook on the floor; kinematic, they froze in the air with whatever outfit piece still rode them. Hiding them with their bodies removed (`HideBoneByName` with `PBO_Term`) stopped her ragdoll the moment the split opened, and her mesh never simulated again until the level reloaded. The spine bodies weigh half, the halves get `splitPush` (300 cm/s) apart, and a lengthwise cut's upper bodies `splitTip` (250) more, so the halves fall open. It bleeds along the lids.

The copy is made without collision, so it gets its physics bodies by `SetCollisionEnabled` when it opens (a profile change alone never made them, and the copy stood frozen); `split copy ragdoll:` and `split copy at 0.5 s / 2.0 s:` log whether it simulates and where its pelvis is.

Known rough edges: the physics bodies are full width on both halves, and accessories on other components aren't split (each goes whole with the half it hangs off). F11 ragdolls her and splits her along the camera's view.

**Cutting along the blade.** The skill-hit message never came in a single-player session (0 calls), so the killing blow comes from three places, best first: the game's skill hit (within `hitWindow`), the last hit reaction on her mesh (`PhysicalHitForce*` and `VertexShakeInfo`, within `meshHitWindow`, logged as `mesh hit:`), or the nearest other character within `attackerReach` (800 cm). Without a swing, one is made up across the push from there: level, slanted or down, through where the mesh says she was hit or else a part of her picked with odds (`swing_at` in `main.lua`): mostly the chest and stomach, then the neck and arms, a leg about 1 in 6, a limb struck 40 % of the way down it. An even pick of 12 bones, a third of them legs and struck at the joint, had put nearly every death through a leg. `death: blow from ... (made up: <bone>)` names it. A made-up blow comes straight down when her mesh's hit points down or the nearest enemy drops onto her faster than `stompSpeed` (a stomp), else at a random angle. A blow from above (`splitDown`) never cuts her across the waist: a rolled torso cut becomes a split along it, and along a real blade the hip, knee, `bodyBias` and one-side rules split her through her spine along the blade instead, and a blade down her length always splits her. A made-up blow only aims the death: what comes off flies with its push, and it says whether the blow came from above, but the death rolls its odds (`death: no blade seen (blow from ...), rolling the odds`). Cut along, its guessed plane went through her spine most of the time, and 4 of 6 deaths in a playtest took her body, none a leg. A seen swing coming down (`splitDown`: a stomp, a slam, an overhead chop) is put on her chest, stomach or neck, rolled with the parts nearer its plane likelier (weight over 1 + cm off / 30), the plane moved through that part at its angle: down her body it splits her, at her neck (nearer the neck than `Spine2`) it beheads her (put on the part nearest its plane, a stomp took one shin). The weapons of the `swingFollow` (3) nearest enemies within `swingReach` are followed, each with its own trail, kept while it stays within `swingKeep` (2.5) times the reach (an enemy stepping back past `swingReach` between attacks had its trail start over and the blow that came right after went unseen; `swing: <enemy> no longer followed (...)` says when one is let go); at the death the one whose blade swept nearest her is the blow (`swing: N enemies followed, the blow is <enemy>'s`). Following only the nearest, its trail started over whenever another came nearer, and in a pack of them the killing swing went unseen and the death rolled its odds. A weapon's sweep is checked every ~15 cm of its blade's travel against her parts (three spots a step left 59 cm between a fast blade and her); a blade whose nearest step stays more than `swingMiss` (45) cm off every part of her missed her, and the death goes on with a made-up blow instead of putting its plane through her (a 433 cm weapon's plane took a standing Eve's shin). The part the sweep met (`swing: ... its blade N cm from her <part>`) is the hit, put on the plane: picked again by the plane alone, a tilted plane through her blocking forearm passed 5 cm from her thigh and the swing across her chest took both thighs and her waist. Any other seen swing more than `swingSnap` cm off her is moved onto her the same way (before, the point was put on the plane and the plane never moved). A plane down her length within `spineReach` of the spine splits her there, even when it misses the spine line itself. A death with a real blade (the skill hit or a weapon swing seen) is decided by that blade alone, nothing rolled over it (`bladeChance`, `bladeSplit`, `bodyBias` and `multiCut.bodyChance` stacked dice on the blade and the outcomes felt arbitrary): a blade across her body cuts it along the blade (the chest or the waist, the crossing nearest the hit clamped between `Spine1` and `Spine2`) and every arm and leg the plane crosses within `multiCut.reach` comes off after it, an arm or leg lying in the plane too (both ends within 10 cm of it: a forearm held across her to block a level swing), cut where it is nearest the hit and leaning with the blade; one down her length splits her along it, after the arms its plane crosses within `multiCut.reach` (an arm raised to block, nearest first, each along the blade; `splitArms`: at the hit the first arm comes off then and the rest and the split at the death). The split takes a body whose only cuts are arm cuts: it saves her weights (`S`), gives them to the copy (`y`) before either half is cut, judges a cut-off arm's side by its clavicle (the arm has flown), and once it opens the copy takes an arm whose shoulder it keeps: the piece's joint on the copy breaks (`break_limb`) with her piece's velocity, and the cut's caps and stubs (noted on its `cuts` entry) ride the copy's bones (`split: the copy takes the arm cut off before`); a split that fails restores her with `R`, the arm cuts kept (`split undone, her arm cuts kept`); one that meets an arm or a leg first takes it and the others it crosses, and goes on into her body where its plane passes through her spine within `multiCut.bodyReach`. For variety each blade is turned up to `bladeJitter` (15) degrees about a random axis and moved up to `bladeShift` (10) cm along its normal, so a sweep across her chest takes one arm, both, or an arm and the chest; a death that would take the same first part as the last one tries up to `bladeTries` (4) other turns of it (one enemy's one attack took the same arm every time). A blade that meets her body nowhere near the hit still rolls the odds. The blade moved in the plane holding the swing's direction and the attacker's forward, through where it hit; when those two are (nearly) parallel, the game's attack direction picks a level plane (left-to-right, right-to-left, slanting a little) or an upright one (front). What the plane goes through decides the cut:

- an arm or leg it crosses within `limbReach` (12 cm) of the hit, when the hit is more than `spineReach` (15 cm) from the spine: that limb, at the crossing, leaning with the blade up to `sliceTilt` (45°) off square. A plane running along the limb doesn't count;
- a plane past the spine that only meets a thigh at its hip joint (under 0.25 down it), or a calf when the hit is close to the spine: across the waist instead (`it only met ...`);
- the neck, with the chest clearly on the other side from the head: a beheading, leaning with the swing;
- anywhere else through or along the spine: the body in two along the plane, at any angle (down the middle, across the waist, shoulder to hip). It is the split above with the blade's plane: the half with the head stays her own mesh and keeps the head, face and hair whole, and where the plane crosses the spine both halves' ragdolls break there, so neither piece is held on by the invisible bodies of the other end. Those invisible bodies fall free, so neither half's kept skin may hang on them: sbslice `g` gets the break bone and which half it is, and the half above it moves every weight on the other side of the break to the break bone (the pelvis is the spine's parent, so its nearest kept ancestor would be no body at all), the half below to the nearest kept ancestor. That skin may shift a few cm at the cut, as the bones it moves between aren't posed alike (`POP_CM`, 4, in the tests); left on the free bodies, it stretched after them: a waist split 9 cm over the pelvis pulled her torso a metre long to a point (playtest 40). The Biped thighs hang off `Bip001-Spine`, not the pelvis, so a break there would free the copy's own legs: the copy breaks one bone up instead (`spine breaks at Bip001-Spine (the copy's at Bip001-Spine1)` in the log), and further up while any limb it keeps still hangs off the break. Her half breaks only when every limb she keeps hangs off the break: a lengthwise slice keeps an arm and a leg on each side, and a break at Spine2 tore her thigh from her shoulder across 1.6 m of skin (playtest 44), so it has no break (`spine breaks at nil`). `test/real/run.sh` throws each half's free side 40 cm off (`LOW=9` with `ACROSS=1`: that split). A plane that meets the spine only between the pelvis and the first spine bone went through her hips (the legs stayed, the crotch flattened into the cut face with the suit stretched over it): it moves up to 40 % of the way from Spine to Spine1, at the same angle (`it crossed the hips`). A plane along her with every arm and leg on one side only grazes her (a 45° blade on an animated death left one half a sliver and the copy a whole body over her, so nothing looked cut): it cuts across the waist instead (`it ran along her`). As a last check, a split leaving either half under `splitMinShare` (12 %) of her vertices, or anything unsunk, is undone and becomes a waist cut (`split ... degenerate`), and so is one whose half cut nothing at all (`split ... failed: the copy cut nothing`: in an animated death just gone ragdoll, a split across her waist left the copy with no seam and nothing sunk, and she died whole);
- nothing usable (no plane, or a plane missing her): the usual roll.

**Legs falling on (`torsoLegs`).** A death's cut across her waist (`torso`) used to put all of her in the ragdoll first. While her animated fall plays, `gore.torso_legs` may keep it: `level` (0.75) odds when the killing blade's plane is within `levelDeg` (30) of flat, `slant` (0.35) when it isn't, `rolled` (0.5) with no blade seen (`waist cut: legs on in the fall at ... odds` in the log). Then main.lua isn't asked for the ragdoll: the cut breaks the upper body's joint as it does a limb's (its bodies simulate, the rest stays on the animation), and it is flung with the blow, while her hips and legs play the fall on, the waist stump's cap riding them. Before the joint breaks, a weapon held in either hand is released onto its physics box, so it cannot follow the invisible animated arm; recovery restores its attachment. Holstered weapons are left alone. convulse.lua counts the arms and head as cut off once a spine bone is (its twitches would have blended them back onto the animation, onto her legs). Her fall not playing still puts all of her in the ragdoll.

The log line `blade (...)` shows the plane, the hit's direction, forward and attack direction, how far each spine bone is from the plane and what it chose. Delete ragdolls her and cuts along a made-up blade through a part of her picked as for a death (level, 30°, 60° or straight down, the attacker where the camera is).

While a cut is on, every component it forced to LOD0 is checked twice a second (`gore.lod_guard`: `ForcedLodModel` 1, `bOverrideMinLod`, `MinLodModel` 0) and forced again if anything took it off (`LOD: <mesh> taken off LOD0 ...: forced again`, once a component): the cut's weights are on LOD0 alone, and drawn at another LOD her cut limbs stretched from the body to the flying pieces (0.4.0 reports, a crowded area). On revive it undoes all of it: skin weights, collapsed bones, caps, effects, forced LOD, physics state and the split's copies.

Settings live in `tune.lua` under `gore`: `enabled`, `sliceOnDeath`, `sliceChance`, `death` (odds of limb / head / torso / split / diagonal, for deaths with no blade seen or the blade left aside by `bladeFollow`), `oddsNear` (those odds, when rolled, shaped by where the blow landed, `gore.near_odds`: each kind's weight times 1 within `near` (15) cm of what it cuts, down to `floor` (0) at `far` (35) cm: the neck's segment for a beheading, Pelvis to Spine2 for a waist cut, Pelvis to Neck for a split, the nearest uncut arm or leg segment for a limb; from the killing blow's `loc` on its pose, else her ragdoll's HitLocation. A blade's plane down her length keeps `across` (0.25) of the waist cut's, one across her none of the split's, a blow from above none of the waist cut's, no plane `noPlane` (0.5) of the split's. Odds left with nothing near the hit are rolled as they are, so odds of one kind alone still do it every death. `main.lua`'s `pick_kind` and `death_roll` take the factors; at the lethal hit they are kept in `hm.lethal.near`. `death: odds by the hit: ...` gives the distances. A clear waist cut rolled a beheading a quarter of the time, playtest), `bladeJitter`, `bladeShift`, `bladeTries` (a limb's blade only: one through her body, a split or a body cut, is taken as it came), `deathSplit` (a blade down her length at death: off, its limbs come off instead), `splitArms` (its arms within `multiCut.reach` come off first: off, each stays whole on its half), `followUpCuts` (false: one cut a swing, her body over the limbs its plane crossed; no `multiCut`, `splitArms`, `armsOverSplit` or `moreLimbs` extras; a laser still cuts everything), `multiCut` (one swing cuts every limb and the neck its plane crosses within `reach` cm of the hit, up to `max`; `body`: when the arm or leg it took first has its plane go on through her spine below the neck within `bodyReach` cm of the hit, or pass within `bodyGraze` (10) cm beside it, through her flank, her body is cut too, across it under her bust (at 0.3 from Spine1 to Spine2), leaned with the blade at most `bodyTilt` (15°) off square (square to the spine when the blade runs down her length), before the other limbs, with at most one leg among the cuts once her body is cut (a thigh, the waist and the other thigh left her pelvis a piece of its own); the log's `then her body at Bip001-Spine1 N cm in` says `the blade N cm beside her spine` and `square` when they apply), `woundAtHit` and `cutAtHit` (main.lua's lethal watch reads her HP bar every tick while she lives; armed once it reads above 0 (it reads 0 through a respawn) and disarmed by a death, the tick it reaches 0, `gore.wound` puts blood and the slice sound where the blade met her, and `gore.blow` runs with `h.early`: a limb, the head or her body comes off then, on her animated body, while a split, the blow's further cuts (held in `gore.held`) and her sword's cut wait for the death, where `gore.release_held` queues them and the death makes no cut of its own; the death show's ragdoll key, the death as the mod sees it, came 0.2-0.5 s after the hit; a blow she then lives through (her bar back above 0: a revive, a skill; or, with it still at 0 as at 1 HP after a fall, `hm.plays_on`: no death `hm.LIVED_S` (6) s on, her movement on, no death screen or death montage, and her capsule `hm.LIVED_CM` (150) cm from where the blow found her; time alone undid the cut of a death slow to show) has that cut undone as a death's end undoes it (`lethal hit: she lived (...)`): it had stayed on her as she played on, a stump's seam at her neck, reported), `openWait` (frames between a cut's stubs and its piece coming off, 0: the frame it waited showed each cut ~50 ms later), `openTime`, `openGap`, `openSwing`, `openPush` (gradual open, `gore.hold_open`: the piece's root joint breaks as before, but a PhysicsConstraintComponent from the nearest body above it to its root (or to each top body `break_limb` broke) holds it on, limited to 0.5 cm and 2° at first; every tick for `openTime` s its linear limit grows to `openGap` cm and its swing to `openSwing`° (twist half), with an `openPush` cm/s nudge along the cut at the start, then it is broken and destroyed and the throw goes in (the throw would be lost on a held piece); the joints are in `hung`, so a reset takes them), `queueGap` (frames between one cut of a swing and the next, 1: a waist cut started right after a thigh's held the frame 126 ms in the engine's weight override, waiting on the render thread), `sliceTilt` (45°), `bodyTilt`, `splitYaw` (a body cut is across her or down her length, snapped by the blade's angle to her spine at 45°: a split always runs through her middle left from right, turned at most `splitYaw` (15°) towards the blade, and a blade down her front or back cuts across her instead; one slanting `diagonal.bladeFrom`-`bladeTo` (20-60°) off her spine and not facing her front or back cuts diagonally), `diagonal` (`gore.diag_plane`, `gore.diagonal`; parked: `enabled` false in tune.lua, no F3 rows or test key, and sbslice.c's two fixes for it, `SHEET_OUT` and `NECK_PAST`, off under `DIAG_PARKED`; the harness runs it only as `test/run.sh diag` with `DIAG_PARKED` 0. A split whose plane runs from `lift` (6) cm over one shoulder's joint to `hip` (8) cm over the other hip's joint and `out` (7) cm out past it, holding her front-to-back axis, `jitter` (2) cm on lift and hip: 33-40° off her spine. Set by the joints, not an angle, so her half always keeps the head and the other arm and the copy that arm and both legs; her half's spine breaks at Spine1 or Spine2 and the copy's at the head (`copy_break`: its arm hangs off the neck). Steeper, nearer the drawn 30°, the far hip's skin her half keeps rides her chest once her spine breaks and shifted up to 9 cm at the cut on a bent spine (`real_split.c` `DIAG=R|L`, `DIAGLIFT`, `DIAGHIP`, `DIAGOUT`; the spine pop allowed there is 6 cm, 5.9 at most at 30° of bend). A chain hanging off a Dm- driver of a limb's side goes with the half that keeps that limb (`split_chains`: a shoulder's gear over the diagonal stayed on her half and flew off with the arm), and a band's point sink on a bone riding another band is held on the band's bone like the sheets (`sink_anchor`: the copy's neck point sat on a hip helper and stuck out through the lid as her spine bent). Rolled with `death.diagonal`, 0 in tune.lua so an older settings.lua's one-kind odds still do that kind every death, 15 in settings.default.lua. Alt+Delete (Test: Diagonal Cut) cuts it from over her right shoulder, then her left), `limbReach`, `spineReach`, `splitDown`, `hitWindow`, `meshHitWindow`, `attackerReach`, `splitPush`, `splitTip`, `splitBleedDelay`, `splitSpine`, `splitSpineWidth`, `splitSpineFlat`, `splitMinShare`, `splitLidGrow`, `splitLidPush`, `attackerIgnore` (class names never taken for her killer: her drone, playable characters), `legRagdoll` (a leg cut on her own death fall switches to the ragdoll), `deathFace` (the death face played by the mod: `enabled`, `variant`, `at`), `voice` (her cry on any death, spawned on her head so it flies with a cut-off one;
