when the head comes off it fades out over `voice.cutFade` (0.08 s), as a sharp stop clicks: `voice: cut off N s in as her head came off` in the log), `blowPose` (her pose, 18 bones' world transforms, is noted with every sample of the enemy's swing while she is alive, and `gore.blow` judges a killing blade against the pose of the sample it came from instead of her bones a frame into the death, which starts on the death show's ragdoll key after the show has moved her arms; what it picks is moved with its bone, or Spine1, to where that bone is now), `weaponSwing` (killing cuts along the plane the nearest enemy's weapon swept: its weapon meshes' blades (the long axis of each mesh's bounds, both ends sampled; the plane holds the blade and the way its faster end moved, `blade <mesh>` in the log, and `swing: <enemy> holds <mesh> N cm on <socket>` when it is picked up; of the steps faster than `swingMinSpeed`, the one whose blade, swept over spots every ~15 cm of the step, came nearest one of her parts where they were with that sample, a fast one up to `swingSpeedPull` cm ahead (all of it at `swingSpeedFull`, 9000 cm/s), an older one `swingAgeCost` cm behind per second before the death (a slow brush past her calf 0.27 s before took a leg over the slash across her body) and one moving away from that part `swingAwayCost` cm behind: the fastest alone was the pull-back after a blocked blow, which missed her and was moved onto a calf; `swing: step N s before of N fast ones, its blade N cm from her <part>` in the log), else the arc of its fastest weapon bone; `swingReach`, `swingWindow`, `swingMinSpeed`, `swingSpeedPull`, `swingSpeedFull`, `swingAgeCost`, `swingAwayCost`, `swingSnap`, `swingBones`), `splitLidSkin`, `splitLidFollow`, `splitRigOff` (a split calls `SetDisablePostProcessBlueprint(true)` on both halves in its prep stage, 3 frames before the cut: the outfit's post-process AnimBP (`CH_P_EVE_09_AnimBP`) poses the Dm- drivers after the limbs, and a half's sinks include drivers of the limbs it keeps (the real-mesh harness prints them: `half A: sink Dm-R-Trape, which a Dm- driver moves`), so in the ragdoll the collapsed skin on them came out through the lid; `gore.rig_back` turns it on again from `undo_split` and the cleanup before she is reused), `probes` (stretch probe, helper drift and masses read back after cuts, off: a stutter of their own; `dev` no longer turns them on), `slowMs` (a tick of the mod's this slow logs as `slow tick` with its parts; sbslice commands this slow are logged, and a cut stage this slow logs its steps; every stage logs when it ran after the cut was asked for, and a frame over 70 ms logs as `frame hitch` with the mod's last tick and stage; once a minute `frame cost` gives the mod's time a frame on her tick, split into the work done every frame and the poll's (each poll's ms, and its costliest `tp.mark` parts, ms a poll: `the poll's parts, ms each: enemies 0.40, outfit, hits 0.21, ...`)) The enemies' part of a poll (enemies.lua's tick) runs half a poll after the rest, on a frame of its own (`screen.enemiesDue`), and an enemy or corpse out of `reach` at its last look is looked at every 4th poll (`FAR_EVERY`), on a poll of its own by its address; active.lua's settle looks over a ragdoll asleep with nothing left to move her every 4th poll. Bone FNames are made once (`FN`) in enemies.lua and active.lua., `bone`, `cutAt`, `hitMarks` (see Hits she lives through), `sounds`, `soundVolume`, `chains`, `splitAnim`, `tilt`, `blowTilt`, `throw`, `deathThrow`, `legThrow` and `legLift` (a cut-off leg that drops off flies 1.4 times as fast and steeper), `throwUp` (the steepest climb of a thrown piece), `pieceGravity` and `pieceFloat` (a cut-off arm or leg falls at 0.55 of gravity for its first 3 s), `throwTicks` (for 3 ticks after a cut the piece's main bodies (a cut-off head or upper body's too) are kept at least as fast as the throw: it went in the tick they started simulating and was mostly lost, `piece ...: N cm/s along the throw` in the log), `splitFly` (both halves of a death split fly with the blow), `splitFlyUpper` (the upper body of one across the waist, faster), `caps`, `capScale`, `boneStubs`, `stubBone`, `spineBoneMax`, `blood`, `sliceFx`, `bleedTime`, `bleedAxis`, `bleedSize`, `bleedPulse`, `capMaterial`, `capBloodColor`, `bloodTint`, `debugCaps` (every cap and lid glows green, to tell them from skin), `bodyBlood` (legacy cut-only ramp, now off by default and suppressed by Blood and Dirt), `hitSplat`, `pool`, `poolScale`, `poolGrow`, `poolTime`, `dripEvery`, `maxDecals`. F9 cuts while she is alive, for testing (a roll of the cut odds, or `bone`); F10 puts a hit mark on her facing the camera, F11 splits her, Delete cuts along a made-up blade. The cut needs a LOD0 CPU copy of the outfit mesh and, for a head, of the face (`CH_P_EVE_Face_Jali3`, the face Eve's blueprint uses); `sbslice.dll` keeps one for every skeletal mesh. The faces and the enemies' bodies used to ship in the core pak with `bAllowCPUAccess` set (`cpuaccess` jobs), and that vanilla copy of her face overrode face mods, whose other packages then didn't match it: the game stopped at launch. They are `extract` jobs now: `build.sh` only extracts them into its cache for the harness below. `native/sbslice/test/run.sh [group...]` (groups `unit`, `stats`, `split`, `waist`, `arm`, `mark`, `enemy`; none runs all, about 10 s: the real-mesh harness is built once and its runs go in parallel; the script's header says which groups a change needs) runs the cut natively on a synthetic body and leg, with the engine calls stubbed: every mode, the hit mark's skin query, the split's flattening (also on a turned pose) and limb point, forbidden sinks, and hanging pieces. When the build cache has the outfits it also runs `test/real/run.sh`: the split on CH_P_EVE_09 and CH_P_EVE_30's real meshes (dumped by `meshtool`, packed by `test/real/pack.py`), both halves on one mesh asset as in game, in the bind pose and in a bent pose that bends further after the cut. Neither half's kept skin may move off the vanilla skin, follow the limbs it drops (flung 40 cm), or leave a piece of the other half off the plane; each half's traced outline must not cross itself and must cover the cut (800 cm² lengthwise, 200 across the waist), her half must keep the whole head and the copy none of it, and a lengthwise split must send both neck lids. `ARM=1` runs F9's left arm cut with the cape cut off on its own as gore.lua does (`KEEPCAPE=1`: kept on the body, `WITH=1`: whole with the arm), then flings the arm and the cape apart and runs the stretch probe: no stretched triangle may stay visible. `LEGFIRST=<thigh>` (with `ARMBONE` the other thigh) cuts that thigh first: with both legs off nothing may stretch, and no seam may collapse into the other stump. For meshes with no cross-section triangles, island connectivity skips core sections that classification never reads; a preliminary index scan detects cross-section triangles and falls back to the full traversal. On the locally extracted wedding dress, four alternating native-harness runs showed a median of 165 ms before and 154 ms after (CPU time including harness and engine stubs, not in-game frame timings). A hanging island is treated as tied across a cut only when over three quarters of its minority-side vertices are mostly weighted to that side; otherwise a few vertices beyond a cut can remain with the body. `native/sbslice/test/real/test_cns_wedding.sh /path/to/dress.bin` checks each thigh and both cutting orders on a locally extracted CNS wedding mesh; the 674-vertex hanging panel previously produced nine visible stretched triangles. `WAIST=1` runs a waist cut instead: no vertex mostly on an arm may stay on the legs. With `ARMFIRST=1 ARMCAPE=1 ARMFLUNG=<cm>` the left arm and its cape are cut off first (the cape on its own, as gore.lua does) and flung with the arm's shoulder that far over the plane (under it: negative) before the body cut, named a drop root as gore.lua names an earlier cut's limb under the body cut (`NODROP=1`: not): no vertex of theirs may move.

Lose Arm at Low HP (`gore.lowArm`, off by default; main.lua `hm.low_arm`, on her tick after `hm.lethal_watch`): her HUD's HP bar (`glass.bar_pct`) dropping on a tick to under `below` (0.25), then holding still there for `hm.LOW_SETTLE` (0.3) s (`hm.LOW_ZERO_S`, 1.5 s, at a reading of 0: the bar reads 0 at about 1 % shown, where a fall left her alive), unless it came as she landed from a drop of `hm.LOW_LAND_CM` (200) cm or more (within `hm.LOW_LAND_S`, 1 s, of it) with no hit on her mesh (`meshHit`, the game's physical hit reaction) from `hm.LOW_HIT_S` (0.5) s before the drop on: fall damage. (Every drop used to need that hit on her mesh, and most blows never set it: ranged hits, grabs, explosions; Nexus: under 70% the arm came off now and then.) It takes the upper arm on the side the blow landed on off where she stands (`side` `"hit"`, the default: `gore.hit_side` names the side from the mesh hit's bone, `-L-` or `-R-`, else from where it landed off her spine along `gore.her_right`, nil within 3 cm of it; the mesh hit if one came, else the weapon sweep's point as the bar dropped; one at random with no side. `"left"`, `"right"`, or `"either"` at random; with any but a set side, the other when F9 already took that one) (`gore.slice_bone`, the F9 path: only the piece simulates, the enemy's swing aims it), once till it is back. The wait is for the bar's slide: a fall damage death read 0.499 -> 0.066 on its way to 0, the arm came off on the killing blow and the cut opening as the death began kept the death from being seen (no ragdoll, the death screen at once); a death that starts meanwhile drops the wait (`screen.low_reset`). With no hit on her mesh it was no blow (a fall, a drain): `low HP arm: ... not a blow, not cut`. An animated death turns all of her bodies' physics off (`animated_death`), then turns the parts cut off her alive back on below their roots (`gore.cut_parts`): turned off with the rest, the arm went back to her shoulder with her animation and flew off it at 72 m/s once the ragdoll came on.

With `noHealGun` (on by default) she can't use heal items or shoot while it is off. These are the game's own blocks, two of its actor states (`ESBActorState`: `ActorState_BlockItemUseHeal` 67, `ActorState_BlockItemUseBullet` 100: her gun's shots use up bullet items). The game keeps a character's states on a native actor of its own, not on the UObject: a `TMap<int32 state, int32 count>` at +0x560, counted up and down by `ApplyActorState(actor, state, add, clear)` (0x1a638c0, called from `FSBActorApplyState::Execute`; a character whose +0x2600 is 3-5 takes no add or take-off, only a clear). sbslice `a<pawn>,<state>,<op>` (op 1 add, 0 take off, 2 read, 3 clear) finds her native actor among the manager's (`TMap<int32 GUID, actor *>`, the getter at 0x1a97c40, its object's +0xc8) as the one whose FGuid at +0x4a4 the game's FGuid → UObject map (the getter at 0x1a97cb0, +0x48; 0x201b1e0) turns into her pawn, and counts one up or down, so a block of the game's own on the same state stays. Answer `ok state <state> <before> -> <after> life <+0x2600> of <native actors looked through>`. Her gun has no state of its own (holding these, she still drew it, playtest), but her aim is a stance: `P_Eve_Gun` and its tutorial, Black Rose and NIKKE kin in `CharacterStanceTable`, the rows whose `Condition_PressCommandKey1` is `SkillCommandState_TPSMode` (43). A stance row lists up to five actor states that rule it out (`Condition_DeactiveActorState1-5`, at +0x38-0x3c: the stance check at 0x1ba9f1e counts each on her with 0x1a64c10, the same map, and drops the stance on any count above 0); the gun's rows use the first two (`BlockSkill`, `BlockingBehavior`). So tables.lua's `gun_gate` has sbslice `B<table>,<row struct>,<sets>,<off>,<value>[,...],<match off>,<match value>,...` write `ActorState_BlockItemUseBullet` (100) into the fifth on every row whose `Condition_PressCommandKey1` or `2` is TPSMode, whatever it held: while the shots' block is on her, she can't aim. (Before that, the skill commands that aim and fire, `SkillCommandTable`'s TPSMode, TPSFire and ranged-fire rows, were made unmeetable, wall-running while she swims: she still aimed, playtest.) The old bytes are kept, and `B` alone puts them back (`ok gated <rows> of <rows>, <left alone: no room>`; `test/run.sh rows`).

With the right arm off, `noSword` (on by default) takes her sword: the game's own block, from Altess Levoire, where her sword is taken from her. The effect `P_Eve_Stance_BlockSword` (EffectTable, infinite, flag of the same name) puts her in the stance `P_Eve_BlockSword` (`bForceFoldingWeapon`: the sword folded; its commands only evades, getups, breakfall and `P_Eve_BlockSword_Normal_*` on the attack keys, the drone's scan blocked; `ActorState_DisableLockonTarget`), her aim then takes `P_Eve_GunBlockSword` (it needs that effect), and nothing blocks heals. `P_Eve_Stance_BlockSword_End` dispels it by its flag. The cheat manager's effect commands are stubs, but `SBBlueprintLibrary::CreateSBZoneEvent(ZoneEvent_AttachActorEffect (2), <EventActorEffectTable row>)` is callable from Lua and puts a row's effect on her: `ATL_01_E_ActorEff_005` applies the stance's effect. No row applies its end, so tables.lua's `sword_gate(false)` names `P_Eve_Stance_BlockSword_End` in `ATL_01_E_ActorEff_002`'s `ActorEffectAlias` (its own `P_Eve_BlockSword` is in no effect table, so it does nothing in the game) with sbslice `d` (the FName's comparison index), fires it, and puts the old name back `SWORD_HOLD` (2) s later from its tick (the event may read the row after the call). The end is fired whatever pawn she has by then: a dispel of nothing does nothing. The stance's attack commands all name the skill `P_Eve_BlockSword_Normal_Attack1_1`, whose one step (`SkillActiveStepTable`, 0.2 s) has no hit detection and only shows the notice `UI_BlockSwordSkill`. A sword in her right hand as the arm comes off alive stays where it falls (active.lua `stand_in`): the game's swordless stance folds her own sword back into her hair at once, so the real one dropped left the floor unseen (playtest). A `PoseableMeshComponent` on her sword's mesh and materials, its pose copied (`CopyPoseFromSkeletalComponent`), drops on the box a dropped weapon rides; hers is hidden (`SetHiddenInGame`, which the fold, going by visibility, doesn't touch) while it is out. The fold puts the same mesh at her head as her hairpin, and hidden there the pin was gone (playtest), so `standins_watch` (every `active.tick`) follows it: once it heads for her head (under 60 cm of `Bip001-Head` after being further, or there already), the copy's box stops simulating and carries the copy up to `SC_HairACC` over 0.6 s, scaling it to 0.15 (`standin_fly`); with the copy landed and hers within 40 cm of her head for 0.3 s, hers is shown and the copy destroyed. Past 60 cm again (drawn with No Sword Without It off: no hand to hold it) hers is hidden. The cut hand is no reference, it lies on the floor with the piece. Every cut undone (`M.mended`): the copy gone, hers shown. A death meanwhile drops neither again, and its end destroys the copy with the boxes.

With `notice` (on by default) her aim or heal key going down while the blocks are on (the keys of her controller's `TPSMode` and `Item_UseHeal` action mappings, AZERTY ones left out, read when the blocks go on) shows the game's own system notice, at most once a second and not while the game is paused. The game's refused aim shows `UI_GetNotice_Fail_ActiveTpsMode` of `StringTable_UI` ("Cannot use right now.", 지금은 사용할 수 없습니다.) at 0x1a68d4b: it makes the text with 0x262d320 (`(FText *out, const FString *table, const FString *key)`, the table named under `/Game/Local/Localize/%s.%s`, so the player's language), builds an event (type 9 at +0, an FString at +0x20, the FText at +0x30) and hands it to the UI manager's logics (the getter at 0x1a978c0, its +0x48: an array at +0x10, count +0x18; each entry's +0x60 a logic) from the last one down, to each whose vtable +0x10 says type 6 (`ESBUILogicType_HUDSystemNotice`), through vtable +0x68, till one answers true. sbslice `U<table>;<key>` does the same and lets its text go after (`ok notice <taken> of <logics>`, `test/run.sh rows` on faked ones). The sound is the notice slot's own (`WB_MainHUD_SystemNoticeSlot` plays `ui_SystemNotice_Cue`). The blocks follow the menu's switch while the arm is off, and go when it is back, at her recovery (a take-off that changed nothing is a clear then), or with her pawn.

It comes back at a camp rest (`hm.rest_watch`), and so does every other part cut off her alive (F9's limbs, head and upper body, `gore.cut_parts`), whether Lose Arm at Low HP is on or not: she sits down out of battle (her `CharacterMovement.MovementMode` 0, `IsBattle` false) and then rests: the camp's chair sequence (`Interaction_Camp_Chair`, only at a camp's seat) has played for `restDelay` (5) s, when the rest's restore comes, or she gets up from it (`Interaction_Camp_Chair_End`); or the game's `Camp_Restore` level sequence plays (the save, her heal, the enemies back; any `LevelSequenceActor` whose player `IsPlaying` and whose sequence or actor is named so, looked for once a second while she sits, each other sequence playing logged as `regrow: sequence playing as she sits`; its name from the player's `GetSequence`, else the actor's `LevelSequence` path, else `?` with the actor's name), or her HP bar, under 0.99 as she sat, reads 0.99 or more within `restWindow` (20) s of her last tick seated (the rest's heal; with no heal items, nothing else of hers fills it so fast). Sitting down alone grew F9's parts back as she sat, before the rest: they come off at full HP. The game's save and zone reset (`RegisterSaveGame`, `ResetZone` on its network controller) were tried as the signal: they never pass through anything a hook sees. Not in photo mode or a pause, which stop her movement too, or at her death's end (the cut goes with every other one, `screen.low_reset`). With `healBack` (off by default) it also grows back once her HP bar reads `healAt` (0.9) or more, however it got there (gear that heals on kills or over time), once the bar has read under it since the cut and 3 s after it with the cut done (`gore.busy`): the test keys cut at full HP, and the arm and her sword came back the same tick, the dissolve begun on a cut still opening. No part dissolves back while a cut opens (`hm.parts_back` waits). A cut undone some other way (a lethal hit she lived through, F8) lifts the blocks. At a rest each part dissolves back in (`gore.ghost_begin`, `lowArm.dissolve`, 3 s): the cut stays on, the part's bodies stop simulating so its bones are on her pose again, and on every mesh of hers it was cut on (her body, her face for a head) sbslice `V<comp>,<lod>,<keep>,<root>...` puts it out of sight (keep 0: each of its vertices, by its strongest bone, whole onto a collapsed sink of its section, the hidden side's if it has one, else one borrowed into the section's BoneMap and given back by `r`; the seam's triangles are lines between the two sinks already and stay so). A copy of the mesh on her pose (`SetMasterPoseComponent`, no weights copied onto it) keeps its own weights, the asset's, for the part, which `V` keep 1 reads off its master's (hers, with the cut), and for the band of the rest's vertices on a triangle with the part's (her seam the cut collapsed; on her cut's weights, copied with `y` before, the arm's seam closed onto a point and that band showed on neither, so the arm dissolved in standing off her shoulder). Everything else goes out of sight onto a point of its own: a bone no vertex of the asset can be on (in no section's own BoneMap, no sink), the one nearest the part's root joint, collapsed onto that joint through the regrow's matrices (borrowed into the sections' BoneMaps; `Z` alone or `r` put it back). Only point sinks ever hide anything: a flattening sink presses its vertices onto the cut plane and a carrier holds them as they were, so the copy's whole outfit, hidden onto the shoulder's carrier, showed rigid on it, 50 cm off once she sat. A kept vertex's weight on any collapsed sink goes onto the bone the sink hangs from. The copy wears the game's corpse dissolve, `MI_DeadMaterial_LurkerA_01` (`MA_DeadMaterial_01`, masked), each slot given its own material's base colour, normal and ORM textures; the despawn show ramps `Dissolve` 0 → 1, `Morph` 0 → 1 and `EmissiveBright` 10 → 0, so the regrow runs `Dissolve` and `Morph` 1 → 0 on a smoothstep, the glow peaking at `glow` halfway (`gore.ghost_set`). Her own skin can't dissolve: `MA_Skin_EVE` is opaque. Sections where nothing could be collapsed (`V` answers their `mats <material>/<section>`) are hidden whole with `ShowMaterialSection`. At the end the cuts are undone under the copy (`gore.ghost_release` takes it off the clones `gore.cleanup` destroys, her hidden sections shown) and the copy dissolves out over her own skin in `dissolve.fade` (0.6) s (`gore.ghost_fade`: `Dissolve` 0 → 1, no `Morph`, no glow), then goes (`gore.ghost_end`; `regrow: ... dissolved in after 3.6 s`): swapped in one frame, the corpse material's look jumped to her skin's (playtest: a snap). The cleanup's `x` puts the cut's sinks back at once, which the copy no longer rides: on them, its hidden side came back with them and her whole body showed round the arm on the fading copy (playtest: a glitchy limb right after it came back), and holding them down dented her own shoulder on them. The rest of the cleanup's put back (sbslice `r`: the copy's point and borrowed BoneMap entries) waits for the copy to go (`gore.sinksHeld`, `gore.ghost_end`). A cut of hers made meanwhile keeps them down for its own cleanup. At her death, in photo mode or at a load the copy goes and the cuts are undone at once. `test/run.sh unit` checks `V` on the made-up leg both ways: no hidden triangle with any area, every kept one where the cut's weights put it (the copy's seam off its sinks aside). `test/run.sh arm` runs the whole regrow on the real outfits (`real_split.c` `REGROW=1`, CH_P_EVE_09, _10 and _30; left arm, right arm, and seated, `REPOSE=1`, the pose changed after the cut): at each stage (cut, dissolve, fade, end) every triangle her mesh and the copy show is held against the vanilla skin on the same pose, and the copy must show nothing of her body, nothing reaching out of her, and not move as her cut is undone under it; she must be her vanilla skin from the hand-off on, with every matrix put back at the end. `REGROWOBJ=<prefix>` writes each stage's meshes, and `test/real/regrow/render.py <prefix>` draws them (her grey, the copy orange; `ZOOM=1` closer). With `effect` false (F3 → Regrow Effect) the cuts are undone at once, with neither. If the dissolve can't be set up (`regrow: no dissolve (...)`) or `dissolve = false`, the cuts are undone (`gore.cleanup`) and each part grows back from its root (`hm.GROW_JOINTS`: an arm, a forearm, a leg or a calf by its joints, the head alone, the upper body by Spine1, Spine2 and the neck; `regrow: ...` in the log). The arm grows back out of her shoulder over `regrow` (1.5) s: sbslice `Z<comp>,<upper arm>,<forearm>,<hand>,<permille>` each tick, on one component of every asset of hers with the arm's bones (her body, a sleeve or a glove), scales the upper arm's subtree about its joint, the forearm's about the elbow and the hand's about the wrist, each from 0.02 to full over 0.6 of the time, 0.2 after the one above it (`regrow_scale`). As the grip's ease does, it goes through the asset's inverse bind matrices: each bone gets `invBind * Posed * T * Posed^-1`, T its joints' scalings (its own deepest first), kept and put back by `Z` alone or `r`. The first frame (every entry at 0 permille) answers `ok regrow <bones set per entry>` (-1: the component or root unreadable), logged as `regrow: first frame: ...`. Her death stops it with the matrices back. `test/run.sh weapon` checks it on the made-up sword (grip and blade standing for the upper arm and forearm: at the start within 2 % of their reach of the grip's joint, halfway the grip further grown than the blade, at the end where the pose has them, and every matrix put back).


A level change while she lives (a level gone into, an elevator, a load) brings a new Eve, uncut. The game's cutscenes play on her own pawn: their sequences bind her as the possessable `Player` (class `CH_P_EVE_01_Sequncer_BP_C`, the editor's stand-in; `MV_ATL01_WLB_TO_AltesLab`, `MV_WLA30_Entering_AltesLab`, `EnvObj_ATL01_ScanOn`), not a spawned copy. So `hm.carry_note` keeps the parts cut off her alive and her HP bar while she plays, `hm.carry_take` (from `forget_world`, nothing after a death) carries them, and `hm.carry_tick` cuts each again on the new Eve once the level's quiet 5 s are up, with `why` "carried": `gore` `stage_open` then only hides the piece's bones (`M.smashed.hide`, as a smash) and shows the stump's caps: no piece, no sound, no blood, no weapon drop. Such a cut is `quiet` in `cuts`: `cut_parts(mesh, true)` (a piece that lies somewhere: piece settling, photo mode's copy, the death's simulated parts) leaves it out; a camp rest grows it back as any. Lose Arm at Low HP's blocks went with the old pawn (the gun's table gate put back) and go on the new one from `hm.low_arm`. A save loaded is a new Eve too: once the game sets the new HP bar (it reads full till then), a value off hers by more than `CARRY_HP` (0.02), or a bar still full `CARRY_FULL_S` (60) s on when hers wasn't, puts the parts back at once (`gore.cleanup`). An outfit change over a carried part cuts it again carried too. Reload sim.

While she plays her meshes take decals (`gore.decals_back` from `hm.alive_watch`): the floor blood's shield (`shield`, no decals on the meshes of a cut while its pools spread) is only for her lying in them, put on again by `on_settled`. Kept on while she played with an arm off, it kept every decal off her body: Altess Levoire's Purification Scanner projects its grid on her as decals (`MA_A_Decal_TrapGrenade` spawned by `EnvObj_ATL01_ScanOn`), which then showed on her face mesh alone. Lowarm sim case 1.
## Active ragdoll

**Animated deaths (experimental, `active.animated`).** Stand and light-hit deaths play her own fall instead of a ragdoll. The death table points `StandDeadShow`, `Step0DeadShow` and `Step1DeadShow` at `P_Eve_Dead`, `P_Eve_Dead2` and `P_Eve_Dead3` (they pointed at the animation-less ragdoll clones), and the ragdoll key in those, `P_Eve_Dead_Nikke` and `Eve_Dead_Down` carries `ImpulsePower 1` and `ImpulseLimitVelocity 701`. That key is only the signal that she died: `main.lua` reads the 701 in `RagdollInfo`, turns physics back off within a frame and lets the show's animation play on. It holds its end pose (`bHoldEndTime`), so nothing jiggles on the floor. Cuts happen on the animated body as on a standing Eve (F9): the piece simulates on its own. A waist cut or a split switches to the ragdoll before cutting (`animated death: ragdoll after all`). Heavy hits (`Step2`, `Step3`), explosions, airborne and ledge deaths stay ragdolls, and so does a death right after a drop: the landing's own damage (`Landing_Height_Damage` in `EffectTable`, no dead type of its own) leaves her at 1 HP, as the game does with the mod off (playtest), so what kills her there is a blow as she lands, and a death within `landWindow` s of landing from `landDrop` cm or more is a ragdoll (`fallRagdoll`); a higher drop's `Landing_Height_Stiff_Damage` plays `Custom5`, `P_Eve_Dead_Falling`, a ragdoll already. Only death shows carry a ragdoll key, so a knockdown (`Result_Knock_Down*`, `Down_BS`, `Groggy_*`, getups) never counts as a death. The log says `death: animated fall, physics off` or `death: ragdoll show`. More falls: the data mod builds eight death shows from her knockdown falls (`Ragdoll/EveDeath_*`: `Result_Knock_Down_Start`, `_Start1`, `_Forward`, `_Backward`, `Result_State_KnockDown_S_Fw`, `_S_Bw`, `_Tumble_Fw`, `_Tumble_Bw`), each a copy of `P_Eve_Dead` with the fall swapped in (`animshow` in `patch_shows.py`), held on its last frame for 120 s (`hold`: ending 0.9 s after the fall let the game's dead pose, a different one, snap in; recovery stops it with `StopAllShow`), `Eve_Dead_Common` (death face and voice) kept and no ragdoll key. Nothing in the game refers to them and they aren't in its asset registry, so UE4SS's `LoadAsset` can't find them: `P_Eve_Dead` refers to all eight (`preload`): a play-show key each, in its play-show channel but disabled (`bEnable` false) and timed at 1e9 s, so they load with it, stay loaded while it is, and it never plays them. A bare import of them, the first try, was dropped by the IoStore conversion; keys in no channel loaded them, but nothing referred to the keys and the next garbage collection took them and the falls (`show ... didn't load` a minute after the show loaded; the line now says whether the host and its first key are there); `verify.py` checks the keys. The knockdown shows and animations themselves are untouched. An animated death picks one of the nine at random (her own show's fall or one of these, `animatedPool`; off by default, so only her own falls play) and plays it over her death show through `SBBlueprintLibrary.PlaySBShowToPlayerCharacter` (`animated death: fall ...`; `PlaySingleShow` is only on the game's show interface, which Lua can't call, and silently did nothing). CUE4Parse can't decode the game's animation codec, so their end poses aren't checked offline: at 3 s and 5 s the log gives her head's height over the floor and flags a fall that doesn't end lying (`NOT LYING, drop this fall`). `meshtool anims` reads the lengths.
A cut on that fall switches it to the ragdoll first when its rule says so (`gore.legRagdoll`, `headRagdoll` on, `armRagdoll` off; `on_need_ragdoll` from `gore.lua`'s limb cut): a headless body playing the fall on read as no ragdoll at all. `active.push` scales the death's own push (`impact` on the upper body and the whole-body `launch`), and the launch clamp's limit with it.


`ue4ss/StellarBladeGore/Scripts/active.lua` runs on every ragdoll (real deaths and F7), settings under `active` in `tune.lua`:

- **Masses.** Vanilla physics assets set `bOverrideMass` on the spine and jiggle bodies without a value, so each weighs the engine default of 100 kg; cape and accessory links weigh up to 80 kg. The main bodies get a human distribution (about 60 kg in all, `massScale`). Every other body is capped at `accessoryMaxKg`, and all of those hanging off one main body share `accessoryShare` (15 %) of its mass: CH_P_EVE_09's cape hangs off the upper arms (a twist body and 7 links a side) and the ponytail off the head (9 links), and at 0.8 kg a link they weighed 6.4 kg on a 1.7 kg arm and 7.2 kg on a 4.5 kg head and pulled both out of their joints. Every body gets a mass override, accessories too: the engine ignores the mass scale of a body authored with its own mass (`bOverrideMass`), so scaled, CH_P_EVE_30's breasts stayed 100 kg each on a 10 kg chest and CH_P_EVE_29's skirt 1400 kg on 2 kg thighs, and those ragdolls shook, took the push slowly and never lay still (CH_P_EVE_10, whose extra bodies have no such override, was fine). The log line shows the masses before and after, what the engine reads back (`reads`), and how many hang off each body. A split's copy gets the same masses (it kept the outfit's 100 kg chest bodies). `stretch at ...` lines (0.5, 1, 2 and 3 s) show the three main joints furthest off their length at the start, in cm; joints gore cut or broke on purpose (a severed limb's root, the split's spine break) are left out.
- **Complete bodies** (`tune.physfix.complete`, `completeWith`). A physics asset missing any of the 20 main bodies (CH_P_EVE_21 has no thighs: its calves are jointed straight to the spine) is swapped for a complete one, InnerSuit's by default, on the component and the mesh asset. A body-less thigh's skin stays on her hip in a ragdoll while the cut calf flies off, and the clothes on her leg stretched between them. The outfit line says `its own <asset> has no body on <bones>: gave it <path>`. While loading is held off (`may_load`: 5 s after a level load, 15 s around a respawn) and the complete asset isn't loaded yet, the outfit isn't settled: it is checked again each tick until it may load (settled without it, an outfit mod's mesh worn as the level loaded, Lemi21's Latex Nun with no pelvis, kept its own asset and her deaths ended the ragdoll on its first poll with no cut; the same for a borrowed asset). An override the game or an outfit system drops again while the mesh asset's own field still holds the given asset is left alone while she lives (re-made every 2-3 s, her bodies made her twitch as she ran, the same report) and put back on her at her death (`physfix.start`: `her physics asset was <x> at her death: gave her <asset>`). The complete asset is one of gore.lua's preloads (loaded and kept with the cut assets while she plays), so after the first level it is in memory even while nothing may load: a death in a level's first seconds ragdolls on it.
- **Kinematic accessories welded** (`tune.physfix.weldKinematic`). The ragdoll simulates every body below `Bip001`, but an outfit's breasts, neck pieces, arm twists and armour plates are authored kinematic (`PhysType_Kinematic`) on joints of 0.1-1 deg with soft stiffness, to ride their bone while she lives. Simulated, those light bodies fought their stiff joints and she buzzed and spun on every outfit but CH_P_EVE_10, whose only extra bodies are its simulated hair and the hips. `physfix` locks those joints solid in every outfit's physics asset when she puts it on, and re-makes her bodies while she is animated (`N kinematic accessory joints locked (...)` in the outfit line), so they ride their bone as one with it; a simulated chain under one (a cape on an arm's twist bone) still swings. Keeping them kinematic instead pinned her: an immovable body jointed to her chest and thighs held her half of a split standing while the copy fell. Only a joint to the nearest body above it in her skeleton is locked, never one that skips over a limb bone (the hips are jointed to the spine past the thighs; locked, they welded her legs to her spine).
- **Momentum.** The death show stops the capsule before the ragdoll starts, so she used to drop on the spot even at a sprint. Her speed from the last third of a second carries into the fall (`momentum`, `momentumMax`).
- **Steady start.** For the first `spin.launchTime` (0.5) s every main body, the torso too, is held to her momentum plus the blow's `impact` plus `spin.launchOver` (400) cm/s, plus what a fall adds since (`launched: ...` in the log): a leg cut that switched an animated fall to the ragdoll shot her torso off at 16 m/s with 57 cm/s of momentum. And every outfit's physics asset gets 16 position and 4 velocity solver iterations on every body (the engine's 8 / 1 let joints come apart and snap back), and the head, hands and feet extra angular damping (`physfix.endsAngularDamping`, 2 / 3 / 3), since her hands and head spun at the cap for seconds after landing (`physfix.steady`; `solver 16/4 on N bodies` in the outfit line).
- **Falling.** A death in the air fell at a steady 2 m/s. From when her pelvis drops faster than `fall.start` (100) cm/s until it slows under `fall.land` (60), the bodies still on her are kept at least as fast downward as a free fall from then, up to `fall.max` (1500) cm/s (`fall: landed ...` in the log). An animated death in the air (her movement says falling, or her feet sink 25 cm in its first 1.5 s) switches to the ragdoll (`ragdoll after all (in the air)`).
- **Impact.** A push along the killing blow (the death key's own impulse, else from where the blow came: her mesh's hit or the nearest enemy, else from the hit location through the pelvis), spread over the upper body (`impact`, 200 cm/s, `impactUp`), plus a small random turn about the vertical (`twist`, 35°/s). On top her whole body is launched along the blow (`launch`, 400 cm/s, kept at least that fast along it for `launchTicks` (4) ticks, as a single push was lost at once (`launch held: ...` in the log); a `launchUp` 0.3 share of it upward so her feet leave the floor; bodies already cut off keep their own throw; never on a split, whose halves fly with `splitFly`): the upper body's push alone only toppled her, she sank on the spot with her feet stuck to the floor. A death used to drop dead on the spot: the key gave no impulse and nothing else knew the direction.
- **Muscle tone.** A `PhysicalAnimationComponent` drives every main joint towards the animated pose in local space, so it shapes the fall but never holds her up. It stays at full strength for `tone.hold` (0.35 s) and fades over `tone.fade` (2.5 s) down to `tone.rest` (10 %), which stays: fully limp, she folded flat at every joint like a sack. The angular damping (`tone.damping`, 0.3 of the strength) keeps limbs from flailing. Legs get about 40 %, so the knees give first and the torso folds over them. The head, hands and feet get none: pulled towards an animated pose the floor was in the way of, they shook at 500-5000°/s while the rest of her lay still. Nor do the neck, clavicles and upper arms: with tone they flipped their spin round on 40-60% of ticks in mid-air (`buzz over the first 2 s` in the log, with `active.bisect`), without it hardly. Tone ends when she lands (`tone.landOff`). Every body's top angular speed is capped (`spin.main` 720°/s for the torso, arms and legs, `spin.ends` 360 for the head, hands, feet and accessories) and put back to the engine's 3600 when she gets up. The engine's cap alone didn't hold (her head shook at 1000-8000°/s on the floor), so every tick each main body turning faster than its cap is slowed to it (`spin.clamp`). A main body flying faster than her torso by more than `spin.linearOver` (1200 cm/s, and over `spin.linearFloor` 1500) is slowed to that every tick too (`spin.linear`; a leg cut that switched her fall to the ragdoll shot a hand off at 15000 cm/s); the log names the first (`flung: ...`) and the settle line counts them. A severed limb's root drive is dropped at once.
- **Settling.** On the floor she kept jittering (head and limbs turning at 50-80°/s for seconds): the tone pulling towards the animated pose against the floor, and accessory bodies far lighter than the main ones. Accessories are now capped at 0.8 kg. She counts as lying still when her main bodies (and a split's copy's) are all slower than `settle.speed` (15 cm/s) or none got further than `settle.drift` (4 cm) over the last `settle.window` (0.5 s); bodies that no longer simulate (a split half's gone limbs) don't count: after the joint limits went in, a body shivering in place stayed fast enough that speed alone almost never settled her. The tone lets go the moment she lies still (it pulled her against the floor and the limits), and after `settle.time` (0.5 s) of it every body is put to sleep (`settled after ...`); at least `settle.after` (1 s) in. Not with her pelvis over `settle.air` (60 cm) above what a trace straight down finds (`active.over_floor`, visibility, her actor ignored; no hit counts as unknown and settles): still at the top of a fall, or let go by something that held her, she was put to sleep and hung in the air (0.4.0 report); she is woken to fall instead (`not settled at N s: her pelvis is N cm over the floor`). An animated death show with her feet over 30 cm above the floor is the ragdoll too, whatever her movement says (killed in mid-air with it already off, the fall played up there: `death: animated show with her feet N cm over the floor`). Past `settle.most` (2.5 s) she settles once nothing drifts more than `settle.driftMost` (12 cm), however fast it shivers. Every 2 s she doesn't, `not settled at ...` names the fastest body, the one that drifted furthest and the fastest turning one. Something moving her faster than `settle.wake` wakes her, and she settles again. A split's copy gets no tone: it plays no animation, so the drives would pull it into the bind pose. Her half lets go of its tone the moment the split opens: with it, her half stood stiff on its one leg and toppled slowly like a plank while the limp copy dropped, and the drives towards her live animation kept her half shaking on the floor. Both halves turn at most `spin.splitMain` (360°/s) and `spin.splitEnds` (240), and settle past `settle.splitMost` (1.5 s) once nothing drifts more than `settle.driftMost`. While her convulsions or a struggle are still due (their `M.due`), settling stops her bodies but leaves them awake: put to sleep, a body took the velocities they set (read back in full) and never moved. They sleep once those are over.
- **Weapons.** Her sword hangs off a prop bone without a physics body, so in a ragdoll it stayed in the air where she died. Each weapon showing (mesh slots Weapon1-4) is attached to an invisible box (`PhysicsActor`, not colliding with pawns, CCD on) (its vertices' box, which `sbslice` reads off the mesh with `W<comp>,<lod>` (the asset's imported bounds are twice her sword's width and far longer than its 140 cm, and the padded bounds made a 217 cm box), at most `weaponMaxCm` long) that falls and tumbles, starting with her hand's speed plus `weaponToss` (150 cm/s), weighing `weaponKg`. A cut through an arm drops a weapon whose grip is within 30 cm of that arm's hand the same way, ragdoll or not (in a death show's fall the animation kept her sword up in a hand that was gone). In a ragdoll death the weapon in her hand isn't thrown off at once: it rides her hand bone, then slips out as her grip eases (`grip`: after 0.35-0.9 s, with her hand's speed, `toss` 30 cm/s and a fifth of the spin; `her grip eased at ...` in the log). Once a weapon is out of a hand (slipped, dropped, cut, an animated death's too), the hand opens, and as her grip eases in a ragdoll death both hands do whatever they held (her sword not showing, or over 30 cm off her hand, kept the fist shut): its fingers have no bodies and kept the animation's fist, so sbslice's `F<comp>,<hand bone>,<permille>` (sent every tick, no answer file) poses each bone under the hand that share of the way back to its bind pose, against the hand as posed, through its inverse bind matrix (`invBind * Open * Posed^-1`, as the sinks do; kept and put back by `F` alone or `r`), on her mesh, its outfit parts and a split's copy, easing to `grip.open` (0.8) over `grip.openTime` (0.6 s). When the killing blade's plane crosses her sword within `weaponCut.reach` (80 cm) of the hit, away from its very ends (a blow she blocked with it; the plane is the one at the blow on her pose then, carried with `Bip001-R-Hand` to where the hand is at the death, or at the hit's release for an early cut: judged on the death pose, the death show had swung the sword out of it), the sword is cut in two: a second component of its mesh on its pose takes the far half (sbslice's `e<sword>,<copy>,<lod>`: each half keeps its side through its own skin weights; a part the plane crosses is pressed flat onto the plane as the cut face, slid onto it along the sword's length (its bind box's longest side, as the part lies now) so its far side lands on its own cross-section (pressed straight along the plane's normal, a plane tilted across the sword laid the blade's whole far side on it squashed and slanted, a small bent copy of the sword), through a sink shared by the crossed parts that moved off their bind pose alike (near the guard the plane crosses up to 11 parts, more than the free bones, and the parts past the fifth had collapsed to a point and stretched their faces over the cut), the parts past it collapse to a point there; the sinks are bones no vertex uses, 13 of her sword's 54, borrowed into the sections), and each half falls on its own box sized to it from the crossing to its end of the vertices' box, across the sword as wide and thick as what that half keeps (`abox` / `bbox` in the split's answer; on the whole sword's guard-wide section the tip lay floating over the floor), stopping `weaponCut.gap` (1.5 cm) short of the break, pushed apart along the plane at `weaponCut.apart` (60 cm/s; boxes meeting or overlapping at the break, at 120, threw the tip off fast) (`weapon cut: ... Slid that way, the far side's wider parts (the blade widening off the break, the guard) landed outside the thin cut face as fins out to both sides of its edge, so each flat sink is also squeezed in the plane, about the cut face's middle, along the blade's width and thickness (its bind box's other two axes as the part lies now) until that half's flat vertices of its group fit the face's extent (where the plane crosses the triangles, read from the LOD's index buffer; `squeezed=` in the answer). The far side's other parts collapse to the cut face's middle, not the blow's point, which lies on the blade's surface or off it: the faces between the flattened and the collapsed vertices fanned out from there as thin strips. split at ...`). A blow through her guard always breaks it. The game keeps her guard native (no property or function shows it, and her stance read `P_Eve_Default` all through a block), so every tick while she lives the guard counts as up when the guard key is held (her controller's action mappings named Guard or Block, read once and logged as `guard: keys ...`; the game's own input reads none, so `weaponCut.guardKeys`, E and L1 by default), when a montage of hers is named Guard or Block, or when her stance is a block stance (`ASBCharacter.GetCurrentStanceName`: `P_Eve_BlockSword`, a `..._Guard1` skill). A killing blow within `weaponCut.blockWindow` (0.5 s) of it cuts the sword whatever the plane did, always on its bare blade (its own crossing landed at 0.22 and ran almost along the sword: a lengthwise slice, a sliver beside the whole sword; any plane within `weaponCut.across` (0.6, ~37 deg) of the sword's length is broken there too) (`breakAt`, 0.55-0.68 of the vertices' length from the pommel; at 0.3 it broke through the open frame over the grip and both halves looked hilted, at 0.40-0.45, where the frame ends, the blade came off whole, tip and all, and lay beside the hilt as a whole sword (`native/sbslice/test/real/run.sh` with `WEAPON=1 WAT=<t> PKG=.../CH_W_Sword_01` shows the halves), and on the imported bounds it fell by the hilt and the blade stayed whole) nearest the hit, the plane mostly along the sword and leaning the way the blade's lay (`(she blocked: broken across its blade)`, `(plane along it: ...)`); any blow crossing it outside `breakAt` within `reach` is broken there the same way (`(crossed it off its blade: ...)`), with no blade seen too. The death logs her stance (`death: her stance at the blow ..., guard up (...)`), and with `dev` each stance she takes is logged once (`stance: ...`) and each change of what holds her guard up (`guard: up (key ...)`). Repeated weapon-cut callbacks identify already split components by their native address, since UE4SS can return different Lua wrappers for the same component (`sim_smash_lifecycle`). A broken or dropped sword stays as the blow left it when the ragdoll starts again in the same death (an animated fall turning ragdoll for a split): the restart had mended it and put it back in her hand. Judged by the plane alone, a blow she blocked mostly missed the sword. Recovery puts each back on its socket, a split sword whole again (`dropWeapon` false turns it all off).

- **F7.** A repeatable fall to tune all of this on, pushed two ticks after physics starts (pushed the same tick, most of it was lost: her pelvis read 0 cm/s right after, her head 100 instead of 900; `F7: pushed ...` logs the pelvis speed before and after). Her whole body gets `push` (200 cm/s, every body the same velocity change, so her centre of mass goes), and on top her upper body is shoved `impulse` (400 cm/s) backwards from her own facing, spread over the chest, neck, head, arms and pelvis, and her feet (calves less) are swept the other way at `legKick` (250), so she goes over onto her back (`direction`, `relativeTo` "camera" for the camera's view, `up`). It was one 1200 cm/s push on Spine1 away from the camera, which yanked that body out of the rest. The F7 ragdoll gets everything above except momentum and the death's impact.

- **Convulsions** (`convulse.lua`, `tune.convulse`, `convulse.enabled` in settings). Parked in 0.4.1, back in F3 in 0.5.0 as Death Struggle, on by default: with `deathMoves` false (settings.lua's or F3's, over `tune.lua`'s) `swing_on` says no, so only her face, voice and neck move (the neck's pulses add each tick's change of speed, as before: `steer` false); the pushes never visibly moved her in game. Each gasp of her face (and her last gasp) in a ragdoll death jerks her body instead (`jolt`, `tune.convulse.jolt`): a short pulse, each body's upward speed steered along a half sine over `rise` (0.14 s; its own speed read and only the vertical set, so gravity and the floor don't eat it), lifting the chest `lift` (1.2 cm, times strength and the face's fade; the last gasp `last` 1.3 times), the spine, neck, clavicles less and a little later, the head 0.3 and 0.05 s behind, the upper arms and hips barely, then let go to fall back on their own (one push up on every body at once threw her up stiff, a hop: playtest); `, her body jerking N cm up over T s` on the gasp's log line. With `body` off (the default) her body lies still and only her face moves (`face_event`), over a shorter run (`faceAgonal` 2.5 s, `faceDuration` 5 s; 8 ran on too long): for `faceAgonal` s winces (brows knit, eyes squeezed, nose wrinkled, lips stretched) with a moan, gasps (jaw dropping, nostrils flaring, eyes flicking open) with a breath, a swallow or 2-3 sobs, 1-3 tics on one side (a mouth corner, an eye, the nose, the cheek, the upper lip, an outer brow), the jaw trembling or the lip quivering, the eyes rolling up, eyelid flutters; then a last gasp and smaller tics, flickers, sighs and quivers till `faceDuration`. A gasp, a wince (its moan) or sobs (a breath) play only when their sound does, ignoring `moanChance`; when it can't (one within `voice.gap`, the voice off) a silent movement comes instead, so her mouth never opens on nothing. Each is a pose, a set of morph target deltas (`POSES`, JALI names) with a sine envelope, that `convulse.face()` sums by name for main.lua's death face to add over what it holds. A pose may be a list of takes (`wince`: open, teeth bared, eyes screwed shut, one side worse; `moan`: open, a whimper through closed lips, a groan; `gasp`: open, through the teeth, rounded with the eyes rolling up), one picked per use, and every delta is scaled by up to `faceJitter` (0.3) either way; the ones dropping her mouth (`jaw_open`, `lowerLip_down`, `wholeMouth_down`) go `faceOpen` (1.8) times as far (at 1 a gasp dropped her chin 1.4 cm of the morph's 2.5). With `body` on, as follows. Once she lies still (the ragdoll settled, at most `startMax` (2 s) in, or her animated fall's pelvis and head both still, at most `animStartMax` (6 s) in; the first spasm always moans) her muscles go on firing for `duration` (8 s, before the death screen about 10 s in). For `agonal` (4 s), fading over `fade`: bursts of 2-5 jerks of one leg (knee kicked straight, foot pointed, hip) or, with `arms` on, arm (elbow curling, wrist, shoulder), her back arching with the head thrown back, head jerks, and gasps at least `gaspGap` (1.5 s) apart (chest, shoulders up, head back, the jaw on the death face). Then a last gasp, and only small twitches of a foot, a calf or the head (a hand or forearm too with `arms`), further apart as it goes on. Her arms stay out of it by default (`arms` false: their jerks looked like the ragdoll glitching). Her voice comes with it, quiet (`voice`): a breath on each gasp and the last one (`vo_eve_cinematic_breath_*`, `breathVolume` 0.25), a moan on about half the spasms (`vo_eve_cinematic_dmg_*` and `_endure_*`, `moanVolume` 0.15, `moanChance`), at least `gap` (1.2 s) apart, never over her death cry and never once she is beheaded or split down her length (`still`; an accepted vertical split now stops the entire convulsion state, including pending body, neck, face and voice work); the recordings are played on her head and named on the movement's log line. Each movement is a muscle: a bone turned against its parent at up to `jerk` (260°/s) times `strength`, rising and falling over 0.1-0.2 s (a gasp 0.5), the parent turned back by the child's share of their masses (at most 0.6) so she moves herself. The axis is the bone's own X where the movement runs about it (Bip001 limbs bend about X: elbows forward, knees back, spine and head back, foot down, checked on CH_P_EVE_09's bind pose), else square to the bone and the way it should go. On a ragdoll the bodies are woken and moved as they lie; active.lua keeps her from settling while one runs (`settleAfter` past it) and lets her settle again, logging only the first. On an animated death only her face and voice move unless `animBody` is on; then the twitching forearm, hand, calf or foot simulates for it, gravity off, held to the pose by a local physical-animation drive (`anim.weak` 40 during the twitch, `anim.strong` 600 for `anim.back` 0.3 s after), then blended back into the animation over `anim.blend` (0.15 s) and animated again; a limb cut off meanwhile keeps simulating. Her back and head stay as the fall left them there. The death face's eyelids flutter open a little, the jaw drops on a gasp, brows and mouth tense with the spasms (`face`). A cut-off arm or leg twitches within itself; a piece's root never turns. When the head is severed or smashed, pending or running struggle stops; queued convulsions, face poses and voice events are replaced by slight, silent leg twitches. Her neck struggles too (`neck`, also with `body` off), as a share `neck.chance` of the movements (later ones half as often and weaker), never the first: her head strains `front` (lifting off the floor), aside or back for `neck.strainTime` s at up to `neck.strain` deg/s, a tremor of `neck.tremor` deg/s at `neck.hz` riding it (half of it crossways, out of step), then gives out and jerks 2-4 times the same way at `neck.jerk`, each weaker; the `strain` face pose (neck cords, `grimace_neck`, clenched jaw) goes with the strain and each jerk. A pulse may carry a tremor (`trem`, `hz`), weighted by the root of its sine envelope so the speed never steps. Each tick a pulse steers its bone's turn against its parent, along its axis, to the speed it asks for then (closed loop, pulses on one bone in a tick adding up), changing it by at most `accel` (6000 deg/s²) a second; the parent is pushed back for the change asked alone (a share of it by mass). Adding only each tick's change of speed, gravity on a lifted arm (about 1500 deg/s²) took it whole. A turn (`turn`) carries the bodies below the bone down the limb (`DOWN`, up to a cut): the same spin, each centre of mass moved round the bone's joint (a forearm and hand left standing outweigh the upper arm about the shoulder and held it still). On an animated death the head is a chain of its own (`CHAIN`), simulating for the struggle like a twitching limb. Nothing moves while the game or photo mode is paused. The log has `convulse: from N s, ...`, a line a movement (`log`), and `over after ...` or `stopped after ...`.
- **Struggle** (`struggle.lua`, `tune.struggle`). Parked in 0.4.1; 0.5.0 brought it back, on by default: `start` does nothing with `deathMoves` false (settings.lua's `deathMoves`, `struggle.strength`, `struggle.length`, `struggle.rise.alone`, F3 → Death → Death Struggle, Struggle Strength, Struggle Length, Sit Up Uncut). A ragdoll death, once she has come to rest (settled, at most `startMax` 0.8 s in: at 2.5 it came far too late): `pick` reads what the cuts left (`active.severed` roots; a limb counts only when none of its bones was cut and they all still simulate). A waist cut (one torso bone cut, `Spine`, `Spine1` or `Spine2`: gore.lua's torso cut breaks `Spine1`) is a plan of its own (`waist`): the upper body from that bone up struggles, its legs left with the lower half (none), the waist its first stump (clutched with both hands where the cut runs, 0.3 up the bone), the roll on the torso bodies from the cut bone up, her frame up from the cut bone to the neck, no `rise`, `prop` or `push` (they need her hips), and none with no arm left. Nothing on a cut through her neck or head, a second cut through her body, or a split (`gore.split_on`, including accepted pending splits and interruption of an existing plan). With `animated` on (off by default) an animated death waits for its fall to rest (convulse.lua's `resting`: its convulsions began), decides there (the sit-up-only roll made then, kept), and if there is a plan goes limp as she lies (`main.ragdoll_quiet`: active.lua's `quiet` start, no blow, no momentum, no tone, which would pull her towards the standing pose her animation goes on to), then picks the plan `quietWait` (0.4 s) into it (before, every limb read as off her: none of her bodies simulated); `animated` false leaves those as they are; with no limb cut, `rise.alone` (0.5) of deaths get a plan of only sit-up and prop tries (`alone`), the rest nothing. It runs `time` times `length` (5-8 s: 8-12 dragged on, playtest; the acts' lengths cut by about a third with it) on a bleed-out curve (`vitality`): 1 + `bleed.surge` falling to 1 over the first `bleed.frantic` share, down to `bleed.low` by `bleed.last`, then to nothing; times `strength` and a 0.5 s rise, it is every act's strength, and its raw value sets the pace (0.5-1, the surge left out: act lengths, crawl strokes, pushes and leg clocks divide by it). Its speeds were halved after a playtest found it frantic (`speed` 160, `accel` 2500, `gain` 5, `surge` 0.1, shakes and reflexes about half, slower breaths, strokes and leg draws). At `bleed.last` she dies (`die_start`): 2-4 tremor pulses in the hands and feet still on her within 0.9 s, a last deep breath ending where her chest stands still, and a 0.6-0.8 s turn of the head about her length 0.4-0.8 s in, gravity taking it on. Her frame comes from what is still on her (`frame`: a pair of upper arms or thighs, else one side's offset from her middle, doubled), and `roll_of` says how far round she lies about her length (0 on her back, +90 right side down, 180 face down). A stump's place: a shoulder or hip mirrors the other side's across her, an elbow, wrist, knee or ankle sits at the far end of the bone it was cut from, along that body's centre of mass, as long as the other side's. Acts (`ACT`, each `start` / `tick` / `done`, `choose` weighted by `weights`, the last one x0.3 except clutch, crawl and push, lengths `acts`): clutch (the hand 7 cm short of the stump plus a 5 cm slip every 1.2-2.8 s and a 7 Hz tremor of `shake` cm, a leg's stump both hands 6 cm apart; rolled `roll.side` towards the wound-up side rocking back by `roll.rock`; legs drawing to 0.45 of their length below the hip and 0.4 in front or out to 0.92 on their own clocks), reach (a hand going out to a stump in disbelief, never taking hold: a stump within `reach.within` (1.3) of the hand's arm lengths, the hand `reach.hover` (14) cm in front of it over the first 0.3, closing to 3 cm by 0.6, touching till 0.7, flinching back to 18 cm, the arm dropped over the last fifth, a 6 Hz tremor of twice `shake`; her neck turned 40 deg towards it to look, rolled 20 deg onto its side; at most twice. It reached for the cut-off limb before: not what people do), turn (on her back: an arm across her, rolled towards 175 deg; past 140 a crawl comes next, failing stops further turns), crawl (face down: each hand from beside the shoulder to 0.8 of the arm past it lifted 10 cm, planted 4 cm into the floor, dragged back at 1.3x, the arms half a stroke apart, the elbow out and up; legs scrabbling; a hand planted and pulling back drags her, `drag` below), rise and prop (on her back, `rise.tries` 1-3 a struggle, not below `rise.least`: Spine1, Spine2 and the neck to 20, 40 and 60 deg x `curl` (prop x0.8) forward of her length as it lay at the start, fixed in the world, shaking by `rise.shake` at 8-11 Hz, for `rise.time` then let go over `rise.give`, at `power` x strength x 0.75 per earlier try; prop sets the upper arm down to the floor beside her and the forearm along it towards her feet and rolls 25 deg onto that side; legs braced; convulse.lua's `effort` puts the strain face on with a moan), push (no arm: on her back heels between 0.4 and 0.95 of the leg below the hips, lifted 8 cm drawing up, `push.press` cm down driving out, `push.bend` of a 2.0-2.8 s push drawing up; face down scrabbling and rolling to 70-110 deg now and then; on her side onto her back first; a heel down and driving, or a scrabbling foot down and shoving back, drags her too), curl (knees drawn up 0.55 in front, hands before her face, rolled 60 deg to a side rocking 15, her back rounded 20 deg), lie. `solve` (exported for `test/run.sh fold`) puts a point into the two bones' directions with `ik`, in the plane the elbow or knee bends in (`hinge`: the forearm's or calf's own X, square to it), and sends the forearm or calf at the bend the solve asks for (at most `FOLD`, 145 / 140) about that hinge from where the upper bone points now. Elbows and knees fold one way only, positive about the lower bone's own X on both sides (CH_P_EVE_01's bind pose: X x the bone's length points the way physfix's 145 / 140 range goes; the other way stops at 3 deg), so the middle joint goes off the line to the target on the side `cross(u, x)` (u: the root to the target), whatever pole the act gave (that pole stands in only when the hinge can't be read). The side had been the act's pole: the wrong one asked her elbows to bend backwards and the joint limit held them straight (playtest, after the hinge fix: still no bend). Before that, solved in a plane of ours, the forearm's direction lay mostly off the hinge. `fold` brings any lower bone direction into the hinge's range (a fold the wrong way straight, or folded fully when nearer that; prop's forearm). The hand is held in line with the forearm while an arm moves (`wrist` x strength, its direction to `Finger2`): nothing held the wrists and her hands flopped about. Her strokes only turn her limbs against her own body, and a hand on the floor has too little weight on it to grip, so they swept the air and she stayed put (playtest): `drag` gives the floor's grip, speeding her torso's bodies (the upper body's after a waist cut) along the floor towards her head, up to `drag.speed` (35) cm/s x strength at `drag.accel` (250), never slowing them, while a stroke presses a planted hand or foot (`planted`: no further off the floor than her chest's or pelvis's bone plus 4-6 cm, the floor's way in her frame); the crawl and push lines say for how long (`pulling for` / `driving for`). Each act's log line adds `(elbows and knees N)`, their own angle off, and `servo` steers each bone's turn against its parent (its twist left alone) towards `gain` x the angle off plus a held term (`hold` x angle x s summed while it is over 2 deg, at most `holdMax`, decaying over 0.3 s once there: gravity, the floor and damping take a steady share of every turn, and the first playtest's 5 / 220 deg/s / 3000 / 27 % a step barely moved her), at most `speed` deg/s x strength, the whole gap each step (`response` 0; above 0, `1 - exp(-dt/response)` of it), capped at `accel` x strength x dt, by turns added to its angular velocity (`SetPhysicsAngularVelocityInDegrees` adding: `AddAngularImpulseInDegrees` as a velocity change did nothing in game) with its centre of mass moved round the joint by the same turn (`w x (com - joint)` added to its linear velocity: turned about its middle alone, the joint dragged it back and nothing was kept), the bodies below it down the limb turned with it about the same joint (`DOWN`, up to a cut: the forearm and hand left standing held the upper arm still), and minus its mass share (at most 0.6) on the parent alone, so she moves herself. A velocity servo, not a spring: a speed asked for can't run away, and contacts and joint limits still win. `roll` steers the torso's four bodies' turn about her length the same way (`roll.gain`, `roll.speed`, `roll.accel`). Pulses (`pulse`, a sine of speed over its time, a tremor riding it, each step steering the bone's turn against its parent to that speed, at most `accel`; the breathing alone adds each step's change, the rise's servo on the same spine owning the rest) carry the breathing (Spine2 against Spine1 about her right axis, `breath.depth` deg at full strength down to a third, every `breath.fast` to `breath.slow` s), the stump reflexes (the stub, the bone a cut root hung from, 1-2 jerks of `reflex.speed` across it with a 10-14 Hz `reflex.quiver`, every 1.2-2.8 s over strength) and the dying tremors. Each act's log line ends with how her joints followed: the angle off on average, and the share of the turn a step set that is still there the next (little kept: something else holds the joint; much kept and still short: too weak), the share read back right as it was set (its first 24 steered steps: low says the engine never took it, high with nothing kept a step later says something undid it), and what active.lua's upkeep did meanwhile (`active.writes`: every turn zeroed by settling, spins capped, flung bodies slowed). Steered at most `rate` (60) times a second, each step reading each bone's place and turn once (`seen`, `spun`). Every step (an alone plan: only during a try) it asks active.lua to keep her awake (`twitch`) and wakes all bodies every 0.5 s; she settles again after. convulse.lua leaves her limbs alone meanwhile (`busy`: no arm swings or body events) and its neck struggle waits through a try (`rising`). Paused or in photo mode nothing moves. The log has the plan as it starts, an act a line and its result (`log`), `dying`, `her last breath`, and `over after ...` (the acts, the roll's range, reflexes, leg draws, chest height, how far she moved), or `none: ...`.

Recovery removes the component, puts the masses back and the weapons on their sockets before gore's cleanup recreates the bodies.

Everything the script does runs from her Blueprint's `ReceiveTick` (game thread). That hook is put on once, from the player controller's `ClientRestart` (game thread, as the level's player starts); it used to be put on from an async loop (`FindFirstOf` over every object, then `RegisterHook`, off the game thread while the save loaded), and now and then a launch crashed a second or two after it (null + 0x88). The loop is left only for a script reloaded mid-game, from 20 s after load, with a name lookup alone.

A death reloads the level, and the old Eve is freed. The script used to keep her pawn and ask UE4SS whether it was still valid, which reads the freed object: the game crashed on the new Eve's first tick. It now follows the actor whose `ReceiveTick` runs it; a new one is taken once the old one has been silent for a second, and everything held from the old one is dropped without being touched (`new Eve ...` in the log).

An object held past a tick (the characters seen, the enemies the swing tracking follows and their weapons, photo mode's widgets, the knockdown checks, an enemy's own objects) is kept as its address (`goreCtx.keep`) and checked before it is touched (`goreCtx.held`): `IsValid` on a freed object reads it and crashed UE4SS. 0.3.0 checked it by its path in the name table, but UE4SS's `StaticFindObject` finds no level actor by its path (`.../F02.F02:PersistentLevel.Name`), so no enemy was ever held and every death rolled its cut; a lookup by name under the level crashed the game at launch. `sbslice.dll`'s `G<object>,<index>,...` answers `ok` and a 1 or 0 for each: whether its item in `GUObjectArray` (found by index, chunks of 64Ki items of 0x18 bytes) still points at it and isn't flagged Unreachable or PendingKill, never reading the object. Every held object asked in the last 3 s goes in one call on the tick's first check (`goreCtx.frame`), the answers kept for the tick. The index is read off the object (`ffffffff` asks for it, answered `<bit>:<index>`) at its first check, within `keepFresh` (2 s) of `keep()`, which runs as a watched object is handed out too (characters as they spawn, photo mode's widgets: `newobj.lua`); a character first checked later (a level loading, no tick) is dropped and the characters are found again with `FindAllOf` (at most every 5 s). Nothing is held before `sbslice.dll` is up. `test/run.sh alive` checks `G` on a made-up object array. The first misses are logged (`held: <address> gone`).

## Her voice

The existing beheading cutoff (`vox.cut`, `voice.cutFade`, default 0.08 s with the Sin fade curve) also applies to head and upper-body **smashes**, at the same cut-opening stage. There is no new delay. Ordinary torso/limb cuts and limb smashes leave the cry playing. If a head/upper-body smash completes before `voice_death` starts the cry, that function checks the mesh’s smashed cut records and invokes the same cutoff immediately after spawning it; this guard does not apply to ordinary cuts. `sim_smash_voice` tests both event orders, the unchanged beheading fade, intact recovery, and real head/torso death broadcasts.

The moment she dies (a real death or a test key's ragdoll) she cries out once: one of Eve's own death cries, `vo_eve_die_01`-`05` (the SoundWaves the game's cues pick from, under `Sound/Dialogue/ActionVoice/Eve`, localized like the rest of her voice), at her head with her action voice's attenuation, pitch 0.97-1.03. Nothing follows it: the screams on later cuts, moans and last breath of the first version played on over her body and are gone. A recording rests `rest` (8) s before it plays again. `tune.gore.voice`: `enabled` (false: only the game's own death voice), `volume`, `sounds` (the recordings to pick from instead: names under that folder, or full object paths of any SoundWave or SoundCue, so another mod's new recordings can be used; a mod that replaces `vo_eve_die_*` in place, as Violent Death Mod does, is heard with no setting); the log line is `voice: <recording> at <volume>`.

## Hits she lives through

A hit she lives through leaves a slash of blood on her skin, no cut (`tune.gore.hitMarks`; parked for now: `enabled` is false and the switch is commented out of `settings.default.lua`, so none of this runs, Alt+F10 included). The hit her mesh reports (`mesh hit` in the log) is marked `delay` (0.25) s later if she is still alive, so a killing blow's hit gets the death cut alone, and with `needDamage` only when her HP bar (the HUD's `ProgressBar_HP`, see Glass cannon) dropped, so a guarded or parried hit leaves nothing. The physics only says where its capsule was hit, so the DLL finds the skin (sbslice `m`): a ray from the axis of the part nearest the hit (neck, spine, upper arms, forearms, thighs, calves) out through the hit point, the first skin it leaves through within 30 cm (not an arm beside her body further out), then the outermost layer within 3 cm of that (a strap or a plate), and the skin's normal there. The mark is a deferred decal (the Raven cinematic's floor blood, `MI_FloorBloodDecal_5`, in an instance of its own shaped by the game's sword scar `T_A_SwordScars_01` with no normal relief, `slash`: the puddle's own lumpy shape and normals read as a dent) attached to the bone that weighs most on that skin, so it moves with her. It projects into the skin along that normal and is stretched `length` (14 cm) by `width` (4, the streak fills about a third of it) along the swing: the nearest enemy's fastest weapon bone's motion over the last 0.3 s, else the hit's push. It's 75-125 % of that size at random, its length kept within 1.3 times the part's radius so it stays on a forearm's curve, and its box reaches only `depth` (0.5) of the part's radius into her, so it never shows on the far side. At most `max` (12) at once, the oldest goes first; `blood` adds the hit's blood burst on it. Marks heal: each remembers her HP bar before its hit, and once healing has lifted the bar from its lowest since then back to that reading, the mark fades over `healFade` (2.5) s and goes (a mark from before the HUD's bar was found waits for a full bar). All of them go when she recovers or respawns. F10 puts a mark on a part at random, on its side facing the camera. The log says `hit mark on <part> (rides <bone>): L x W cm at ..., N cm out of the bone, N cm off the reported hit`, `hit mark healed: ...` and `hit mark skipped: <why>`. `test/run.sh mark` checks the skin query on CH_P_EVE_09, 10 and 30, plain and posed: rays four ways out of the torso, an upper arm and a thigh must meet the skin 3-20 cm out, on a vertex that part carries, with the normal along the ray.

## Blood and dirt on her

Experimental, off by default, available under F3 → Blood and Dirt (`stains.enabled`). The system only drives the game's existing skin layer. Eve's Blueprint keeps `BloodValue` and `DirtValue`, which feed `BloodOffset` and `DirtOffset` in `MPC_PCDirtyOffset`. Her skin materials read that collection through `MF_SkinBloodFX`; clothing has no equivalent native layer. The mod writes those native values and leaves materials, textures, masks, tiling, colours and outfit slots alone. The old custom wounds, drops, render targets, skin/outfit painting and outfit dirt tinting have been removed; old settings for those paths have no effect. Appearance follows each outfit's existing skin shaders, with no painted fallback.

`stains.lua` accumulates target blood/dirt in 0–1, then moves the visible native offsets toward them at `rise` (0.6/s) or `clearRate` (0.35/s). Updates run at most every 0.1 s, with elapsed time capped at 0.25 s to avoid a catch-up jump after a pause. Blueprint values and collection offsets stay in agreement. The collection and native library are found in memory, cached per pawn/world, with a one-second retry when absent; no synchronous asset loads or GPU painting. The HP watcher reads the already-discovered HUD rather than starting its own global widget search. Nearby-hit checks inspect up to eight characters per update and rotate through the list; collection writes are skipped while the applied values and Blueprint fields are unchanged. A stained, seated Eve checks the camp sequence immediately and then at most every five seconds until clean. Slow updates log timings for HP, nearby hits, movement/rest and writes separately. The 2026-10-31 log showed stains updates of 100 and 44 ms, but did not identify the individual operation, so in-game profiling is needed to confirm which work caused them.

HP loss adds `hit.base` (0.04 once per blow) plus `hit.perHp` (0.9) times the fraction lost, multiplied by `scale`. Enemy physical-hit reactions inside `splash.range` (now 1000 cm, previously 350 cm) add `splash.amount` (0.06), linearly reduced with distance; enemy cuts call `splash` with `splash.cut` (0.12). Hit polling compares both location and force/shake state so another blow at the same point counts, while an unchanged hit counts once. First observations establish a baseline. Hard landings and knockdowns add native dirt; after 1.5 s dead, dirt can accumulate up to `dirt.death`, except underwater. A cut/smash or a test ragdoll adds no independent skin-soaking impulse; actual HP loss still adds blood.

With `heal.enabled`, recovering a share of missing HP removes that share of the remaining target blood and dirt. Healing to full targets zero, including regeneration steps below the per-update threshold, and visible coverage fades out gradually. Swimming (native movement mode 4) reduces both targets by `wash.rate` (0.35/s), even while dead. Camp rest targets clean; `fade` optionally clears stains over a number of minutes (0: no passive fading). A new pawn or next life starts clean. Turning the feature off clears its own native values once, then releases ownership; it never continually zeros the collection while off.

**Flash fix.** The old `gore.bodyBlood` cut-only ramp competed with stains over the same collection and could restore stale values on cleanup/reload. It now defaults off. Even if explicitly enabled in old player settings, `blood_up`, `blood_tick`, `blood_down` and the pending reload restore cannot write over Blood and Dirt while `stains.enabled` is true. This gives the enabled system one owner of the native skin offsets, with no per-cut saturation pulse. Particle blood, stump materials and floor pools retain their own handling.

The smash stress simulator fixes random scatter for its falling-pelvis check, so it verifies the exact 40 cm source movement rather than comparing two random chunk offsets.

`bash ue4ss/test/run.sh stains` checks native offsets and Blueprint agreement; gradual damage buildup, partial/full healing and tiny regeneration steps; complete water cleanup alive/dead; 8 m enemy hits, range rejection and same-location repeated hits; native dirt; camp/next-life cleaning; no render targets, wound queries or material replacements even with old painting options enabled; all six arm/head/torso cut/smash cases and recovery with an explicitly enabled competing legacy ramp; disabled ownership and freed-world safety.

## F3 layout

Grouped Layout (`menuGrouped`, on by default, F3 → Mod) puts blunt smash and its test toggle beside the Gore master, the death-screen delay beside its mode, and enemy cut chance beside Enemy Gore. It adds a tab name and row counter; switching it off immediately restores the previous order and hides those additions. Row values, key bindings and tabs are unchanged. A parent-off dependency dims its child labels and values without disabling navigation or editing; nested and inverse dependencies (for example, the chance used only when forced ragdoll is off) are checked explicitly, not inferred from indentation. The grouping is reversible while the menu is open and settings remain intact. `sim_menu_layout` checks both orders, row identity, selection and dependencies; the actual UMG layout still requires in-game visual inspection.

## Blunt smash

Developer Keys: F10 (`keys.smash`, rebindable) starts a test ragdoll and smashes a random remaining part using limb/head/torso death odds; F8 restores her. It works with the automatic smash options off. Hit Mark now defaults to Alt+F10.

Experimental, on by default (`gore.smash.enabled`, F3 → Blunt Smash); Smash Every Death (`smash.always`) stays off by default. A blunt killing blow replaces the normal death cut with a smashed arm, leg, head or upper body; Death Cuts and Death Cut Chance still apply. Blunt Kills Only Ragdoll takes priority, and falls and laser deaths keep their own handling. `smash.always` forces the smash judgement for testing; with no blow, the kind is rolled on the limb/head/torso death weights, excluding split. Parts absent from the skeleton or already smashed cannot be chosen; zero-weight kinds are never chosen, including at RNG zero. Each killing blow chooses one impact-based part (or one rolled part without a hit), so it cannot smash both legs simultaneously. Separate test smashes can remove different parts sequentially; overlapping chunk bursts are supported. Ordinary post-death hits follow their existing cut/mark logic, except blunt ones smash: a blunt melee follow-up tries `gore.smash` before the blade cut, so a second hammer blow after the upper body went takes the lower body instead of blood-only. When the upper body is already smashed, a blunt leg hit explodes the lower-body remainder as full-body (pelvis and legs hidden, both thighs' flesh added to the burst).

`sw.blunt_of` checks the killing enemy's weapon meshes, weapon bone, then enemy name/class against `smash.sharp` and `smash.blunt`.
