Sharp matches win within a name and across weapon slots, independent of mesh order. The existing `enemies.kinds` and `bosses.kinds` definitions resolve opaque Blueprint names to their aliases (for example `CH_M_NA_16_Blueprint_C_7` → `M_SkullHammer`); unknown enemies still use the configured name lists and unarmed/stomp fallback, except the clawed/sickle species in `smash.species` (CH_M_NA_13, CH_M_NA_42, CH_M_NA_26 marionette), whose weaponless swipes always cut. Hair whips (`LeftHandHair`, Marionette) are sharp and never match the bare-hand `hand` substring.

Flight guards: an animated split's per-body opening speeds are capped at `gore.splitVelMax` (a mid-cut socket read gave 93-210 m/s and the halves ended 495 m off at the world origin); no main body ever exceeds `spin.linearMax` on any tick, torso included, since the torso-relative cap is blind to the whole body flying together; past `spin.leash` sideways from where she went limp, or above it, a half stops and the log says which half and how far (falling down stays free; a split's copy took off upward, playtest).

Poll cost: the nearby-character list and its engine validation (`live_chars`, 100-700 ms per poll in a hub) are cached up to `gore.charsRefresh`, instead of rewalked every poll window; positions still refresh through `char_at`'s own clock cache, every consumer guards dying and vanishing actors itself, fresh spawns set a dirty flag for an early rebuild, and the seed backfill still waits for a quiet poll. The cut-asset preload likewise waits while polls arrive late (one async ask took 553 ms and popped the lighting once on resume), and a slow async ask logs its asset. Another enemy's cached weapon is ignored. A torso smash waits for death rather than cutting the body at the early lethal hit; deferred grab hits retain their original location. Temporary smash/bone flags are cleared even if a cut throws, and a failed cut records no phantom piece.

The normal cut builds the surviving stump. Its missing side gets no cap or bone stub, no throw/float callbacks, no gradual-opening constraint and no newly enabled piece/body collision. `M.smashed.out` reads the piece's centre before `HideBoneByName` collapses its transforms, hides its bones with PBO_None (never PBO_Term), and hides a head's hair and socket attachments. The surviving stump still bleeds: smashed arm/leg openings use `smash.limbBleedScale` (0.8) of the normal size; head and torso keep the normal size. Smash bleed duration uses `smash.bleedTimeScale` (0.84, normally about 2.1 seconds); an indefinite bleed remains indefinite. Ordinary cuts are unchanged. Descendants of a smashed part count as gone; photo-mode and settling lists exclude the smashed piece. Recovery restores bones and visibility and destroys chunks, retained effects and decals; a world reload drops old references without querying freed objects.

Default whole-part counts are forearm/hand 6, whole arm 10, calf/foot 10, whole leg 16, head 8 and upper body 44. `M.smashed.profile` snapshots remaining flesh before inserting the new cut record: upper arms contribute 4 plus their forearm’s 6, thighs 6 plus their calf’s 10, and the upper body 8 plus both arms and head. A prior severed or smashed child subtree is excluded, including a forearm still attached to an already severed arm. The upper-body count uses the 36-unit intact flesh weight as its denominator, so increasing its meat count does not change blood size or missing-child deductions. Counts remain configurable (`chunks`, `chunksForearm`, `chunksLeg`, `chunksCalf`, `chunksHead`, `chunksBody`), bounded to 0–64. Default chunk diameters are 7–14 cm, multiplied by 0.9 for forearms, 1.05 for calves, 1.15 for whole legs and 1.25 for the upper body; final diameters remain bounded to 100 cm.

The game's `SM_meat_01`–`04` meshes fly from that centre, two per gore tick, at about `smash.speed`. Each rides an invisible box whose half-extents are 85% of the scaled mesh bounds along each axis (minimum 0.5 cm), CCD and angular damping 3, explicitly on PhysicsBody; this brings the visible surface closer to the floor for thin meshes. Missing bounds fall back to 0.30, 0.24 and 0.17 of the diameter; only WorldStatic and WorldDynamic are blocked, Pawn/PhysicsBody and overlaps ignored. The static meat mesh itself has no collision. Actors whose spawn, component creation or physics setup fails are destroyed immediately. Every burst keeps its own chunk list for delayed diagnostics. Chunk counts are limited to 0–64, sizes to 1–100 cm, speed to 0–3000 cm/s and mass to 0.01–10 kg; non-finite values fall back to defaults, and reversed size limits are sorted.

By default every meat material slot shares the **actual surviving seam material instance**, including its flesh colour, roughness, textures and subsequent tint changes. Procedural seams supply their material component from `cap()`; a sphere seam supplies its root component. Without a seam (caps disabled or unavailable), chunks use the same `set_cap_material` pipeline: `capBloodColor`, `capMatte` or Tachy's flesh settings, as applicable. This avoids the base meat material's orange emissive default. An explicit `smash.material` path still overrides it, and `false` keeps the source meshes' materials. Enabling Smash or changing its meshes after startup adds its assets to the existing preload budget ahead of audio.

The smash burst uses the game's scalable `NS_Hit_Flesh_Slice_BloodOnly` instead of overlapping world-space grinder GPU drops, which do not shrink with component scale. One burst is spawned at the missing part's centre with scale `min(0.825, 0.825 * sqrt(remaining flesh / 36))`; `burstScale` multiplies the flesh value (0–2), and missing children reduce it. Its source stops after two gore ticks and its component is destroyed after 180 ticks. The separate hit effect uses the same size based on remaining flesh. `burstFx` can override the effect; disabling blood suppresses both, while a missing burst leaves the hit effect. The previously built grinder clone remains in the pak but is no longer used by default; vanilla grinder effects are unchanged. Chunk blood trails and pools remain in use. Visual scale and motion require in-game verification. Chunk creation starts three gore ticks after opening, the hit effect four and the smash-centre splat five; the main burst remains at opening, spreading the work instead of spawning both effects and two chunks together. The slow-stage profiler now marks seam collapse, smash hiding, setup and burst separately. In the 17:35 playtest the first head opening logged 64 ms (51 ms unclassified Lua, 10 ms smash) and a 152 ms frame hitch; another torso run logged 16 ms in pools. Those measurements do not establish a single cause, and the effect of this scheduling change needs a new in-game log.

`M.smashed.has(mesh, bone)` distinguishes a destroyed part from an ordinary cut. Upper-body destruction gates `struggle.start`, `due`, `busy`, `rising` and every struggle tick, including acts already running when the smash lands. Convulsions discard pending muscle pulses, face poses, simulated animation chains and gasp jolts before the next physics update. After an upper-body smash no new muscle pulses are scheduled; after a head-only loss the existing silent calf/foot pulses may still run for four seconds. The shared soft-voice entry also rejects head loss/upper-body smashes, so another caller cannot restart moans after the existing death-cry cutoff. Ordinary waist cuts retain their upper-body struggle and voice.

Body, pelvis, spine and shoulder-opening pools use a base radius of 110 cm; detached limbs retain their own smaller radii, and a head cut uses the body radius on its surviving side. Floor pools use `poolScale` 0.85 and `poolGrow` 4 s by default. Opening trackers take priority over chunk trackers in the one-decal-per-pass budget, so a burst of meat cannot delay the body pool; a stationary opening starts pooling after 0.1 s. The fallback body tracker starts 0.5 s into an uncut death and still waits for the body to slow before making a pool.

During an upper-body smash, later meat batches follow the surviving pelvis downward and horizontally from the original burst position (ignoring upward bouncing and displacements over 300 cm), avoiding a prolonged stream from a fixed point in midair. For an upper-body smash, chunk launch retains horizontal scatter but caps upward speed at 5% of launch speed, including for vertical hits; the 44-piece count is unchanged. Smash meat asset lookups are spread one per tick; the first available mesh immediately starts the meat batches, while remaining varieties load alongside them. The first batch is scheduled two gore ticks after opening and the blood burst one tick after opening, retaining a fast visual response without stacking both effects onto the opening frame. Delayed steps exceeding 10 ms log their label and time, so remaining stalls can be attributed without changing their scheduling.

The first `max(1, smash.drips)` chunks track a small landing pool, capped at `smash.chunkPoolRadius` (11 cm radius, multiplied by `poolScale`); the first `smash.drips` also leave trails while moving. They share the floor tracer, decal cap and one-decal-per-pass budget, and respect the blood and pool switches. The hit burst and floor splat stay at the smash, while the hidden piece never bleeds. The lethal wound plays the blunt crunch; the opening chooses either gore impact plus blood power or gore splash plus blood, always including a blood cue even inside the ordinary sound gap, with no blade-slice cue. The thud-only pairing is excluded; individual in-game cue variants still need listening verification. Blood and sound switches remain independent of physical meat.

Run `bash ue4ss/test/run.sh smash smash_hits smash_lifecycle smash_stress smash_effects smash_material smash_key smash_voice smash_struggle`. These run shipped Lua against fake engine calls: supported bones, 10,000 weighted picks, 90 death/respawn cycles, box collision setup and simplified modelled floor/wall ballistics at 15/30/60/144 fps, overlapping bursts, cancellation/reload, injected failures, bounded tuning, effect/audio switches, seam-material reuse, per-part counts/sizes and missing-child deductions. Blood checks cover per-part and missing-child burst scaling, source shutdown without particle destruction and cleanup; movement checks cover destruction before/during struggle, normal waist cuts, detached legs, silence and recovery. These simulations do not render Niagara or run the game’s PhysX solver; appearance and physical motion still require in-game validation.

## Hits after death

An enemy still swinging at her body cuts it again (`tune.gore.afterHits`). During the death the script keeps following the nearest enemy's weapon bones (the killing swing's tracker) and checks the step its fastest weapon made each tick against her body segments in 3D: full limb segments where `SWING_AT` names both ends, bone spheres for head and torso, each with its anatomical radius plus 4 cm slack and capped by the configured `reach`/`handReach`. The blade segment is sampled along this step's travel (about every 15 cm, up to 8 samples), with endpoint and midpoint sweeps also checked against each body segment; hand/claw motion without a blade mesh is measured as a moving point against the same body segments. An overhead swing that clears these approximate volumes fails the segment test; missing sockets mean no hit. Imported weapon bounds are unchanged, and those bounds, estimated radii and capped sampling limit accuracy. A step faster than `swingMinSpeed` crossing within its part's allowance is a blow there: `cutChance` (0.75) of them cut along the plane the weapon swept, the same way a killing blow does (a limb, the head, or, while she is still whole, her body in two), the rest leave a hit mark where they passed (see Hits she lives through), all with blood. A blow that would split a body already cut takes the arms and legs its plane crosses near the hit instead (nearest first, the rest queued), else her body along the blade while it is whole, else a mark. One hit per swing (the weapon has to slow down first), at most `max` (6) a death, none in the first `from` (0.6) s, so the killing swing doesn't land twice. A split body only bleeds. The log line is `after death (N s in): <enemy>'s <bone> at N cm/s passed N cm from her <part>, hit i of n`. `sim_after` and `sim_after_nomontage` cover a direct crossing and an overhead miss; real weapons still need an in-game check.

## Death face

Eve's death face is [Proper Death Expression](https://www.nexusmods.com/stellarblade/mods/2716) by **blabmice**, used with credit as its permissions allow (details and the file's origin in `third_party/proper-death-expression/README.md`). The vanilla face is a blank stare.

- `vendor` job: the author's `EVE_Facial_ActionVoice_DIE1` facial animation (12 s) ships unchanged in place of the vanilla one, which the death voices `DIE1` / `DIE2` already play.
- `voice` job: `CSS_PC_EVE` gets `DIE3`, the same face with a breath cue at 0.0001 volume, as the author's fixes do.
- `notify` job: the falling death animation plays no death voice, so it gets a new `SBAnimNotify_CharSESound` for `DIE3` at the author's time. The author's own fix reused an existing notify, which silenced a cloth or footstep sound; here every vanilla notify stays. The author also fixed the grapple (`Eve_Dead_DownFaceUp` / `DownFaceDown`) and explosive (`Eve_Dead_Explosion`) deaths the same way; since 0.4.7 that comes from their shows instead, because [Alternative Death Animations](https://www.nexusmods.com/stellarblade/mods/3303) replaces those three animations and a whole-file copy in this pak would override its version. `show_voices` on a show job adds an `SBShowCharSESoundKey` (a copy of her laser death's `Die1` key, channel 56) playing `DIE3`: in `EveRD_Explosion1/2` at 0.0813 s, in `Eve_Dead_Down` at 0.2496 s. `Eve_Dead_Down` picks `DownFaceUp` or `DownFaceDown` by her facing on its anim keys, a check a voice key lacks, so both get the face-up time (face down, at 0.4152 s, hides her face).

`verify.py` checks the vendored package is unchanged, the new voice copies `DIE1` except for its sound, volume and start, and the falling animation has exactly one new notify with every vanilla one kept, and each listed show voice is a new key in channel 56 with its voice and time. Don't install the original mod alongside this one; it replaces the same files.

In the mod's deaths the face never stayed: `DIE1` starts as she dies (her eyelids were closing 0.2 s in), but by 1 s every curve was back at 0 and she stared with open eyes. The mod holds a dead face itself (`tune.gore.deathFace`): from `at` (0.3) s into a death it sets morph targets on her face mesh, fading them in over `fade` (0.5) s and setting them again every tick until she gets up (a master-posed face adds its own morph targets over the ones it takes from her body's curves). `morphs` lists each as its JALI name, its ARKit twin and a weight; the first name her face mesh has is used, so the eyes never close twice. An entry may have `from` and `over`: its weight as the face comes on, eased (smoothstep) to its weight over `over` s. `morphs` holds what every death wears (her eyes half open, closing over 7 s; her pupils widening); one of `moods` is added per death, picked by `weight` and never the same twice running: `agony` (brows knit, a squint, nose wrinkled, lips stretched, slackening over 4-6 s), `grit` (teeth bared and clenched, letting go late), `shock` (eyes and mouth wide, brows up), `sorrow` (brows up in the middle, mouth down, chin up) `fading` (the pain nearly gone, eyes closing in 4 s) and `dread` (one eye half shut, the other staring, brows up unevenly, the mouth twisted to one side, the jaw off to it; `mirror` turns it to the other side half the time, swapping `_L`/`_R`, `_left`/`_right` and `Left`/`Right`, and leaves out `jawSide`); a mood's entry replaces the base one for the same morph. Every weight, start weight and time is scaled by up to `jitter` (0.25) either way, each side on its own; the eyes end `lidsShut` (0.7-1) shut, drift up, down or aside by up to `gaze` (0.35), and the jaw hangs to one side by up to `jawSide` (0.25), picked per death. The convulsions' poses are added over it, morph targets it doesn't hold included (set while a pose moves them, then 0). The log names the mood and what it holds (`face (death): grit, holding N of her M morph targets`), or lists the face's morph names when none matched. Page Up shows the moods in turn on her living face, 6 s each.

## Enemies

The generic humanoid enemies get the same cuts as Eve (`enemies.lua`, `tune.enemies`): the skulls (sword, spear, hammer, gunner), the lab mutant, the three statues and the two lurkers (`kinds`, each with its `CharacterTableID` and body mesh; hunchbacks have no Bip001 spine and VipGuard's head helpers are hair only, so they're left out), and the Airborne Squad, the angels of the endings (`N_AirborneSquad`, 30030). Every live character of those classes is adopted as it shows up, the game's own slicer switched off on it (`nativeSlice` false). When one dies within `reach` (25 m) of her, a blade cuts it (`cutChance`): her swing (`bladeChance` of the time; the arc of her right hand's fastest stretch over the `swingWindow` s up to its last hit, else before its ragdoll started, at least `swingMinSpeed` cm/s, else that stretch and her forward; 1.5 s of her hand are kept, since the ragdoll starts a while after the blow) through where its mesh was hit in the last `hitWindow` s, or through the part nearest the plane; with no swing, a level, slanted or downward blade from her side through a hit or random part. The hit is moved on with the body since (its pelvis then and now), and a hit more than 30 cm from every part (the game reported one above a skull's head, and the plane missed it) is moved onto the nearest. Gore then cuts along that blade as on her (`gore.blow`, why `enemy`): a limb, the head, or the body along the blade, then every other limb it crosses (`multiCut`); a blade at the hips cuts the waist square; one down its length takes the limbs it crosses (never a split), else rolls `odds` (limb, head, torso). No face, no death show. Gore busy with another cut retries for `killWait` s, the blade moved on with the corpse meanwhile, falling back limb, head, torso near the hit. Her swings through a lying corpse cut it again (`corpseHits`: a part within `reach` cm of her swing's plane and `blade` cm of her hand, her sword's length; up to `max` per corpse, `cutChance`, else a hit mark there), and a corpse that leaves (destroyed, pooled and hidden, or gone from the level) takes its pieces, caps, bleeds and decals with it. The game dissolves a corpse some 15 s before it hides it, its despawn show swapping the materials for `MI_DeadMaterial_*`: once a slot shows one, its caps, bone stubs, bleeds and drips go (`dissolving` in the log; they hung in the air where it had been), and it is cut no more. `maxCorpses` cut corpses at once.

A dying enemy's throw (`enemies.pushScale`, `push`, `pushUp`) is followed whether the cuts are on or not: `push` cm/s along the blow (its mesh's last hit force, else away from her) on every body under `Bip001` as a velocity change when its ragdoll is first seen; `pushScale` reads its pelvis's speed 0.06 s later (the death show's ragdoll key fires 0.01 s in) and adds it again times the scale less one. Every vanilla monster death show has a ragdoll key (`Dead_Monster_StandCommon`, `EffectType_*`, `SkillType_*`), so the game ragdolls every death of theirs itself.

`smoothAnim` sets `bEnableUpdateRateOptimizations` off on every live character's mesh (`enemies.lua`'s `smooth_tick`, once a second, each character once, given back when switched off).

Keys (`settings.lua`): F5 spawns the picked NPC `spawnDistance` cm in front of her, facing her (through `SBNetworkPlayerController:ServerRequest_CreateActor`, which runs locally in single player), Insert picks the next kind, Home shows or hides the spawner window. The window (`native/sbslice/spawner.c`, in `sbslice.dll`, its own thread) lists the kinds from `spawner.list` (written by the script) and has Spawn, Spawn 3, Remove all and Kill nearest; it asks the script through `spawner.req`, so all spawning happens on the game thread. Closing it only hides it. While it shows (`spawner.state`, written by the window), the script gives it the mouse: the player controller shows the cursor and takes game and UI input unlocked (`WidgetBlueprintLibrary.SetInputMode_GameAndUIEx`), and goes back to game-only input when it hides (`spawner window open: mouse freed` in the log). The game had kept the cursor captured, and nothing in the window could be clicked. Kill nearest (also End) ragdolls the followed character nearest her, which the script then takes for its death and cuts.

F3 → Spawner (`tune.spawner`, Developer Keys on) spawns any NPC, in foldable groups built from the spawner's kinds (Naytiba, Angels, Bosses, Custom): a header and one select row per NPC, like the play-as menu's character list, with the selected NPC's avatar beside the rows (its `portrait`, else the album's own picture of an NPC kind). Enter on a name picks it; Spawn makes Spawn Count of them. The Custom ID entry spawns `spawner.id` (any CharacterTable ID; an unknown Blueprint is adopted through the pending spawn request). `spawner.hostile` sets what spawned NPCs fight: `hostile` turns even the angels hostile to Eve, `passive` stops their AI, `game` leaves the game's. Hostile patches the kind's CharacterTable row while one spawned of it lives (`bIsHostile` when the row names one — a plain byte or a single-bit bool, anything else left alone; `bApplyDamageWhenPlayerAlive`; the `Passive_Immortal` dispel; all put back exactly on forget, as the bosses' rows are) and keeps its blackboard `*Target*` keys on Eve — or on each other with `spawner.infight`. A row the spawn didn't name (a custom ID) gets blackboards only, told in the log. Only spawned-here actors are touched (`mine`), never story NPCs. `sim_spawner` covers the pick, custom-ID adoption, the row patch and its exact restore, infight pairing, passive stop/start, and the menu rows.

The Airborne Squad are built like Eve: `CH_P_EVE_01_Body` on her skeleton and physics, a face mesh of their own (`CH_P_EVE_Face_003`) and her hair. They are cut with her rules (`face = true` on the kind): a head cut moves the face whole onto the head, as on her, and their `Ab_` helpers aren't sinks. They are passive NPCs (no behaviour tree, a tribe her blows don't hurt), so they die only by Kill nearest, and they are never taken for her killer (`gore.attackerIgnore`). Their body is the same mesh asset as her first outfit: while a cut angel lies around, Eve in that outfit may pinch near the helpers its cut used. The game has no hostile angels besides the bosses Tachy and Raven; the Elder's angels in the final fight are his skill's own meshes, not characters.

Enemies of a kind share one mesh asset, and a cut collapses inverse bind matrices on that asset (its sinks). So a cut corpse releases its own override with `x<comp>,<lod>,1` (its weights only, the asset's sinks kept), and the asset's sinks are restored (`r<mesh asset>`) once no cut corpse of it is left; the full `r` (which also gives back borrowed BoneMaps) only runs with no cuts at all. Live enemies of a kind may pinch near the helpers a cut used while one of its cut corpses lies around. An enemy's `Ab_` bones may be sinks (they are its only deform helpers; its head vanished without them), and its head is cut like a limb (`MODE_CUT`: past the plane goes with the head) since it has no separate face. When a section has no sink of a side in its BoneMap, the DLL borrows the nearest helper of that side into it. The bodies' LOD0 CPU copy is kept by `sbslice.dll` (the `extract` entries under `Art/Character/Monster` and the Airborne Squad's in `mod/ragdoll_spec.json` only put them in the build cache for the harness). `native/sbslice/test/run.sh` cuts an arm, the head and the waist on each kind's real body (with the enemy sink list) and checks no stretched triangle stays visible; the waist test's flung arms take the arm helpers that hang off the clavicle along, as the animation does.

Since 0.4.1 an enemy's cut sends its physics bodies too (`bodies_of`, after `fffe` in `n`), so its stump seams close in the opening's middle on the stump's own body, as hers do; without them they closed on helpers 40-50 cm off the cut. `find_sink` never takes a bone the cut bone hangs from (a statue's root, `Base`, at its feet), and `body_hops` counts a bone under no body at all as 3 hops, the farthest. Without bodies (a waist cut's halves) a sink's collapse point is kept within `SINK_DEEP` (6 cm) past the plane and, if outside the opening's rim, moved into the rim's middle. A piece whose rim doesn't go round the plane point closes on its own rim's middle put on the plane, but only when that middle is within `SINK_DEEP` of it: a statue's arm seam follows its weights at the shoulder, some 30 cm up from a cut down the arm, and a tilted plane put its point 20 cm down the stump. In a limb or torso cut (`MODE_CUT`) a vertex wholly on an earlier cut's sinks is marked collapsed (`vkind` 9), and a triangle of only such vertices starts no seam (a waist cut after a thigh cut took the collapsed thigh's triangles and drew its opening 72 cm up). The harness's `SEQ=bone:at[:tilt],...` (`real_split.c`) cuts an enemy one cut after another as its corpse takes them, flings every piece after each and holds the seams (`sink_hold`), and fails on more stretched triangles showing than right after the cut, or a stretched edge longer than twice the opening plus 15 cm (`SEQNAMES`, `SEQSINKS`, `SEQON`, `SEQFROM` print bones, sinks and where vertices went; `ENEMYNOBODIES=1` cuts without the bodies).

Corpse hits (`enemies.lua` `corpse_hit`) skip parts already off the body (it or a bone it hangs from cut off, `gore.enemy_off`, parents asked once per enemy through `GetParentBone`), come at most one per `corpseHits.gap` (0.5 s), none in the first `from` (0.6 s) nor while a cut on the corpse is opening or queued (`gore.enemy_cuts`), and cut along her blade only (`blade_only`: no fallback to another part). Gore sounds are at least 0.35 s apart (`sfx_last.apart`). A cut that finishes on a corpse already dissolving has its caps and effects removed at once (`enemy.faded`, set by `enemy_fading`, cleared by `enemy_gone` and `forget`).

## Sandbox

**Sandbox Bosses** (`bosses.lua`, `tune.bosses`, F3 → Spawner → Bosses). Tachy (`M_Tachy`, CharacterTable 50210, `CH_M_NA_21`), Raven (`M_Raven`, 50530, `CH_M_NA_53`) and Scarlet (`M_Scarlet`, 59610, DLC `CH_M_NA_961`) are `tune.bosses.kinds`, which `enemies.lua` follows with its own kinds: spawned through `ServerRequest_CreateActor` (Spawn, at `distance` 700 cm), cut at their death as the skulls are (`face = true`: the face goes with the head). All three skeletons have every Bip001 bone the cuts use (Tachy's 54, Raven's 55; Scarlet is on `CH_P_EVE_01_Skeleton`).

A boss can't die as the game has it. Its CharacterTable row lists `Passive_Immortal` in `DefaultEffectArray` (`ActorState_Immortal`, infinite), its `DeadAlias` row dies by a show of its own (`M_Tachy_Dead`, the cutscene) or by `Dead_Monster_StandCommonNoRagdoll` (Raven, Scarlet, Elder), and Raven's `DespawnTimeAfterDeath` is 1 s. `prepare` (before each spawn of a kind) changes the row in game with sbslice's `d` (4 bytes at a field: `DeadAlias`'s name index, `MaxHP`, `MaxShield`, the despawn float) and `N` (a name swapped in a `TArray<FName>`): `DeadAlias` → `deadAs` (`M_SkullSword`, whose dead rows are the common deaths with a ragdoll key), `Passive_Immortal` → `Passive_Immortal_Dispel` (its dispel, an effect no boss row lists, so the swap back is exact), HP and shield times `hpScale`, the despawn at least `despawn` (120) s. Every old value is kept and written back (`put_back`) once none of that kind spawned here is left and none was asked for in 15 s, when Sandbox Bosses goes off for a non-forced row with none live, and at a level reload (the table outlives levels; a table made again at another address is left alone). A forced Spawner request keeps its row through the 15-second adoption window even with Boss Spawning off. `sim_bosses` checks both rows come back byte for byte.

Phases put HP floors on a boss mid-fight: the EffectTable's `MinimumHP_<n>p` rows (23, 95% down to 0.5%: `StatType` `ActorStatType_MinimumHP`, `EffectCalculationType_MaxHPRate`, infinite, flag `MinimumHP`), put on by its tree and skills (Scarlet's counters and talks put on `MinimumHP_60p`, her `M_Scarlet_HPRecover` `MinimumHP_5p`; the Raven Beast's row lists `MinimumHP_85p`) and dispelled by the phase change (`MinimumHP_Dispel`); Scarlet also has `M_Scarlet_Immortal_Infinite` (`ActorState_Immortal`, infinite). The game's own cheat, `Test_EnemyKillToBoss`, kills one by dispelling `Immortal` and `MinimumHP`. A phase change that never plays held one for good: a passive one's (its tree stopped), Scarlet's outside her arena (hers goes on with the level's `MV_Nikke_Scarlet_Phase2` cutscene; she stood there doing nothing, reported). So while any row is mortal those rows hold nothing: `bosses.lua` (`M.HOLDS`) sets each one's enum byte (`StatType` 107 -> 0, `ActorState1` 27 -> 0; `TEnumAsByte` fields, so the aligned 4 bytes around it are read and written back with that byte changed, `sbslice d`) only where it holds what the vanilla table has (a row another mod changed is left and logged), and puts them back once no row is (with the rows: none of yours left, Sandbox Bosses off, the level reloaded). The EffectTable is read live when an effect is put on (as `Down_Eve`'s `LifeTime`), so a floor put on later holds nothing; one put on before the change keeps its stat. A boss's HP then goes down to 0 and the game's own death follows. Its HP is read where the game keeps it: `sbslice.dll`'s `vb<actor>,<MaxHP>,<MaxShield>` (stats.c) scans the actor two pointers deep, as the glass cannon's find does, for a stat array whose MaxHP and MaxShield are the ones its row was given (any HP and shield under them), else whose MaxHP / MaxShield ratio is theirs within 0.5% (a difficulty scaling both), keeps that path per actor and reads through it after (every 0.2 s). Discovery resumes across ticks: each `vb` call checks at most 256 pointers or 64-float chunks and yields after about 1 ms, returning `pending`; only one boss read runs per tick, oldest due first. A hash set replaces the scan's linear visited-address search. Exact maximums win over a ratio match, which waits for the full search. Only completed misses count toward the three tries, 5 s apart; pending work continues on the next tick and clears any stale HP. Parent pointers and the final candidate are revalidated after yielding. Eight native slots hold independent searches; `busy` waits without spending a try rather than evicting a pending search. `vB<actor>` cancels a boss's search on adoption, death or removal; `vB0` clears them on a level reload or Remove Spawned. Glass cannon paths and candidates stay untouched. One still up with that HP at 0 for `zeroWait` (1.5) s, or with its HP unread the boss bar (`WB_MainHUD_EnemyInfo_C`'s `ProgressBar_HP`, which shows the targeted boss; the nearest live one spawned here within `barReach` is taken for it) at 0 and a hit on it in the last 10 s, is taken down: `enemies.kill_one` stops its AI (`BrainComponent:StopLogic`, `StopMovement`), turns its movement and capsule off and ragdolls it, and the tick takes the ragdoll for its death. The mod no longer guesses at a hold from symptoms: blows that took none of its HP (a Raven blocking blow after blow at full HP and shield was taken down 8 s in, playtest; with her HP unread, by her hit reaction, which bosses mostly don't play, 9 s in), its bar held while it stood in place, or its bar at a sliver and hit again each took down a boss that was only fighting.

Its death (`died`): a face of its own (`goreCtx.new_face`, main.lua's death face made per face by `goreCtx.make_face`; its morphs, its moods, `deathFace.at` s in) and a struggle of its own: a fresh copy of `struggle.lua` (its `st` is a chunk local, so each copy has its own) with a context whose `rag` stands for active.lua's ragdoll view (the boss's mesh, settled when its pelvis is under 20 cm/s for 0.3 s, `severed` from `gore.enemy_off`), no convulsions and no animated fall. Both tick from `bosses.tick` (from `enemies.tick`, every poll) until the corpse goes.

**Playing as another character** is a mod of its own, [Stellar Blade Play As](https://github.com/Warexpor/stellar-blade-play-as) (its README). This mod asks nothing of it: `gore.looks_as` reads who she looks like off her meshes at her death (`TachyNPC` in her body's mesh name: Tachy; `CH_M_NA_961`: Scarlet; a visible `CH_M_NA_53_Hair` attached to her body: Raven), and `voice_death` then takes the death cry from `tune.gore.voice.as[who].die` and keeps the set (`vox.as`) for `convulse.lua`'s breaths and moans (`gore.voice_as`), unless `voice.sounds` is set or `voice.asHer` is false. `hair_of` also takes any visible mesh named `*Hair*` on her body's master pose (SBPlayAs's Raven hair), so the smash and photo-mode freeze treat it as hers; a death cut needs nothing for it, since the hair follows her body's bones, the cut-off head's too. `sim_voiceas` checks Tachy, Raven and Eve.

## Glass cannon

A realistic glass cannon mode, dev only for now (`dev = true` in `settings.lua`), off by default (`tune.glassCannon.enabled`; Page Down turns it on and off in game). While her shield is up a hit takes the shield, never HP (vanilla lets part of it through). Once the shield is down, any hit kills her on the spot: cut along the enemy weapon's swing when one landed (as any killing blade, so a swing through her arm goes on into her body), else where it landed (the head off; the body split when the blow came down from above, else cut across the waist; an arm or a leg off). When the HP runs out the usual way, the finishing blow takes off what it landed on. Her blows do `damageMult` (8) times the damage: what every enemy within `foeReach` of her loses, HP and shield, is multiplied, though a multiplied blow leaves it at 1 HP rather than killing it.

The kill goes by her HUD (`WB_MainHUD_PlayerInfo`), which the game keeps live: her HP bar (`ProgressBar_HP.Percent`) dropping by more than `barDrop` (0.002) since the last tick is a hit (`glass: her HP bar 0.94 -> 0.88; landed on ...`), and her shield is its segments (`WB_MainHud_Shield_C` widgets, each a `ProgressBar_Shield`, found with FindAllOf: the HUD has no property to reach them by; `glass: her shield up/down (...)`). The game lets part of a hit through the shield, so a drop with any shown segment holding some (before or at that reading) is the shield's: no death, and the HP is given back (below). The bar slides down over a few ticks after a hit (1.000 -> 0.994, then 0.951), so drops within 0.8 s of one the shield took are its too. Not while the game is paused (gear that raises her MaxHP lowers the bar's share). None of this needs her stats from memory.

Her death is the game's own if it can be had: with her stats found, HP 0 written; without them, the game's own calls, `SBNetworkPlayerController`'s client RPCs, which a single-player game runs locally (the game itself never sends them there): `ClientBroadcast_ActorApplyStat` (her actor's `BroadcastSpawnInfo.ActorGuid`, which read 0 in the thirty-fourth playtest, so the calls may have gone to nobody; HP, value 0), and if she is still up `deathWait` (0.3) s later `ClientBroadcast_ActorDead`; still up as long again, the mod's own death (she gets up after `recoverAfter`). A shield hit's HP goes back the same way (`ClientBroadcast_ActorApplyStat` with the HP before it, her MaxHP read off the HUD's HP text), and the log says what the bar did after (`glass: HP bar ... after giving back ...`). What these calls do with their arguments is untested. The damage multiplier still needs her stats.

Where a hit landed: the nearest enemy weapon's steps over the last `swingBack` s (the swing tracker of the killing cuts) passing within `reach` cm of a part of her, else where her mesh says it was hit (`meshHitWindow`), else a part picked by weight (`glass: ... landed on ...` in the log).

Her HP and shield exist only in native memory (no reflected property, and the cheat manager's HP and effect commands are stubs in the shipping build), so `sbslice.dll` finds them (`native/sbslice/stats.c`): an array of floats indexed by `ESBActorStatType` (HP 1, MaxHP 2, Shield 7, MaxShield 8), looked for in her actor and the objects it points to, two pointers deep, anchored on the numbers her HUD widget holds (`WB_MainHUD_PlayerInfo`'s `HpValue`, `MaxHpValue`, `ShieldValue`, `MaxShieldValue`, from the live instance showing a MaxHP: `FindFirstOf` handed back the all-zero class default). Never by the array's shape alone: thousands of float runs look like stats, and the one taken read HP 0 of 700. The game fills those numbers only now and then (both live widgets read MaxHP 0 for a whole fight in the twenty-third playtest, so nothing was guarded and her damage wasn't multiplied), so without them the stats are searched by her hits: `vs` notes every stats-shaped array in her actor tree (candidates), `vd<actor>,0` after a quiet spell drops the ones whose MaxHP changed, whose HP rose or whose HP changed on 6 checks running (timers; two running lost her array to a combo), and `vd<actor>,1` just after a hit on her mesh keeps the ones whose HP is under what it was when noted (so a hit the script missed doesn't lose her array); each hit's log line also shows what her HUD widgets hold (numbers, HP bar, HP text). Her HP bar (`ProgressBar_HP.Percent`) is the one HUD value the game keeps live (0.41 after a hit in the twenty-sixth playtest, the numbers 0, the text a stale "123 / 123"), so every 0.5 s, once the bar has held still at a new reading under 0.98, `vd<actor>,3` (the reading in LocX) keeps the candidates whose HP / MaxHP is within 0.015 of it, heals included, and only a second reading can take one (one reading took a stranger's array out of 6730, thirty-second playtest); the bar is read off the widget showing it, kept (FindAllOf only every 5 s, and not at all for the HUD's numbers once that widget shows none: a 50 ms hitch every 5 s). Once what is left reads as one array, its paths are the stats (`glass: after a hit: ok found=...`). The first hit lands as in the vanilla game. Her hits mostly go unseen (the mesh's hit shake came 18 times in a whole log), so her death settles it: `vd<actor>,2` keeps what went from above 0 to 0 (nothing at 0: nothing dropped), the bar's check goes first (that playtest's death left the bar at 0.044 and nothing at 0: `err none at 0`); her first life in the mode is the vanilla game's, and a note says when her HP is found (`glass: at her death: ...`). the stats found are checked against the HUD's maximums every 2 s and looked for again after three misses (the HUD's HP lags what the guard gives back, so it isn't compared). An enemy read at HP 0 isn't followed. The pointer path is followed again on every read, and enemies, also `ASBCharacter`s, are read through the same path. Every tick the DLL gives back HP a hit took while her shield was up, multiplies the enemies' losses and writes what happened to `sbslice.evt` (`glass: hit ...`, `guard ...`, `enemy ...`). Page Down shows a note at the top of the screen that fades out by itself ("Glass cannon ON" / "OFF"): a click-through popup drawn by `sbslice.dll`'s window thread (`toast\t<text>` in `spawner.cmd`), since the shipping build strips the engine's own screen messages. `damageMult` is 8 by default (it was 4). A head or body hit sets her HP to 0; if the game doesn't take that as a death within `deathWait` s, the mod's own death runs (with the cut) and she is up again after `recoverAfter` at full HP. `native/sbslice/test/run.sh` checks the find (her array, not a decoy of the same shape; refused without the HUD's numbers, none when they match nothing), reads and writes through the path, the guard, the multiplier and a lost actor on made-up actors (`test/test_stats.c`). Untested in game: whether the found array is the one the game uses, and whether HP 0 kills her.

## Asset tools

`assetkit/sbtool`: `dump <uasset> <json>`, `fromjson <json> <uasset>`, `dumpall <content_root> <json_root> [class]` (batch dump with a JSON round-trip check, package paths on stdin), `validate <dir>` (byte-exact round-trip check), all with `SB_USMAP` set. `build.sh` prefers `mappings/SB.usmap` (UE4SS `DumpUSMAP` from the game, full engine schema) over the header-generated one. Only that file decodes engine assets like `SkeletalMesh` and `PhysicsAsset`.

`assetkit/meshtool` (CUE4Parse, built with `assetkit/meshtool/build.sh`) reads meshes from retoc legacy output. `dump` writes bones, sections, positions and skin weights as JSON; `bones` writes only reference skeletons, used by the outfits step; `gltf` writes a `.glb` that imports into Blender with all 323 bones and weights. New meshes can't go back in without the UE editor to cook them. The generated mappings decode 172/197 of Eve's shows byte-exactly. The rest only miss engine structs (camera-shake oscillators, post-process settings) and still round-trip as raw exports.

## UE4SS script

Blood and Dirt reacquires the material library and collection when writing native skin offsets rather than retaining asset wrappers between ticks, because Lua wrappers do not keep objects alive. Missing assets are retried after one second. The reported attack crash with Ultra Violence is not diagnosed.

Weapon drops deduplicate candidate components by underlying address before creating physics boxes, including candidates supplied as extras. `sim_smash_lifecycle` checks duplicate weapon slots; the reported guard-break duplicate still needs an in-game test.

For the NPC skin report, split-only death weights now bypass the both-arms-instead override. A degenerate split still retries along the center; if both attempts fail while split is the sole enabled death-cut weight, the mod restores the body and logs the failure instead of creating a waist cut. Mixed odds retain the waist fallback. This does not repair incompatible skin geometry or guarantee an actual split; the reported third-party meshes are unavailable locally. Sandbox enemies still explicitly reject vertical splits because player split reconstruction and ownership assume Eve; the separate request remains unimplemented. `sim_smash_hits` covers exclusive/mixed odds and the real failed-stage fallback.
The death camera multiplies its combined mouse and right-stick pitch input by -1 when `cameraInvertY` is enabled. The default is off and the F3 Camera section exposes the setting.

The log (`stellarbladegore.log`) is made anew each start, the last kept as `.old`, capped at 20 MB (errors only past it). `tune.verboseLog` (`lg.v` in main.lua, `ctx.vlog`, the modules' own `vlog`) gates the per-poll death watch (`p###`, `a###`), face and camera probes, hook calls, every cut stage (else only slow ones), the active ragdoll's settle lines and each enemy followed; without it slow ticks and frame hitches are summed once a minute (`screen.perf_note` / `perf_flush`).

`ue4ss/StellarBladeGore` is the UE4SS script. It started as a probe for the ragdoll flinging (runtime fix + logger) and now also runs the gore and the active ragdoll. Install it with `./ue4ss/install.sh`, which sets up the Stellar Blade UE4SS build (pinned and checksummed), and add the Steam launch option `WINEDLLOVERRIDES="dwmapi=n,b"`.

The pinned `UE4SS_v3.1.0-6.zip` contains an enabled `UE4SS_Signatures/FText_Constructor.lua`. A Windows launch ended in UE4SS with `Crashed calling FText constructor`; renaming it to `dFText_Constructor.lua` allowed startup. Fresh development installs disable it; existing UE4SS configurations are left intact and produce a warning if it is enabled. The release zip does not bundle UE4SS, so manual installation instructions cover the same step. Other mods may depend on the signature.

The first probe run showed the impulse isn't the problem. Her pelvis barely travels, but every bone keeps 500–1400 cm/s and up to 9000 deg/s and picks up energy again after settling. That points to self-collision. The mesh runs the `Ragdoll` profile (object type `PhysicsBody`, blocks `PhysicsBody`), so the bodies of her outfit's physics asset collide with each other and with `SBHair` (PhysicsOnly, attached to the head).

On each ragdoll the script makes the mesh ignore `PhysicsBody` and turns `SBHair` collision off. Self-collision is off by default (`tune.selfCollision = false`): with it, an arm landing on her body set her spinning and folding at wild angles. Ignoring `PhysicsBody` didn't stop pairs inside her own physics asset, so the data mod disables those (`outfits.self_collision`); this switch still covers unpatched outfits. Muscle tone ends as soon as she lands (`tone.landOff`: pelvis fell faster than `fallSpeed`, then slowed under `landSpeed`). `"auto"` keeps it when the outfit's physics asset carries the patch (detected by the pelvis body's damping). It restores both on recovery, and re-attaches the mesh to the capsule if respawn left it detached.

The invisible model after respawn had two causes in SB's engine. `ASBCharacter::SetLockupSimulatePhysics(true)` copies each mesh's world transform into `LockupTransform` (an SB addition to `USkeletalMeshComponent`), and the mesh stays pinned there; the script clears that lock on recovery. The death ragdoll also sets a non-reflected counter on `USceneComponent` (+0x180) that stops the parent from moving the component. Only the mesh had it, so the capsule respawned and the body stayed at the death spot. Lua cannot reach it, so `sbslice.dll` clears it (formationPath `u<hex>` on the same cheat), on recovery and once after the respawn. The probe runs from Eve's `ReceiveTick`: this game gives UE4SS no `UEngine::Tick` hook, so `ExecuteInGameThread` callbacks can land on Blueprint worker threads, and one deadlocked the game. The `LoopAsync` that finds her Blueprint hooks that tick once a session and stops, so in play nothing of the mod's Lua runs off the game thread. New objects (a death screen, a character, a knockdown check made) are no longer caught with UE4SS's `NotifyOnNewObject`, whose callbacks ran the mod's Lua on the thread making the object, a loading thread included, at the same time as her tick: with the Nexus "SB UE4SS 1.3" build that crashed the game at launch inside UE4SS's Lua (a table freed under the game thread, a Nexus report's dump; launching with the watches off it started clean). `sbslice.dll` adds its own listener to the engine's `FUObjectArray::UObjectCreateListeners` (`GUObjectArray+0x68`; `AllocateUObjectIndex` calls each one's `NotifyUObjectCreated(object, index)`, vtable `+8`, outside its lock, and the engine's shutdown calls `+0x10`, where ours takes itself out of the list as the engine checks). It queues an object of a watched class or subclass (`UStruct::SuperStruct` at `+0x40`, found by walking a known pair at setup), never a default object, in a ring of 8192 (more are dropped and counted). `newobj.lua` takes them on her tick, up to 200 a tick: `O` puts the next one still alive (its `GUObjectArray` item, as `G`) into the cheat manager default object's `DebugCameraControllerRef` (UE4SS's Lua can't make an object of an address), the script reads it and hands it to the watches whose class it `IsA`, till it reads null. `O<field>,<child>,<parent>,<class>,...` sets it up, `O?` counts. Photo mode's pawn is the one exception, still `NotifyOnNewObject` (`newobj.watch(..., true)`): a native class whose default object is made at boot and whose pawn is only spawned on the game thread as photo mode opens, and the freeze has to come at that moment (on her next tick photo mode had started and a cut-off head snapped back to her neck, playtest). Without `sbslice.dll` the watches are `NotifyOnNewObject` again; `tune.newObjectWatch = false` turns them off. `sbslice.dll`'s own thread watches the game thread's mod commands: one running over 3 s goes to `sbslice.stuck`, and a crash in the DLL's code or inside a command to `sbslice.crash` (code, offset, command; a vectored handler that only looks); the next start logs them (`sbslice.dll last session: ...`). Its DllMain notes each step of its own start (the address search, the CPU copy patch) in `sbslice.boot`, by its own path (the game's working directory may not be set yet), logged as `sbslice.dll boot: ...`: a launch that closed before the first window left nothing else to go by. It logs bone motion to `Win64/ue4ss/Mods/StellarBladeGore/stellarbladegore.log`.

SB's ragdoll drags the capsule after `RagdollInfo.CapsuleComponentFollowingSocket` a step every tick, which made the camera stutter after her. The data patch sets it to `None` in every Eve ragdoll key, and the script clears it on every ragdoll tick as well (`tune.capsuleStill`), for an animated death that goes ragdoll partway and the test ragdolls, which never run a key and keep the default `Bip001-Pelvis`. The log's `capsule: drifted at most N cm during the ragdoll` (while she simulates, not recovery's move to her body) says whether it held. That wasn't all: an animated death that goes ragdoll partway (a split, a torso cut, a leg) has the game move the pawn after her body itself, 50-65 cm a tick, with the socket `None` and her movement already `MOVE_None` (playtest 41), and the view rides the pawn; putting the capsule back every tick made the camera strobe. So at a death the view holds still instead: a `CameraActor` is spawned where the view is (its rotation and field of view) and made the player controller's view target, turned every frame by the mouse and the right stick, read off the input (`GetInputAnalogKeyState` MouseX/Y, Gamepad_RightX/Y; `cameraMouse` degrees a unit of mouse input (3; SB reports a slow move as about 0.06 a frame), `cameraStick` degrees a second; the game turns the controller's rotation only sideways while she is dead), all the way round both ways, over the top upside down, about the point it looked at (her capsule's distance down the view, at least 150 cm), kept 10 cm short of whatever a Camera-channel trace from that point meets, flown by WASD, Q/E (world down and up), the left stick and the triggers (`IsInputKeyDown`; forward along the view, sideways on the level; `cameraMove` cm a second, `cameraFast` times with Shift, at most `cameraRange` from where it started, the point stopping 10 cm short of what a trace along its step meets; once flown it no longer slides after her), that point sliding after her pelvis on the level by as much as the pelvis has moved since she died, eased in (`cameraTrack`, 2.5: the share of the gap closed a second, as 1 - e^(-rate dt) each frame; its height kept, 0 holds it), and the view goes back to her at recovery (`tune.cameraStill`; `camera: view held at ...`, `camera: view back on her`, `camera at N s: ...` for 3 s). `capsule: moved N cm this tick` logs the pawn's moves (at most 5). The flying keys count only once all of them have been let go (she died running, W down, and it flew off at once). Esc on a real death (parked for now: the key binding is commented out in `main.lua`; not the test ragdolls; `tune.deathSkip`) sets the global time dilation to `deathSkipSpeed` (6) until the game's death screen widget `WB_MainHUD_Dead_C` is in the viewport and visible, or the game's time is past `recoverAfter`; the recovery clock counts the game's time meanwhile, so she still gets up under the screen (`death: Esc, fast forward x6 ...`, `death: fast forward off after N s (the death screen is up)`). While the death screen is up, the log says every 0.5 s, when it changes, what covers the view: the widget's `black` and `FadeOut` images, its `BackgroundBlur_116` and `Root` (visibility, render opacity, colour alpha, blur), and the camera manager's fade and view target (`death screen (N s into the death): ...`, `death screen: gone`; only in a death, from 5 s in, the widget found once and kept: a `FindFirstOf` every 0.5 s cost 17 ms each): it came up all black, nothing of the game behind it.

Cut stages and frame time: each `c` is mostly the engine building the new skin weight buffer (`SetSkinWeightOverride`, 15-30 ms in game; the DLL's own work is a few ms, the whole real-mesh harness runs in about 50), so no frame carries more than one. The split runs `cut` (the copy made), `cut2` (her half's cut), `cut3` (the copy's, then the checks), resumable `caps`/`caps2` (outline, each mesh, skin binding, spine and neck in separate frames), `bodies` (the copy's physics bodies made kinematic in her pose, `split_bodies`), `layout`/`layout2` (body inventories and ancestry), `rehome` (held-ragdoll outfit riders), `masses`, then `open`; the limb, head and waist cuts run `cut`, `caps`, `stubs`, `open`, and bleed the tick after the opening.

With `cameraFollow` on (off by default: it fought how the death plays out, and the game died a second after it moved a really dead Eve's capsule), during any ragdoll her capsule, which the camera rides, drifts after her pelvis instead of staying where she died: nothing within `cameraSlack` (40 cm), then `cameraRate` (0.06) of the rest each tick, held `cameraLift` (95 cm) over it, ignoring her bodies meanwhile so it never shoves them (top level of `tune.lua`: `cameraFollow`, `cameraSlack`, `cameraRate`, `cameraLift`). A recovery then gets her up where she lay.

Enemies attacking her after her death (`tune.enemies.afterDeath`, enemies.lua `M.after_tick`; `enemies.afterDeath.enabled` in settings, F3 → Enemies → Attacks After Death, off by default). Parked from 0.4.1 to 0.4.7: its swings never found an attack to play, because the paths of the enemy's mesh and of the loaded animations were read with `GetPathName`, which UE4SS's objects don't have (it answered nothing: `its mesh  is in no monster folder`). Paths come from `GetFullName` less the class now (`path_of`), and an attack animation counts anywhere under the monster's folder with `Anim` in its path. While she lives, every 2 s, each character within `reach` cm (not hers, not `gore.attackerIgnore`) has its AI controller's `Blackboard` read: the `BlackboardKeyType_Object` keys of its `BlackboardAsset` (and parents) that hold her are kept per actor. From her death (`goreCtx.dead_since`) for `time` s: every `SBBTTask_SbDetectTarget` with `bEnemy` and not `bDead` gets `bDead` true (the search takes dead targets; kept through `goreCtx.keep` with their indexes read at once, put back for the ones still alive at the end), and every 0.25 s each live enemy within reach whose kept keys no longer hold her gets them set back (`SetValueAsObject`); one with no kept key uses its object keys named `Target`. The log has `after death: enemies keep at her ...` (searches changed, their `TargetCancel` values, enemies with her on their blackboards), the first 5 keys set back, and `after death: over ...`. The trees' target searches also get `TargetCancel` Destroy (2), the patrol's `SbMoveToHome` with `bDetectTarget` the same, and every `SBBTDecorator_SbIsAlive` on the target has its `CheckType` swapped (Alive 0, Dead 1); those nodes are looked up ahead (`after_scan`, while no enemy is within `scanClear` cm, whenever enemies of a new class turn up), not in the death's frame (four `FindAllOf` there were 195 ms). That brings them to her body, but the game's skills never fire at a dead target, so an enemy within `swingReach` (450 cm) of her body turns to it and plays one of its own attack `AnimSequence`s on its mesh (`PlayAnimation`, its animation Blueprint given back after), lunging in to `lunge` (120 cm) over the swing's first third. Its weapon then cuts her through Hits After Death (main.lua `hits_after`), which follows every enemy `track_swing` follows (`sw.list`, each armed again once its own weapon slowed down), not only the nearest one.

Enemies attacking her while she is down (`tune.enemies.attackDown`, enemies.lua): about 100 of the game's 149 enemy behaviour trees (`GameDesign/Combat/BehaviorTree`) hold their attack branch behind an `SBBTDecorator_SbCheckActorEffect` on the target (`ActorType` Target) whose `EffectAlias` / `OrCheck_EffectAliasArray` / `AndCheck_EffectAliasArray` name her knockdown effects (`KnockDown{Forward,Backward}[Tumbling[Ready]]_Eve`, and the shared `Getup`, `FastGetup`, `Down`, `DownFace{Up,Down}[_E]`). Her knockdown effects (`EffectTable`) set no `NoDamageNoHit`, so nothing else keeps her from being hit. The script finds the loaded decorators once (`FindAllOf`, 50-90 ms: run every 3 s it stuttered the game), then catches each new one as it is made (`newobj.lua`) and, a second later, once its asset's names are in, swaps those names for `SBGore_NotDown`, which no effect has; off, it puts the originals back (kept by the decorator's full name). The monsters' own `KnockDownForward` / `KnockDownBackward`, `Item_Resurrection_Ground` and `LV_FinishQTE_FailDown` are left as they are. With `enemies.attackDown` off and `holdDown` on, the 281 target checks whose name lists hold `Down` get the data mod's `Down_Eve` in place of `KnockDownForward` (no check lists one without the other), so the trees wait through her held knockdown; the 11 with `EffectAlias` `Down` alone (Juggernaut, Scarlet, Tachy, Sawshark trees, inverse checks) are left.

Ultra Violence's blood at a respawn (`tune.uvClean`): UV clears her blood only at a player restart (a load), Eve gone, a camp rest or in water (its `tickCombat`, every 0.5 s, reads `CharacterMovement.MovementMode` 4 as swimming and calls its `forceClean`), and the game's respawn (`NotifyBP_ReInitActor`, the same pawn) is none of these. So when the mod gets her up for a respawn (not a revive in place) it writes `MovementMode` 4 as a property for `screen.UV_POLLS` (36, 1.2 s) with her movement component's tick off (else its swim physics puts the mode back outside water), then `SetMovementMode(1)` and the tick back on, or leaves the movement to the game's respawn if it comes first.

As she comes into the world her ponytail starts on top of the sword on her back or off her hair tie and jiggles there till photo mode is opened and closed. The ponytail is cloth (`SBPonytail`, no animation of its own; KawaiiPhysics only moves her front and side strands) and collides with her bodies, which physfix re-makes as she spawns; the cloth settles on the wrong side of the tie, held there by those collisions, and cloth resets, `ResetAnimInstanceDynamics` and the game's `NeedPonytailClothConfigUpdate` all ran it again from where the collisions let it. Photo mode switches every character mesh's collision to query only while it is on (0x1423a33e0), which lets it drop back through. So `screen.cloth_soon` / `cloth_tick`, `CLOTH_AFTER` (4 s) after the last of a new Eve, `NotifyBP_ReInitActor` (a respawn), `NotifyBP_SetMesh` or `ApplyMeshInfo` (an outfit) or her bodies re-made (physfix `ctx.on_remade`), set every `SkeletalMeshComponent` in her attach tree (`screen.cloth_meshes`) with physics collision to `QueryOnly` (kept with `goreCtx.keep`, asked alive before they are given back), reset the cloth once (`ForceAllClothNextUpdateTeleportAndReset`, and on each mesh `ResetAnimInstanceDynamics(ResetPhysics)` and `ForceClothNextUpdateTeleportAndReset`), and give the collision back 1.5 s later; at once in a death or photo mode. Hair simulated as bodies on her own mesh (CH_P_EVE_30's `Ab-TL-HairB01-09`: bodies simulating that aren't under a simulating `Bip001` bone, read before the switch) stops while the mesh is query only and goes onto her pose; as the collision comes back it is set kinematic, and 0.15 s later simulated again from that pose (it simulated on from where it had been before, a second snap, wrong). The log says `cloth: her ponytail reset (<why>): N mesh(es), M of them query only for 1.5 s (as photo mode), K simulated hair bodies on her mesh`, `cloth: her meshes' collision given back (M)` and `cloth: her hair's K simulated bodies simulate again from her pose`. Some spawns still left it wrong, and F8 put it right every time (playtest), so `screen.auto_f8` runs F8's own recovery (`sandbox_recover`: her bodies' physics off, `gore.cleanup`, `rag.stop`) `AUTO_F8_S` (2) s after the collision is given back, once she is up, has nothing cut off (`gore.cut_parts`), isn't regrowing and photo mode is closed (`auto F8: ...`). A reset set going by that recovery itself (her bodies re-made) schedules none.

Photo mode up close (`tune.photoUnhide`): photo mode hides her actor (`AActor::bHidden`, set inline, not through the virtual `SetActorHiddenInGame`) when its camera comes close. Its subject visibility (0x14239c410) hides her while any of its reasons is set (+0x220 hidden as it opened, +0x228 a count, +0x22d, +0x231, +0x22f); +0x22f is the close-range one, set by 0x14239b2c0 (the camera's distance to her bone against `SBPhotoMode_CloseRange_BoneToCamDistanceThreshold`) through `mov cl, 1` at 0x14239c0b3. Keeping +0x22f alone from being set (`mov cl, 0`) still left her hidden (playtest), so sbslice's `f6<0|1>` NOPs the jumps for +0x231, +0x228, +0x22d and +0x22f (0x14239c45b, 46b, 478, 485) or puts them back, every one's bytes checked first (`ok photo close <0|1> (was <0|1>)`); +0x220 still hides a subject that was hidden as photo mode opened. main.lua sends it once (logged once while sbslice starts), and again when the setting changes. Photo mode's own hide-a-character option goes with it. Besides that, main.lua `screen.photo_unhide` runs while photo mode is on (its manager's `+0x84` not 0, read by `f3` every 0.5 s, or `GetPlayerPawn` or the camera's view target a PhotoMode class; `photo probe: ...` logged as these change): her actor and her parts (her root's attach tree as it opens; one hidden then, an outfit piece the gore took off, stays hidden) are shown again, hidden, invisible and owner-no-see undone, her actor taken out of the controller's `HiddenActors`, and the near clip plane is `tune.photoNearClip` cm (`r.SetNearClipPlane` through `KismetSystemLibrary::ExecuteConsoleCommand`, back to the game's 10 as it closes). That runs from her ReceiveTick, which photo mode's pause stops; with dev on, a watcher every 0.25 s from that same tick logs `vis watch: ...` (sbslice f9, her parts' hide flags, the camera's distance while photo mode is up) as these change; it doesn't see through the pause. It was a `LoopAsync(250)` off the game thread, which crashed the game at a death screen (it found the command channel and read a pawn the respawn was remaking while the level loaded). Not the cause, found on the way: the gameplay camera's own player hide (`PlayerHideDistFromPlayerCameraXY`/`Z`, 0x1424085e0) and MPC_DitherActor's `SBWorldEnv_DitherDisable` (BG actors only).

Holding her down (`tune.holdDown`, main.lua `screen.held`): `tables.lua` adds `Down_Eve` to `Local/Data/EffectTable` (a copy of `Down`, ID 3009001, `LifeTime` 100000) and chains her tumbling knockdowns into it; `Down` allows the fast getup, so dodge or jump still gets her up. Off, `gore.row_float` writes the row's `LifeTime` back to 1 in the loaded table: `sbslice.dll`'s `d` command finds the row by name in the DataTable's row map and sets the float at the property's offset from the row struct's reflection.

Test keys (only with `dev = true` in `Scripts/settings.lua`, which lays the player's switches over `tune.lua` key by key): F7 ragdolls her with a shove onto her back using `Scripts/tune.lua` (`push` on the whole body, `impulse` on the upper body, `legKick` sweeping her feet the other way, `direction` relative to her facing or the camera, `up`, self/hair collision, mass scale), F8 recovers, F9 cuts a limb off, F10 puts a living hit's mark on her (see Hits she lives through), F11 splits her (Gore), Delete cuts along a made-up blade, Page Up shows the death face's moods in turn on her living face, 6 s each (see Death face), F5 spawns an NPC, Insert picks the next kind, Home shows the spawner window, End kills the followed character nearest her (see Enemies), Page Down turns the glass cannon mode on and off (see Glass cannon). (`SBSuicide` is a no-op in the shipping build, so there is no real death key.) Alt+F6 (Test: Animated Death, `hm.at`) plays the game's own death shows on her, the next each press (P_Eve_Dead, P_Eve_Dead2, P_Eve_Dead3, Eve_Dead_Down), so a mod replacing their animations (Alternative Death Animations' death_anim_P: Result_Stand_Dead, Eve_Stand_Dead2/3, the down deaths) shows in the mod's animated death. The death starts with the show, on the next tick: on her alive its ragdoll key never fires (a first version waited 3 s for it, and the death and its cut came as the animation ended, playtest). It is animated even with `active.animated` off, her own fall (`forceFall` "own", none of the knockdown falls over it), with no cut (a split, a beheading or a leg would take her to the ragdoll; the animation is what is tested), and she lies till F8 (`saved.manual`). F8 ends a test death that stayed animated (`on_end`): with no physics to turn off, the tick never ended it, and F8 did nothing. F6 tests the struggle (`STRUGGLE_TESTS` in main.lua): each press the next of a ragdoll death with her right arm off at the shoulder, her left leg off at the hip, a waist cut, her left arm and right leg, both legs, her left forearm at the elbow, or no cut (a sit-up-only plan), with F7's shove a couple of ticks into the ragdoll (`f7_push`), so she goes down with it rather than folding where she stood. The death starts on the next tick straight into the ragdoll (`ragdoll_collision`, the fall's own pose, `ragdoll_now`), its cuts made one after the other (`gore.slice_bones`: the first now, the rest queued as a swing's further cuts), and she lies till F8 (`saved.manual`). `goreCtx.struggle_test` is set across `on_start`, so `struggle.start` runs whatever `deathMoves` says. It replaced the test death (her death shows in turn, the cut rolled), which mostly ended in an animated fall or a split, with no struggle. On first load the script also runs UE4SS `DumpUSMAP` once, giving the full engine schema needed to edit physics assets. Remove it with `./ue4ss/install.sh --uninstall`.

The script without the game: `ue4ss/test/run.sh [fall|regrow|struggle|waist|f6|after|reload|reload_head|sanity|keys|hairpairs|lemi|outfit|photo|fold|crawl|push|animdeath|odds|diag|lowarm]` runs the shipped scripts, unchanged, against a made-up game in Lua 5.4: UE4SS's globals (`fake.lua`: hooks, key binds, `StaticFindObject`, `FindAllOf`, a clock the scenario drives, sbslice.dll faked at its command boundary with every command recorded) and a scripted Eve (`eve.lua`: her skeleton standing, movement, physics on or off, HP bar, death screen, a fall, a death, a respawn, components added to her). `sim_fall.lua`: a 16 m fall whose death show gives no ragdoll is taken over and its death screen held to 10 s; the controls (the show's ragdoll working, a fall leaving her at 1 HP, a death while swimming) are not. `sim_regrow.lua`: Alt+F8, a camp rest at the chair, the dissolve and the hand-off, checked for order (the cut undone, the copy destroyed, only then the sinks back) and the fade's curve (no ripple, no glow). `sim_struggle.lua` (`deathMoves` on in its settings; `sim_waist.lua` runs it with her death cutting her across the waist instead: the struggle must start on her upper body, the waist first and no legs, with no sitting up or pushing. `sim_f6.lua` presses F6 through all seven struggle tests with `deathMoves` off, F8 between: each must make its cuts and begin the struggle on the stumps it left (the sit-up-only plan for the one with none).lua): an arm off alive, a ragdoll death, the struggle on a made-up ragdoll (`rag.lua`: her bodies as joints that turn, each body's turn against its parent's turning what is below it; no gravity, floor or limits): its acts must run to her death and keep most of each turn (86% here, 0% in every playtest, so what undoes them in game is the engine's or a part of the mod the fake has no stand-in for; the act lines' `read back at once` and `meanwhile her turns zeroed` say which). `sim_after.lua`: two made-up Naytiba by her body with their trees' nodes and attack animations: the trees changed and put back, their own attacks found and played with a lunge, their blades through her cutting her, the further one's too. `sim_reload.lua`: a level reload with an arm off alive puts the asset's sinks back. `sim_reload_head.lua`: one with her head cut off alive (F9) puts back her body's and her face's (each cut records its asset, the face's too: `gore.forget` puts back every asset of a cut of hers that the new Eve wears). `sim_hairpairs.lua`: `physfix.alive_pairs` keeps the vanilla table's pairs of her hair against her main bodies, not two main bodies' (`L` read once, `t` written with them). `sim_lemi.lua`: an outfit mesh whose own physics asset has no pelvis, worn while loading is held off: no verdict till it may load, then the complete asset given; the override dropped later with the mesh asset still ours not re-made while she lives, and put back at her death. `sim_outfit.lua`: the same through main.lua, her bodies made from the asset she reads at each re-make and only a bone with a body simulating: the vanilla outfit and a thighless one (given the complete asset at once) each an F6 death that ragdolls and keeps its cut; the Latex Nun from a level load (no verdict while loading is held off, given after, an F6 and a real death ragdolled); a reload with the complete asset preloaded and a death 2 s in; the Latex Nun switched to mid-game and its asset dropped every 2.5 s for 30 s with no re-make, put back at her death. On the old physfix it fails as the report's logs did (ragdolls on LtxNun_Physics, 10 re-makes). `sim_photo.lua`: P in a death holds it while photo mode is open and lets the hold go as it closes, or at once when it doesn't open (the death screen up once the death's clock reaches it); a death held with Shift+F4 first stays held through photo mode till Shift+F4 again. `sim_fold.lua`: struggle.lua's `fold` and `solve` on both elbows' and knees' real hinge axes: a fold the way it goes kept, the wrong way straight (or full when nearer), and a target needing a 100 deg bend reached with its own fold whichever side the act's pole was on. `sim_crawl.lua`: face down, an arm off alive and a leg at her death, only crawling: a planted hand pulls, and the velocity added to her torso points headward along the floor (`rag.lua` sums it in `R.lin`; its `faceDown` lays her on her front); `sim_push.lua` the same on her back with no arms, her heels driving. `sim_animdeath.lua`: Alt+F6 plays P_Eve_Dead and its death starts with it, animated with `active.animated` off, her own fall, no cut; she lies past 20 s and F8 gets her up; the next press plays P_Eve_Dead2, its death starting too. `sim_lowarm.lua`: Lose Arm at Low HP's side by the hit: a hit on her left forearm takes the left arm, one on her chest 14 cm to her right the right arm, a drop with no hit seen one at random. `sim_sanity.lua` / `sim_keys.lua`: the shipped settings (and Developer Keys) loaded, F3, twelve seeded deaths each respawned, a fall death, every key of the mod pressed, a camp rest, with no hook or tick error and no failure line in the log. The engine itself (physics, skinning, shows, the menu's UI) isn't in it: `native/sbslice/test/run.sh unit` checks the skinning side (the copy's hidden side shown again by the sinks' put back under it).

## Settled-limb copy and cap fallback

When component creation itself fails, `hide_pieces` returns without creating a piece state. The settled-piece watch now records that failure just as it does a failed pose copy, leaving the existing limb visible and avoiding repeated creation attempts for the same mesh and parts. Once all cuts are gone it clears the failure so a later cut of the same limb can retry. `sim_piece_create_fail` verifies the failed creation, retained limb, single attempt and retry after undo; `sim_piece` covers successful copies and failed pose cleanup. The latest session did create its copy successfully, so this does not explain its crash.

When a procedural cap section fails after its actor has been positioned at the outline mean, the fitted fallback sphere now moves to the fitted ellipse centre before attachment. `sim_cap_fallback` checks that case and confirms a successful procedural lid retains its original centre. The latest session logged procedural lids, so its reported stump misplacement remains undiagnosed.

## License

GPL-3.0 (see `LICENSE`). Not covered: `third_party/` keeps its authors' terms (the death face, see its README), and `mappings/SB.usmap` describes the game's types; the game and its assets are Shift Up's.

Automatic cloth recovery backs off while a cut piece is off, before scanning level sequences. A pending initial cloth reset waits for the piece to recover; collision restoration still runs. Piece anti-gravity integrates real elapsed time scaled by the current game time dilation.

For roster and custom-ID requests, the spawner snapshots the currently listed character addresses before sending the request; proximity adoption excludes those addresses. Newly appearing nearby actors remain a heuristic match, not a confirmed identity.
